# The D2 checks (2026-10-18)

The reports [ADR-0105](../../0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md) and [ADR-0106](../../0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md) cite: the three rounds of adversarial checks of the D2 wave and the check before its merge. Each round had builders, then several checking lenses, then a refuter for each blocking finding, who tried to show it was not real. The reports were kept only in the session's scratchpad under Temp (`C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/`). The text is unchanged; Claude added the headings and the layout. A `<` that GitHub would read as the start of an HTML tag is written `&lt;`, so that it shows. The clones, red runs, mutation logs and gate logs the reports name are not kept.

## Round 1: the D2 wave (2026-10-18)

*From `d2-wave-result.json` (the session's scratchpad under Temp). Three builders (b1 the small follow-ups filed for the wave's first commit, b2 the late close of ADR-0106, b3 the sandbox of ADR-0105), five lenses.*

### What the builders reported

#### b1 (head commit aa44400)

All eleven follow-ups in the backlog row "Small follow-ups for the first commit of the D2 wave" are done, in three commits on fix/fx-d2 (09cdcd7, f8ff208, aa44400), and the row is removed.

1. **Advice (09cdcd7).** In `packages/runner/src/activities/asset.ts`, `ENDS_SENTENCE` now accepts `\p{Pi}` closing quotes, built test-first. German and Danish close a quote with “ ‘ «, so before this the advice note added a second stop, as in „mehr.“. New tests cover:
   - every comma, colon and semicolon form `GIVES_WAY` names
   - the ellipsis counting as a stop
   - advice that is only digits, in four scripts
   - the cut at a word against the cut at a character, on both sides of the halfway point

   ADR-0104 gets a "## Later notes" section on where its Decision and the code differ:
   - keycaps such as 1️⃣, and ℹ️, count as advice on their own
   - the halfway point is measured in UTF-16 units, and a cut can split a letter from its accent, a flag or a joined emoji
   - advice ending in a Tibetan shad gets a full stop after it, and a Greek question typed with ";" is shown with a full stop instead
   - the closing-quote fix above

2. **Renderer start-up line (f8ff208).** `rendererCheckWithin` takes `{ built }`. If Chromium has not started within the 30 s, the line now says "Playwright's Chromium had not started 30 s after the app was built and the engine was up." In contributor mode, which builds nothing first, it ends "after the engine was up." `serve.ts` passes `{ built: mode === 'start' }`.
   - The new `scripts/test/serve.test.ts` reads the source of `main()` in `serve.ts`. It checks that the renderer check starts before the build with no limit of its own, that the limit starts only after the build and the engine, that the next statement prints the line, and that nothing else uses the check or the limit.
   - ADR-0084 gets a dated "## Later notes" bullet: the 30 s now count from when the app is built and the engine is up, not from the check's start.

3. **Docs (aa44400).**
   - The bullet in `docs/adr/README.md` now says its check of a late "changed by" link applies from ADR-0103 on.
   - The licence note under "Before any distribution" in `docs/backlog.md` now says sharp can load at run time. Next's image endpoint `/_next/image` is live, because `next.config.ts` leaves image optimization on, and Next loads sharp when that endpoint resizes an image. Neither package loads through minimizer-webpack-plugin.
   - The finished backlog row is removed.

#### b2 (head commit c31f107)

The shared-browser hazard is fixed and recorded as ADR-0106, in three commits on fix/fx-d2:
- **957da70 (renderer).**
  - When a page's context is late to close, its browser is retired instead of being closed under the other renders. New loads launch a fresh browser, the loads still on the old one finish there, and it is closed when the last of them ends.
  - A backstop closes a retired browser at the latest timeoutMs + closeTimeoutMs (35 s with the defaults) after it was retired.
  - Opening a context now runs under the deadline and the cancel. A context that opens after its load gave up is closed as it opens.
  - byway's own browser closes carry a reason. A load they stop says so, for example "The page could not be drawn: byway closed the page renderer as it shut down."
- **ecfb810 (CI).** A failed job uploads the server log and Playwright's traces and screenshots.
- **c31f107 (records).** The ADR, its index row, ADR-0103's "changed by" link and the backlog.

The browser bookkeeping (counts, retiring, the backstop, closes with a reason) is a new small module, `packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts`. That lets it be tested with stand-in browsers and fake timers, with no Chromium. `pages.ts` uses it in `dispose`, `loaded`, `ready` and `close`, and its two failure mappings name byway's own closes.

**Finding beyond the plan.** A probe of Playwright 1.63 showed that only a call already running when byway closes the browser carries the reason. Calls made afterwards get the bare "Target page, context or browser has been closed", the same text a crash gives. So the renderer also remembers which browsers it closed and why, and words the failure from that. A mutation (M16) shows that matching Playwright's message alone misses a render between two calls.

SEC-5's intent holds:
- Every load ends at its deadline or cancel, now including the wait for its context.
- The render that owns a stuck context still ends at its limit plus at most two close limits, as before.
- A hung Chromium is closed once its last load ends, and the backstop bounds that in any case.

Items:

- **1. Retire, don't kill, with a backstop** (done): Late close → retire: the browser is forgotten without waiting for a relaunch on its way, and closed when its last load lets it go or at the backstop (timeoutMs + closeTimeoutMs after retiring). Closing a retired browser waits at most closeTimeoutMs. pages.close() closes retired browsers too. A load is counted from the moment it gets the browser and released in a finally after its context is disposed, which also covers a failed newContext. ready() does not count. Code in packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts and pages.ts.
- **2. newContext under the deadline and cancel; close a context that arrives late** (done): newContext runs inside the work raced against the limit; the launch keeps Playwright's own timeout. dispose() chains close onto the pending context, so one that opens after its load gave up is closed as it opens. One that neither opens nor closes within closeTimeout retires the browser. This closes the E4b gap.
- **3. Tag byway's closes with a reason and map them in drawFailure/readFailure** (done): The two reasons are 'byway closed the page renderer after a page in it took more than N s to close' and '... as it shut down'. The renderer also remembers which browsers it closed (closedWith), because calls made after a close carry only the bare text (probe). loaded() turns such failures into RendererClosed, except a cancel or a StepError of its own, and drawFailure/readFailure word them plainly. The bare text now means Chromium went away by itself.
- **4. Optional: do not count the engine's own pause as Chromium's lateness** (not-done): Left in the backlog. After item 1 a false late verdict costs one relaunch and no failed render. The skeptic showed 'look again after setImmediate turns' fails, and the form that works adds wall-clock judgement into production for little gain. It was testable with fake timers; I chose not to build it, per the skeptic's advice. The backlog row records the working form and the evidence.
- **5. CI artifacts on failure** (done): verify.yml: a step finds os.tmpdir() as scripts/e2e.ts does; two upload steps pin actions/upload-artifact@043fb46d1a93c77aae656e7c1c64a875d1fc6a0a (v7.0.1, from 2026-04) and run if: failure(). Artifacts are e2e-server-log-&lt;os> and playwright-results-&lt;os> (apps/web/test-results), with if-no-files-found ignore and retention 14 days. No use of the secrets context. ci.test.ts gets a new test that reads the workflow as YAML, ties the paths to e2e.ts and playwright.config.ts, and runs the step's script. workflow-guard passes. The README CI section says how to fetch the artifacts.
- **Record ADR-0106, index row, changed-by links, backlog** (done): docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md: Accepted, User-approved, System track. Index row added in System; count 104→105 records, 98 accepted (15 continuation, 38 delegated), 7 proposed. A 'changed by' link was added only to ADR-0103, whose §3 the CI uploads extend. ADR-0084, 0099 decision 8, 0081, 0074 and 0080 still hold as written; the ADR explains why. The backlog row is replaced by one listing what is left, and ADR-0106 was added to the row about evidence kept only in %TEMP%.
- **Tests without wall-clock assertions, mutation-checked** (done): pages.test.ts: 5 new tests (co-tenant survives a held late close, and the browser closes after it; hung newContext read ends at its deadline; same with a cancel; a late-arriving context is closed; byway's close named in three victim cases). render-browsers.test.ts: 7 tests with fake timers. ci.test.ts: 1 new test. All existing pages.test.ts tests pass, including the never-settles test's ['context','browser'].

#### b3 (head commit 0da7bf0)

D2 is built on fix/fx-d2 and recorded as ADR-0105, in five commits (72a9a53, 093155f, f6f978e, e36a0aa, 0da7bf0). Chromium now draws pages inside its OS sandbox by default. The only way to switch it off is the switch in Settings, and byway never switches it by itself in either direction.

**Where the setting lives.** One setting for the whole data directory, in the store (migration 8, table `renderer_settings`). Never set means on. I did not put it in the per-workspace Preferences: the engine has one renderer serving every workspace, so a per-workspace value could disagree with the browser in use. The only writer is `Store.setRendererSandbox`, called only by Settings' new route `PATCH /api/renderer`, which accepts `{ osSandbox }` and nothing else. A new test, `guard-sandbox.test.ts`, fails if anything else in the product switches it.

**How the renderer uses it.**
- The setting is read before every page load. A read that fails counts as on.
- A browser launched with the other setting is retired using builder 2's mechanism (ADR-0106): new loads launch with the new setting, and pages already being drawn finish on the old browser before it closes.
- `launchChromium({ osSandbox })` takes only the setting, so nothing can add a flag.
- `RENDERER_OS_SANDBOX` (off) is now `RENDERER_OS_SANDBOX_DEFAULT` (on).

**Readiness.** `ready()` now draws byway's own test page, whose script must run, the first time it is asked on each browser. That means the sandboxed page renderer itself has started before a web-page run (or a run whose examples become cards) spends anything; a launch alone never started it.

**Start-up check (`checkRenderer`).**
- With the sandbox on and failing for no reason Chromium gave, it draws the test page once more without the sandbox, to tell whether the sandbox is the cause.
- With the sandbox off, it tries the sandbox on the test page to see whether it could start now.
- Both probes draw only byway's test page and never change the setting.
- `serve.ts` prints the result (on; or off and whether the sandbox could start now; or not available with the reason) and records it in the store for Settings.
- `pnpm e2e` now fails unless the line says "Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox".

**When the sandbox cannot start** (`packages/runner/src/sandbox.ts`):
- A failure is recognised from Playwright's "Chromium sandboxing failed!" or Chromium's own three markers, anywhere in the error. The first line alone only says the browser closed, which is also what a crash says.
- The message gives the reason and fix for this computer:
  - **Windows:** security software or company policy is blocking it; the fix names Playwright's Chromium file.
  - **Linux:** running as root, running in a container, AppArmor on Ubuntu 24.04+ (run `sh scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh`), user namespaces switched off, or a reason byway could not identify.
  - **macOS:** a slot that says macOS is not supported yet.
- No message suggests switching the sandbox off.
- A drift test runs the installed Playwright against a stand-in that prints each marker, so a Playwright upgrade that changes these words fails a test.

**In the app.**
- **Settings, "Page renderer's OS sandbox":**
  - The switch.
  - Turning it off asks first, with the owner's risk wording, and "Keep it on" has focus.
  - Under the switch: why the sandbox could not start and the fix, or "Turn the sandbox back on" when it is off and the last start-up check found it could start.
  - Focus returns to the switch when the question or the offer goes away, and a status line announces the change.
- **A banner on every screen** while the sandbox is off, or while it is on but could not start at start-up. It follows the demo banner's pattern and links to the Settings section.
- **A run note:** each step whose pages, page-text reads or cards were drawn with the sandbox off gets one note, shown in the Run timeline and in the Result's "How this was made".

**Linux.** CI's Linux job used to write an AppArmor profile inline. That profile is now `scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh`, a script the person runs as themselves (it asks for sudo). The README gives it, byway's message names it, CI runs it, and `ci.test.ts` holds all three to the same path. The README's Renderer section has a new "The OS sandbox" part covering each system's case, how to switch the sandbox off, and the risk.

Items:

- **RENDERER_OS_SANDBOX becomes a setting, on by default, read at each launch; a change retires the running browser (ADR-0106) without killing renders in flight** (done): The setting is read before every load. render-browsers.ts retires a browser launched with the other setting, with the reason "byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed". Tests: render-browsers.test.ts (stand-in browsers, no clock) and pages.test.ts (real Chromium: a render held part-way finishes on the sandboxed browser while the next render launches without the sandbox; the command lines confirm both).
- **Readiness exercises the sandboxed page renderer before 'ready' and before any paid step** (done): ready() draws byway's own test page with a script, once per browser; beginRun and the model step's readiness check use it. checkRenderer does the same at start-up. Mutation evidence: with ready() reduced to launch-only, two pages tests fail (red-04-ready-launch-only.log).
- **Plain message for Playwright's sandbox failure, drift test, per-OS reasons and fixes with a macOS slot** (done): sandbox.ts plus sandbox.test.ts. The drift test launches the installed Playwright 1.63 with Node standing in for Chromium and printing each marker. It checks the first line ('browserType.launch: Target page, context or browser has been closed') and the 'Chromium sandboxing failed!' log. It passed on Windows locally and on Linux and Windows in CI.
- **Setting lives in the store, written by apps/web Settings, read by the engine at launch; off only set by the person** (done): It is one setting for the data directory (migration 8, renderer_settings), not part of the per-workspace Preferences, because the engine has one renderer for every workspace. Settings writes it through PATCH /api/renderer; the worker reads it through storeOsSandbox(store). guard-sandbox.test.ts holds the sources to that.
- **Settings section: state, switch, confirm with plain risk, per-OS reason and fix, offer to turn back on; keyboard and screen-reader correct** (done): PageRendererSettings.tsx with view helpers in src/view/renderer.ts. Uses the existing Switch (it now accepts a ref), ActionButton, useAction and the confirm-inline pattern. Checked visually in light and dark at desktop and phone width.
- **Persistent banner on every screen while off, linking to Settings** (done): Shown by the root layout (role=note, demo banner pattern). It also shows when the sandbox is on but the last start-up check found it cannot start.
- **A note on each run whose pages were drawn with the sandbox off** (done): A new 'renderer-sandbox-off' event, written once per step call as its first page is drawn without the sandbox. Shown with the step's notes on the Run timeline and in Result's 'How this was made'. Tests: sandbox-note.test.ts and timeline.test.ts.
- **Start-up line says on/off and, when off, whether the sandbox could start now; the check records its result in the store** (done): rendererLine() and Store.recordRendererCheck. serve.test.ts holds serve.ts to reading the setting, then checking, then printing, then recording; e2e.ts requires the 'inside its OS sandbox' line.

The 23.10 correction is in ADR-0105 item 9 and in a later-notes bullet on ADR-0084. The 2026-10-08 verification report still says 23.10; I left it as a historical record.

Left open: **Not yet proven:**
- The AppArmor script has run only on CI's fresh Ubuntu 24.04 machine, not on a person's own Ubuntu install, and it covers Playwright's default browser folder only. It should be tried on the first real Linux machine byway runs on, and before Linux is announced. This is in the backlog row "What D2 left (ADR-0105)".
- Playwright's seccomp profile for running Chromium with its sandbox in a container is untested; the README says so.

**Known limits:**
- The banner, Settings' findings and the offer to turn the sandbox back on all come from the last start-up check. After someone applies a fix, they update only when byway starts again; runs do not wait for that.
- With the sandbox off, the start-up check launches Chromium a second time, with the sandbox, to test whether it could start. If that probe hangs rather than failing fast, the 30-second wait ends and the line says the renderer is "not available" even though it is ready. A sandbox that cannot start normally fails fast, so this is unlikely.
- If close() runs while a page load is reading the setting and the browser in use has the other setting, a browser can be launched after close(). A load that begins after close() could already do this before this change; the window is one SQLite read.
- When the test page fails for a reason Chromium did not give, the Windows advice in that message does not name the Chromium file. The launch-failure message and the start-up check do name it.

**Choices for the owner to review:**
- The setting is one per data directory rather than part of the per-workspace Preferences the brief named; ADR-0105 gives the reason.
- I edited builder 2's ADR-0106 item 3 on this branch. The rule allows it because ADR-0106 is not on main yet, but the coordinator may prefer to word it differently.
- The 2026-10-08 verification report still says "the default on Ubuntu 23.10 and later" in its appendices. I left that historical report unedited; ADR-0105 and the ADR-0084 note record the correction.

### The checks

Confirmed by the refuters (real and blocking): S1, S2, retire-2, WEB-1, DOC-1, W1, W2, W3.

#### Lens: sandbox (blocking findings)

What it checked: I worked in my own clone, C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-sandbox\repo, at 0da7bf0, after pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline. Probe scripts and their outputs are kept beside it in ...\scratch\d2\check-sandbox\. The worktree, main's checkout and byway_review were not touched. Nothing was pushed, nothing was stashed, no paid call was made, and no processes were left running. The scratch Chromium copy was removed.

**Verified, holds:**

1. **On by default on Windows, measured at the process level.**
   - Probe integrity.ts/.ps1 launches through playwrightPages, draws a page, and reads each Chromium child's token integrity level through OpenProcessToken/GetTokenInformation, filtered to the probe's own process tree.
   - Sandbox on (the default): browser 0x2000; gpu-process 0x1000 (Low); network service 0x2000; renderer 0x0000 (Untrusted); no --no-sandbox on any process.
   - Sandbox off: every process at 0x2000 with --no-sandbox.
   - Playwright 1.63's `if (options.chromiumSandbox !== true) push('--no-sandbox')` (coreBundle.js:43346) matches chromiumLaunchOptions.
   - A mutation making launchChromium ignore the setting fails 5 tests in launch.test.ts.
2. **Readiness exercises the page renderer.** ready() opens a context, navigates to byway's test page and requires the script's title '42', so a renderer process must run, and with the sandbox on renderers run at Untrusted. proven is set only after goto succeeds, per browser. beginRun (web-page runs) and the model step's rendererReady (three or more references with an image) call it before any spend.
3. **A sandbox made to fail for real on Windows** (a job object forbidding new desktops):
   - The start-up check correctly finds 'fails' through its probe without the sandbox, prints "not available: ... inside its OS sandbox ..." with the Windows reason, and the store records it.
   - The banner shows 'cannot-start' and Settings shows why, the fix and "Until it can, byway cannot draw pages".
   - With the setting off, the probe reports that the sandbox still cannot start.
   - beginRun refuses before any store write.
   - The setting stays on throughout, and nothing switches it.
   - Defects: the run's message (S2) and the named file (S1).
4. **Never automatic.**
   - setRendererSandbox is called only from api.ts's updateRenderer, reached only through PATCH /api/renderer.
   - recordRendererCheck never changes os_sandbox (a new row is written with 1).
   - A setting that cannot be read counts as on: wanted() returns `!== false` and treats a throw as on, and serve.ts catches a failed read as on.
   - The CSRF/Host guard in proxy.ts/guard.ts covers the route. Rendered pages cannot reach localhost, which is not on the allowlist.
5. **Per-OS messages.**
   - Linux order: root (uid 0, or Chromium's crbug.com/638180 marker, which I confirmed survives in Playwright's call log in a probe), then container (/.dockerenv, /run/.containerenv, the container variable, cgroup), then AppArmor (=1), then user namespaces off, then unknown.
   - macOS and other systems: the 'not supported yet' slot.
   - No message suggests switching the sandbox off.
6. **Drift test against the installed Playwright.** doRewriteStartupLog in playwright-core 1.63.0 coreBundle.js (about line 43206) uses exactly the markers 'crbug.com/357670', 'No usable sandbox!' and 'crbug.com/638180', and writes 'Chromium sandboxing failed!', matching sandbox.ts. The test checks the first line and the 'Browser logs:' text, so it would catch a rewrite.
7. **Linux README steps against verify.yml.** Both run `sh scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh`, and the profile glob matches Playwright 1.63's Linux paths (chrome-headless-shell-linux64/chrome-headless-shell, chrome-linux64/chrome).
   - CI run 39137471023 at e36a0aa was read with gh run view (read-only): both jobs succeeded. The Linux log shows "kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1", the profile added, and "e2e: renderer Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox"; the Windows log shows the same line.
   - The 23.10/24.04 correction matches published sources (Openwall oss-security and Qualys: restriction introduced in 23.10, on by default from 24.04).
8. **Tests:** sandbox, renderer-check, launch, render-browsers, sandbox-note, renderer-settings, guard-sandbox, renderer (web), serve and ci all pass here: 10 files, 127 tests. The ADR-0105 tests in pages.test.ts pass: 4. None depends on wall-clock time.

- **S1** (blocking, false-ui-copy), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2222-2229 (executableOf), used at pages.ts:2242 (launchChromium) and pages.ts:2297-2305 (checkRenderer's sandboxFails); sandbox.ts:124; README.md:149; docs/adr/0105-...md:36

  On Windows, the fix byway shows names the wrong executable. executableOf() calls chromium.executablePath(), which in Playwright 1.63 returns full Chromium (registry 'chromium': coreBundle.js:39661-39662, chromium-&lt;rev>\chrome-win64\chrome.exe). byway launches headless, and for headless Playwright runs 'chromium-headless-shell' (coreBundle.js:43375), so the file that is actually blocked is chromium_headless_shell-&lt;rev>\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe. Allowing the named chrome.exe in security software does not unblock the renderer, and after `playwright install --only-shell` the named file does not exist. The start-up line, Settings' 'fix' and the stored check all carry this path. README:149 and ADR-0105 decision 4 say 'the message names the file'.

  Evidence: Probe .probe/exe.ts (copy at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-sandbox\exe.ts):
  - fix byway gives: "Allow Playwright's Chromium (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1243\chrome-win64\chrome.exe) in that software..."
  - what launchChromium runs (Win32_Process children of the probe): C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium_headless_shell-1243\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe
  
  In the real sandbox failure (S2's setup), flow.out shows Settings' found.fix and the start-up line both naming chrome-win64\chrome.exe.
  
  No test can catch this: launch.test.ts mocks executablePath() to EXECUTABLE, and sandbox.test.ts:78 hands in a headless-shell path itself.

  Suggested fix: Name the file byway actually launches. Either resolve the headless-shell executable (Playwright's registry entry 'chromium-headless-shell'), or derive it from the revision and check that it exists, falling back to Playwright's browsers folder. Add a real-Chromium test that compares the named path with argv[0] of a browser launched with chromiumLaunchOptions(STATED) plus --enable-automation, read through the existing commandLine() helper (Browser.getBrowserCommandLine). Until then, change README:149 and ADR-0105:36 (the ADR is not on main yet).

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I could not refute it. byway always launches Chromium headless with no channel and no executablePath (chromiumLaunchOptions, pages.ts:310), and for that Playwright 1.63 runs 'chromium-headless-shell' (coreBundle.js:43375). executableOf() asks chromium.executablePath(), which returns the registry's full 'chromium' entry (coreBundle.js:39661-39662), so every Windows "fix" that names a file names chrome.exe instead of the binary byway runs. This happens every time that message is shown, not only in an edge case.
  
  It is reachable through the start-up line and Settings: checkRenderer's sandboxFails runs both when the sandbox is on (an unmarked failure, then success without the sandbox) and when it is off (the probe with the sandbox fails). It is also reachable through launchFailure on a marked launch failure.
  
  The registry also builds the path without checking the file exists. After `--only-shell` the named file is not there, and after any Playwright upgrade the revision in the path changes.
  
  README:149 and ADR-0105:36 say "the message names the file", which is false as built. The code's own intent is the headless shell: launch.test.ts:34 and sandbox.test.ts:78 hand in chrome-headless-shell paths, and allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:5 says the headless shell is what byway draws pages with. The mock in launch.test.ts hides the mismatch, so no test can catch it.
  
  The only arguments against "blocking" are that a Windows sandbox failure is rare, and that the "why" sentence still points at security software. But the one actionable instruction on the platform the owner asked for now (Windows) is wrong whenever it is shown, the docs make a false claim, the ADR is not on main yet, and the fix is small. The Linux branch is not affected: the AppArmor profile glob already covers chrome-headless-shell (ci.test.ts:111).

  Evidence: Reproduced in my own clone (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-sandbox-S1\repo, branch fix/fx-d2 at 0da7bf0, Playwright 1.63.0). Probes are under packages/runner/.probe/ in that clone.
  
  1. .probe/exe2.ts
  - chromium.executablePath() = C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1243\chrome-win64\chrome.exe
  - argv[0] of a browser launched with chromiumLaunchOptions({osSandbox:true}) plus --enable-automation (Browser.getBrowserCommandLine) = C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium_headless_shell-1243\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe
  - same file: false
  - The production launchChromium({osSandbox:true}), read from Win32_Process children of the probe, runs ...\chromium_headless_shell-1243\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe.
  
  2. .probe/check.ts: checkRenderer with a stand-in launch that fails only with the sandbox on (no marker) and a Windows host.
  - Sandbox on: sandbox 'fails', with fix "Allow Playwright's Chromium (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1243\chrome-win64\chrome.exe) in that software, ..."
  - Sandbox off: the probe's fix names the same chrome.exe.
  - checkRenderer gets the path from the real import('playwright') in sandboxFails, so this is the production path that feeds the start-up line and Settings.
  
  3. Code
  - coreBundle.js:43375 `return options.headless ? "chromium-headless-shell" : "chromium";`
  - coreBundle.js:33137-33139: findExecutablePath only joins path parts and never checks the file exists.
  - browsers.json: chromium and chromium-headless-shell are both at revision 1243.
  
  The worktree and the main checkout are untouched (git status clean).

  Fix: 1. Name the binary byway launches. Replace executableOf() in pages.ts with a helper that works it out from chromium.executablePath(), using Playwright's public API only:
     - Take browserDir = dirname(dirname(full)). It must match /^chromium-(\d+)$/.
     - folder = dirname(browserDir) (Playwright's browsers folder; this respects PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH).
     - shell = join(folder, `chromium_headless_shell-${rev}`, 'chrome-headless-shell-win64', 'chrome-headless-shell.exe').
     - If existsSync(shell), return { executable: shell }.
     - Otherwise return { folder } with no file. sandboxAdvice's Windows fix then reads "Allow the Chromium in Playwright's browsers folder (&lt;folder>) ...", so byway never names a file that is not there.
     I checked this prototype (.probe/derive.ts in my clone): the path it builds matches argv[0] of the real headless launch exactly.
     - Alternatives: (a) parse the `<launching> (.+?\.exe)(?= |$)` line from the launch error's call log, which is exact but only exists for launch-time failures, not readiness or test-page failures; (b) the internal registry via playwright-core/lib/coreBundle (`.registry.registry.findExecutable('chromium-headless-shell')`), which works at 1.63.0 but is an internal shape and needs a direct playwright-core dependency. The derivation plus the drift test below is the proportionate choice.
  
  2. Add a drift test with real Chromium (Windows CI already runs real-Chromium tests). Launch with chromiumLaunchOptions({osSandbox:true}) plus --enable-automation, read argv[0] through pages.test.ts's existing commandLine() helper, and expect it to equal the helper's executable, compared without regard to case on Windows. Skip the test off Windows, or make the helper per platform.
  
  3. Fix the tests that hide the bug. launch.test.ts:35 mocks executablePath() to return the headless-shell path itself. Mock it to return the full-Chromium path that Playwright really returns, and expect the derived headless-shell path in the message (and the folder fallback when the file is missing). sandbox.test.ts:78 can stay as it is, since it tests sandboxAdvice's wording.
  
  4. Docs. Keep README:149 and ADR-0105:36 ("the message names the file") only once (1) lands, and add "or, when that file is missing, Playwright's browsers folder". If the code fix is deferred, change both now to "allow Playwright's Chromium (chrome-headless-shell.exe under Playwright's browsers folder)".
  
  5. Also consider mentioning the folder alongside the file, or instead of it. The revision in the file path (-1243) changes with every Playwright upgrade, so an allowlist entry for one file stops working after an upgrade. This is a judgment call; record it in the ADR either way.

- **S2** (blocking, wrong-behaviour / false ADR and code-doc claim), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:339-347 (launchFailure), 2251-2252 (readyFailure passes a launch StepError through), 701-726 (ready); lifecycle.ts:28 (beginRun); model-step.ts:189-197; contradicts docs/adr/0105-...md:79 and pages.ts:271-273

  On Windows, a sandbox that really cannot start makes the run's refusal blame a missing install instead of the sandbox. Chromium never prints the three Linux markers there. In a real failure the browser itself dies at launch, so launchFailure() returns the generic message. ready() passes that StepError through unchanged (readyFailure's first line), and beginRun refuses the web-page run with: "The page renderer could not start (Target page, context or browser has been closed)... If Playwright's Chromium is not installed: pnpm ... playwright install chromium". That message has no sandbox, no Windows reason and no fix, and its advice is wrong because Chromium is installed. The same start shows a banner and Settings saying the sandbox could not start, so the run's message contradicts them. ADR-0105 Consequences (line 79) says the machine 'is told why and how to fix it ... in a run's failure', and ready()'s doc (pages.ts:272) says a sandbox failure 'says why on this computer and the fix'. Neither holds for this failure on Windows. The spend guard itself holds: nothing is written or spent before ready().

  Evidence: Real failure on this Windows 11 machine, scoped to my own process tree. .probe/nodesktop.ps1 runs a probe inside a job object with JOB_OBJECT_UILIMIT_DESKTOP; Chromium's sandbox needs an alternate desktop, and Chromium without the sandbox needs none.
  - whydie.out: the sandboxed browser exits with exitCode=2147483651 (0x80000003) and prints no marker.
  - nodesktop.out:
    - ready() with the sandbox on: REJECTED StepError "The page renderer could not start (Target page, context or browser has been closed), ... If Playwright's Chromium is not installed: pnpm --filter ./apps/web exec playwright install chromium"
    - ready() with the sandbox off: ready
    - checkRenderer(true): {ready:false, sandbox:'fails'} with the Windows advice
  - flow.out (real store in a temp folder):
    - setting before: true
    - start-up line "not available: The page renderer could not start inside its OS sandbox..."
    - recorded lastCheck.sandbox 'fails'; banner kind 'cannot-start'
    - beginRun: REFUSED (StepError) with the generic install message; calls after ready: [] (no updateRun or appendEvent)
    - setting at the end: true
  
  The tests pin the generic message: launch.test.ts:222-231 ('Chromium died' with osSandbox true expects launchFailure(DIED, {osSandbox:true}), the install wording, from pages.ready()).

  Suggested fix: With the sandbox on, a launch failure that has no marker and is not a missing executable ("Executable doesn't exist") should name the sandbox as one possible cause, with sandboxAdvice for this OS, as possibleSandboxCause already does for a test page that fails. Do this in launchFailure when opts.osSandbox is true, or in readyFailure. Alternatively, ready() can use the store's last start-up check when it found 'fails'. Change launch.test.ts's 'Chromium died' case to expect that wording, and add a Windows-only test that reproduces the real failure with the job-object trick: run checkRenderer and ready() in a child process assigned to a JOB_OBJECT_UILIMIT_DESKTOP job. It is deterministic and needs no clock. Otherwise reword ADR-0105:79 and pages.ts:272 so they do not claim it.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I reproduced it in my own clone and the claim holds. On Windows, a sandbox failure that kills the browser at launch never names the sandbox. A web-page run is then refused with Playwright's "Target page, context or browser has been closed" and the install-Chromium hint, while the banner and Settings say the sandbox could not start.
  
  The marker branch in launchFailure cannot fire on Windows. Its three markers ("No usable sandbox!", crbug 357670 and 638180) are printed only by Chromium on Linux. readyFailure passes the launch StepError through unchanged, so possibleSandboxCause is never reached. ADR-0105 Consequences line 79 ("in a run's failure") and the ready() doc (pages.ts:271-273) are therefore false on the platform where the owner works. ADR Context line 21 names this exact message ("would have blamed a missing install") as the problem D2 fixes.
  
  What I tried in order to refute it:
  - **The trigger is contrived (a job object).** It is a stand-in, but the gap does not depend on the trigger. Any marker-less launch death with the sandbox on gets the generic text, and on Windows every such death is marker-less.
  - **The person is not left uninformed.** The start-up check, the banner on every screen and Settings all diagnose it correctly, and nothing is spent. That limits the harm.
  - **ADR Decision §4 scopes the sandbox message to marker failures and to test-page failures.** But Consequences and the ready() doc claim more than that.
  
  So it is real. It blocks as a false claim in an accepted ADR and its code doc, and as a run message that contradicts the banner on the same screen. The user-facing harm is moderate, because the banner tells the truth.

  Evidence: Clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-sandbox-S2\repo at 0da7bf0. The probes are in packages/runner/.probe/ (probe.mts, flow.mts and nodesktop.ps1, plus the outputs nodesktop.out and flow.out). nodesktop.ps1 starts its own powershell, puts that process in a job with JobObjectBasicUIRestrictions = 0x40 (JOB_OBJECT_UILIMIT_DESKTOP), then runs the probe. Only my own process tree is affected.
  
  **Baseline, no job:**
  - Raw launch draws the page with the sandbox on and off.
  - ready() resolves with the sandbox on and off.
  - checkRenderer(true) returns {ready:true, sandbox:'starts'}.
  - beginRun starts and writes updateRun and appendEvent.
  
  **Inside the job (nodesktop.out):**
  - Raw launch with the sandbox on fails with "browserType.launch: Target page, context or browser has been closed". The call log shows "process did exit: exitCode=2147483651" (0x80000003, a CHECK breakpoint). There are no [err] lines and no marker, and isSandboxFailure is false.
  - Raw launch with the sandbox off: ok, title 42.
  - ready() with the sandbox on rejects with StepError "The page renderer could not start (Target page, context or browser has been closed), so pages, and the cards ... cannot be drawn. If Playwright's Chromium is not installed: pnpm --filter ./apps/web exec playwright install chromium".
  - ready() with the sandbox off resolves.
  - checkRenderer(true) returns {ready:false, sandbox:'fails'} with the Windows why and fix.
  
  **beginRun inside the job (flow.out, store stubbed, run medium 'web-page'):** REFUSED with the same generic StepError, and no store writes after ready ([]).
  
  **The tests pin this.** launch.test.ts:222-231 ('Chromium died' with osSandbox true expects the generic text from pages.ready()). The 9 tests pass.
  
  **Nothing else catches it.** Run starts are not gated on lastCheck: lastCheck is read only in apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts, for the banner. So the run's stored failure says "install" while the banner says "sandbox".
  
  **Adjacent defect, same evidence.** The Windows fix names the wrong file. checkRenderer advised "Allow Playwright's Chromium (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1243\chrome-win64\chrome.exe)". The process actually launched was chromium_headless_shell-1243\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe, as the &lt;launching> line shows. executableOf() uses chromium.executablePath(), which returns the full Chromium, not the headless shell that headless:true runs. launch.test.ts's EXECUTABLE fixture assumes the headless shell, which hides this.
  
  Nothing was written to the worktree, main or byway_review; no paid calls; no push.

  Fix: 1. **launchFailure (pages.ts:339).** Add a branch after the marker branch for when all of these hold:
     - opts.osSandbox === true (strictly true, so launchFailure(DIED) with no options keeps its text);
     - the error has no marker;
     - its first line does not match /Executable doesn't exist/.
  
     Return a launch-specific message. Do not reuse possibleSandboxCause, whose "started but could not draw byway's test page" is wrong for a launch. For example: "The page renderer could not start (&lt;launchSaid>), so pages, and the cards that show a reviewer the examples beside the work, cannot be drawn. One possible cause is that its OS sandbox cannot run on this computer; if so: &lt;why> &lt;fix> If Playwright's Chromium is not installed: &lt;INSTALL_CHROMIUM>". Keep the install hint, because a marker-less death can also be a broken install. Every renderer path (render, card, ready, lineup) then gets the same message, which keeps launch.test's "same message on every path" property.
  
  2. **checkRenderer (pages.ts ~2321).** When both attempts fail ('unknown'), report the attempt made without the sandbox: message plain(false, without.error), not plain(true, first.error). Otherwise step 1 makes the start-up line blame the sandbox just after the check proved Chromium also fails without it. renderer-check.test.ts:126 (MISSING) still passes.
  
  3. **Name the executable that actually runs in the Windows fix.** Take it from the error's "&lt;launching> &lt;path>" log line, which every Playwright launch error carries, in launchChromium and in checkRenderer's sandboxFails. Fall back to no path rather than to chromium.executablePath(), which returns chrome-win64\chrome.exe while headless:true runs chrome-headless-shell.exe.
  
  4. **Tests.**
     - launch.test.ts 'Chromium died' row: expect the new wording, which contains "One possible cause is that its OS sandbox" and the Windows why and fix.
     - Add a row: MISSING with osSandbox true keeps the plain install message.
     - renderer-check: a DIED failure in both attempts reports the message from the attempt without the sandbox.
     - Windows-only (skipIf not win32) real test: a child powershell does Add-Type with CreateJobObject, SetInformationJobObject(class 4, 0x40) and AssignProcessToJobObject(self), then runs a node script. Inside the job, playwrightPages({osSandbox:()=>true}).ready() rejects with the sandbox-named message, and the sandbox-off ready() resolves. It is deterministic and needs no clock. The probe scripts in my clone's packages/runner/.probe/ are a working template.
  
  5. **Docs.**
     - ADR-0105 Decision §4: add a bullet for a launch that fails with the sandbox on and no marker, other than a missing executable.
     - Keep Consequences line 79 and pages.ts:271-273 true by citing that bullet.
     - If the code is not changed, the minimum is to reword line 79 and the ready() doc: on Windows a run's failure does not name the sandbox; the start-up line, Settings and the banner do.

- **S3** (non-blocking, script false success), at scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:19-22,38

  On Linux, Playwright's default browser folder is $XDG_CACHE_HOME/ms-playwright when XDG_CACHE_HOME is set (coreBundle.js computeDefaultCacheDirectory, about line 32664). The script refuses only when PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH is set. It always writes the AppArmor profile for $HOME/.cache/ms-playwright and then prints "Playwright's Chromium may start its OS sandbox now". With XDG_CACHE_HOME pointing elsewhere, the profile does not cover the Chromium Playwright installed, and the sandbox still cannot start after a run that reported success.

  Evidence: playwright-core 1.63.0 lib/coreBundle.js: `if (process.platform === "linux") return process.env.XDG_CACHE_HOME || path.join(os.homedir(), ".cache");`, then defaultRegistryDirectory() = join(that, "ms-playwright"). The script's line 38 hard-codes "$HOME/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium*/..." and checks only PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH (line 19).

  Suggested fix: Use "${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-$HOME/.cache}/ms-playwright" in the profile, applying the same whitespace check to it, or refuse when XDG_CACHE_HOME is set to anything other than $HOME/.cache, as the script does for PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH. Mention it in the README's AppArmor bullet.

#### Lens: retire (blocking findings)

What it checked: I checked this in my own clone (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-retire\repo at 0da7bf0), installed with pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline. I did not touch the worktree, main or byway_review, and pushed nothing. I left the clone clean: git status is empty, and the experiments are kept in ...\check-retire\experiments\.

**The original hazard, on the parent (7eabcfe) against the branch** (zz-e1-repro, zz-stall-*, zz-e10-e4b):
- Held close plus co-tenant. On the parent, B failed with the CI's exact text "The page could not be drawn: the page renderer failed while drawing it (Target page, context or browser has been closed)." On the branch, B rendered: A's late context retired the browser, and the browser closed after B.
- The investigation's 6 s SQLite stall of the engine's thread. On the parent, B failed with the same text. On the branch, B was ok.
- E4b (a suspended Chromium, and a render waiting in newContext with a 3 s deadline and a cancel at 4 s). On the parent it was still pending at 12 s. On the branch it failed plainly at 5.0 s (3 + 1 + 1).

**Trying to break the branch** (zz-e2-break), all with real Chromium:
- **Truly hung Chromium** (browser process suspended): loads ended at 5.0 s and 6.0 s, within T + 2C. The next load launched another browser and worked in 206 ms. The hung browser's processes were gone after Playwright's 30 s kill.
- **24 overlapping renders, reads and readiness checks**, every third close 1.5 s late: none failed, 4 launches, and at most 1 browser was still connected before close(); close() left 0.
- **A retired browser killed with taskkill**: the load on it failed with the bare crash text (as the ADR says), the next render worked, and no PIDs were left.
- **Sandbox setting flipped during 16 renders**: launches followed the setting (false/true ×3), none failed, and close() left 0 browsers connected.
- **pages.close() during retirement**: loads on the retired and the current browser both said "byway closed the page renderer as it shut down". close() took 85 ms and no PIDs were left.
- **newContext arriving after the deadline**: 500 ms late, the context was closed as it opened and the browser kept; 3 s late, the browser was retired and closed with byway's reason, then the newContext failed.
- **Fuzz of render-browsers.ts** (stand-ins, real short timers; 80 seeds with launch failures, crashes, hung closes, flips, find() and close()): no count leak, no close under a held load, everything settled and closed. Without a timing margin, two seeds showed the backstop firing within timer lag of a load held exactly T + C. In pages.ts a load's deadline timer is always due first, so this cannot change a load's result.
- **Hypotheses I tested and rejected**: window.close() in a page is ignored (it never produces the bare text). A close while a read is inside page.evaluate is named correctly ("page.evaluate: byway closed …").

**Mutations** (tools/mutate.mjs, each source restored and checked byte for byte; logs in ...\check-retire\mut\):
- Caught: launch path not counted (8 tests), closedWith set after the close (1), always retire (4), setting change kills instead of retiring (2), dispose ignores a context not yet open (5), backstop at timeoutMs only (1), release ignores the reason (1).
- Survived: the StepError guard and the aborted guard in loaded()'s RendererClosed mapping, and the !k.backstop guard in get()'s loop. No test or document claims to cover these narrow races, so I do not report them.

**Gate on 0da7bf0**: vitest packages/runner 52 files / 1,280 tests passed; scripts/test 7 files / 105 passed; pnpm -s typecheck exit 0. CI run 39137471023 (read with gh run view) succeeded on Windows and Linux, and pages.test.ts (61 tests) and render-browsers.test.ts (13 tests) ran on both.

**Records**: the evidence ADR-0106 cites exists (the CI log, diagnose-closed-result.json, probe-reason.log, inv1/skeptic1). The commit it cites, 99b52f1 (2026-10-07), introduced the late-close escalation. ADR-0103's index row carries the changed-by link to ADR-0106, and adr-records.test.ts passes.

**Not run**: pnpm e2e.

**Processes**: I killed every process I started; no process with check-retire in its command line remains. Three chrome-headless-shell processes from 2026-10-17 (PIDs 59344, 55248, 45028), whose parents are gone, are not mine and I left them.

- **retire-1** (blocking, false-statement-adr), at docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md:92 (Consequences, "More than one Chromium at once"), and :55 (Decision 7, third bullet)

  ADR-0106 says a retired browser runs "each for at most 35 s after it is retired" and "A hung Chromium is closed once its last load ends". Neither holds for a Chromium that does not answer, which is the case this record is about. byway only asks such a browser to close at that point. Playwright kills it 30 s later, so it lives up to about 65 s after it is retired with the defaults, not 35 s.

  Evidence: Experiment E9, my clone at 0da7bf0 (experiments/zz-e9-hung-timeline.test.ts). Settings were timeoutMs 4000 and closeTimeoutMs 1000, so the ADR's bound is 5 s after retirement. I suspended the browser process with NtSuspendProcess (tools/suspend.ps1).
  - 1,077 ms: suspended.
  - 5,989 ms: load A ended with "The page did not finish rendering within 4 s". This is when the browser was retired; A was not the last load on it.
  - 11,017 ms: browser.close("byway closed the page renderer after a page in it took more than 1 s to close"), at the co-tenant B's release.
  - 41,369 ms: every process of the hung browser was gone, 35.4 s after retirement against the 5 s the ADR's formula gives.
  E2 shows the same: processes alive until about 36.5 s, retirement at about 5 s.
  Cause, in Playwright 1.63's own code (node_modules/.pnpm/playwright-core@1.63.0/.../lib/coreBundle.js):
  - :39848 `close: () => closeOrKill(... DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT)`
  - :39831-39843 closeOrKill races gracefullyClose against a 30 s timer, then calls kill(), which is taskkill /T /F on Windows.
  - DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT = 3e4.
  With the defaults, byway's close comes at most timeoutMs + closeTimeoutMs = 35 s after retirement, and the process can live 30 s past that. Decision 2 (:40) already says "after that, Playwright's own close goes on, with its kill fallback", so line 92 contradicts the record's own Decision.

  Suggested fix: ADR-0106 is not on main yet, so fix the wording on the branch before its Decision freezes.
  - Line 92: "Two can run at once, or more in a cascade. byway closes each at most 35 s after it is retired; one whose browser process does not answer is killed by Playwright up to 30 s after that close, so up to about 65 s after it was retired."
  - Line 55: "byway closes a hung Chromium once its last load ends, and in any case within the backstop; if it does not exit, Playwright kills it 30 s after that close."
  No code change is needed.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: no.

  Reason: Line 92 really is false, but line 55 is not, and that splits the claim. The sentence that is false sits in Consequences, which does not freeze at merge. The sentence that does freeze, in Decision 7, is true in the Decision's own wording.
  
  - **Line 92 is false.** It says retired browsers run "each for at most 35 s after it is retired". When the browser process does not answer, byway only calls browser.close() at the 35 s bound and stops waiting after closeTimeoutMs (render-browsers.ts:91-99). Playwright then kills the process up to 30 s later. I reproduced 65.3 s with the default limits.
  - **Line 55 is not false.** Decision 2 (line 40) uses "closed" for byway's close: "A retired browser is closed no later than timeoutMs + closeTimeoutMs ... Closing a retired browser waits at most closeTimeoutMs; after that, Playwright's own close goes on, with its kill fallback." Decision 7's bullet "A hung Chromium is closed once its last load ends, and in any case within the backstop" uses the same word in the same sense, and in that sense it is true.
  - **It is not blocking.** Only Context and Decision become final on main (docs/adr/README.md:11; scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts checks only those parts). Line 92 can therefore be corrected even after merge. The claim's urgency ("before its Decision freezes") only applies to line 55, which is true.
  - **There is no behaviour change.** Relying on Playwright's 30 s kill is how the old code worked too, and Context line 29 says so ("34.6 s, through Playwright's own 30 s kill"). SEC-5's guarantees still hold, because no render, step or byway itself waits on that kill.
  - **Still worth fixing now.** It is one wrong number in a fresh record, and the edit costs a line.

  Evidence: My own clone of fix/fx-d2 at 0da7bf0, at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-retire-retire-1\repo. The experiment is experiments/retire-lifetime.ts, with experiments/suspend.ps1 calling NtSuspendProcess.
  
  Setup: the real launchChromium and renderBrowsers with the default limits (timeoutMs 30000, closeTimeoutMs 5000). Two loads take the browser, the chrome-headless-shell main process is suspended, the browser is retired, and load A is released.
  
  Run 1: co-tenant B keeps holding the browser, so the backstop fires.
  - 1,329 ms: retired.
  - 36,335 ms: byway calls browser.close("byway closed the page renderer after a page in it took more than 5 s to close").
  - 66,652 ms: the main process is gone, **65.3 s after retirement**. Only then did 'disconnected' fire and close() settle.
  
  Run 2: B is released at once, so the count path closes the browser.
  - 1,388 ms: byway calls close().
  - 6,395 ms: byway's shut returns, after closeTimeoutMs.
  - 31,593 ms: the process is gone, 30.2 s after retirement.
  
  So "closed when its last load ends" means byway's close call, and the process outlives that call by about 30 s.
  
  Cause, in playwright-core 1.63.0 (node_modules/.pnpm/playwright-core@1.63.0/.../lib/coreBundle.js):
  - The server's Browser._close (:52692-52697) awaits browserProcess.close().
  - That is closeOrKill(DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT) (:39848). DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT is 3e4 (:4594).
  - closeOrKill races gracefullyClose against that timer, then calls kill() (:39831-39843).
  - Chromium's graceful close only sends the CDP message Browser.close (:43222-43224). A suspended browser never answers it.
  - kill() is `taskkill /pid N /T /F` on Windows (:9393-9402).
  
  Sections and wording:
  - docs/adr/README.md:11 says "A record's Context and Decision are final once it reaches main's first-parent history as Accepted". Consequences are not frozen.
  - Line 92 is under Consequences. Line 55 is under Decision, and Decision 2 at line 40 already names the kill fallback.
  - `git grep` finds no other doc stating a 35 s process lifetime. pages.ts:20-22 and render-browsers.ts:8-11 describe byway's close and say Playwright's close goes on, which is accurate.
  - ADR-0106 is not on main yet: `git merge-base --is-ancestor c31f107 origin/main` is false.

  Fix: Edit only the documentation, on the branch (ADR-0106 is not on main yet). No code change is needed.
  
  1. Line 92 (Consequences), required. Replace the second sentence with: "Two can run at once, or more in a cascade. byway closes each at most `timeoutMs + closeTimeoutMs` after it is retired (35 s with the defaults). One whose browser process does not answer that close is killed by Playwright's own fallback 30 s later (a fixed Playwright timeout, not a byway setting), so it can run up to about 65 s after it was retired." Keep the rest of the bullet. A hung page's renderer still stops at once, because the browser process answers the close: stuck pages closed in 4 to 5 ms, as the Context records.
  
  2. Line 55 (Decision 7), optional, and only before merge, because Decision freezes on main. It is accurate as it stands, given Decision 2's wording. For readers who take "closed" to mean the process is gone, it could read: "byway closes a hung Chromium once its last load ends, and in any case within the backstop. If it does not answer, Playwright kills it 30 s after that close (Decision 2)."
  
  Keep the 30 s tied to Playwright's DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT in closeOrKill, so that no one later scales it with byway's limits.

- **retire-2** (blocking, false-statement-adr), at docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md:46 (Decision 5) and :51 (Decision 6); README.md:196

  Two more Decision statements in ADR-0106 are untrue as written, and its Decision freezes once it reaches main.
  (a) Decision 5 gives only two close reasons: "… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close" for "a retired browser, at its last load or its backstop", or "… as it shut down". Since ADR-0105 (same branch), a browser retired because the sandbox setting changed is closed at its last load or its backstop with a third reason, "byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed".
  (b) Decision 6 says "When a job of verify.yml fails, it uploads two artifacts", and README.md:196 says "A failed job also keeps two artifacts". A job that fails at the typecheck or the unit tests uploads nothing, and one whose suite never started uploads only the server log.

  Evidence: (a) packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:
  - :69 defines `changedReason = `${BYWAY_CLOSED} after its OS sandbox setting changed``.
  - :127 calls `retireWith(b, changedReason)`.
  - The close uses that reason at the last release (:161, `k.reason ?? retiredReason`) and at the backstop (:108).
  - ADR-0105:33 documents it, but ADR-0106:46 still lists two reasons.
  - My mutation MX28 (release ignores k.reason) is caught by render-browsers.test.ts "a change retires the browser in use…", so the code does give the third reason.
  
  (b) package.json:19 is `"verify": "pnpm -s typecheck && pnpm test && pnpm e2e"`, so a typecheck or unit failure never runs scripts/e2e.ts.
  - byway-e2e-server.log (scripts/e2e.ts:81) and apps/web/test-results then do not exist, and both upload steps use `if-no-files-found: ignore` (verify.yml).
  - A suite that fails in waitUntilReady leaves the log but no test-results.
  - The workflow's own comment says so: "A run that fails before the suite starts has neither, and uploads nothing."
  - Run 39079760987, cited in ADR-0103's note, is such a case: a unit test failed and the browser tests did not run.

  Suggested fix: Fix both on the branch, before ADR-0106 reaches main.
  - Decision 5: add "… after its OS sandbox setting changed" (a browser retired for a setting change, ADR-0105), or say the retired reason depends on why the browser was retired.
  - Decision 6 and README.md:196: say the artifacts are kept when the browser tests ran and failed, and that a job that fails earlier (typecheck or unit tests) uploads nothing. Match the wording of the workflow's own comment.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I couldn't refute either part. Both statements in ADR-0106's Decision are untrue of the code and workflow on fix/fx-d2.
  
  (a) Decision 5 lists two close reasons, and its parenthesis ties every retired browser to the "5 s" one. The code has a third: a browser retired because the OS sandbox setting changed. It is closed with "byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed", at its last load and at its backstop.
  
  (b) Decision 6 says a failed job "uploads two artifacts", and README:196 says a failed job "keeps two artifacts". In fact a failed job uploads 0, 1 or 2:
  - 0 if it fails before scripts/e2e.ts runs (the install, the typecheck, the unit tests).
  - 1, the server log only, if e2e fails before the suite (waitUntilReady), or after a suite that passed (the renderer line, the "checked" line, the key-leak check, an unclean stop).
  - 2 only when a browser test itself failed.
  
  It is blocking for merge, not for behaviour. docs/adr/README.md:11 makes a record's Context and Decision final once it reaches main. scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts fails any later edit, so after the merge only a correcting ADR could fix these lines. Branch commits are drafts, so fixing them now is a cheap text edit. ADR-0105 has already edited ADR-0106's item 3 on this branch for the same reason.
  
  One weak point in the checker's evidence, which does not change the verdict: run 39079760987 predates the upload steps, so it shows nothing about uploads. The argument rests on the workflow and the scripts, not on that run.

  Evidence: I worked in my own clone, C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-retire-retire-2\repo, at 0da7bf0. ADR-0106 is not on main: c31f107 is in origin/main..HEAD.
  
  (a) packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:
  - :69 defines changedReason.
  - :127 calls retireWith(b, changedReason).
  - :107-108 stores k.reason and the backstop closes with it.
  - :161 closes at the last release with k.reason ?? retiredReason.
  
  To reproduce, I wrote a temporary vitest in the clone (stand-in browsers, fake timers, osSandbox switched true to false), ran it, then deleted it. It printed:
  - `backstop reason: ["byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed"]`
  - `last-load reason: ["byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed"]`
  
  No close used the "took more than 5 s" reason. The existing test render-browsers.test.ts:241-257 also expects CHANGED.
  
  pages.ts:645-646 shows the reason in what the person sees: closedWith(b) feeds RendererClosed. So a load stopped at the backstop of such a browser reports "…after its OS sandbox setting changed", a message ADR-0106 decision 5 says is never given.
  
  ADR-0105:33 documents the third reason. But ADR-0105:59 says ADR-0106 "is used as it stands", with only item 3 extended.
  
  (b) Why a failed job does not always upload two artifacts:
  - package.json `verify` = `pnpm -s typecheck && pnpm test && pnpm e2e`.
  - The only writer of byway-e2e-server.log is scripts/e2e.ts:81, which runs only when e2e starts.
  - apps/web/test-results is Playwright's outputDir (playwright.config.ts:29).
  - Both upload steps in verify.yml use `if-no-files-found: ignore`.
  - scripts/e2e.ts:112-160 can set the exit code to 1 after a suite that passed (the renderer line, the "checked" line, the leak check, an unclean stop). With `trace: retain-on-failure`, that leaves the log and no traces.
  - The workflow's own comment (verify.yml:93-96) says "A run that fails before the suite starts has neither, and uploads nothing."
  
  grep found the same overbroad wording in ADR-0106:88 (Consequences) and README.md:196. No test pins the README sentence.

  Fix: Fix this on fix/fx-d2 before merging. It is text only; no code changes.
  
  1. ADR-0106 Decision 5, line 46. Make the list complete, and word it so a later cause of retirement does not make it false again. For example: "Every browser close byway makes gives Playwright a reason that starts 'byway closed the page renderer' and says why: '… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close' (a browser retired for a late close, at its last load or its backstop), '… after its OS sandbox setting changed' (a browser retired for a change of that setting, ADR-0105, at its last load or its backstop) or '… as it shut down' (`pages.close()`). A record that retires a browser for another cause gives it its own reason with the same start."
  
  2. ADR-0105, line 59 ("What this changes in earlier records"). Say that ADR-0106's item 5 was also extended on this branch before it reached main, with the third reason, next to item 3. Today it says item 3 alone.
  
  3. ADR-0106 Decision 6, line 51. Replace "When a job … fails, it uploads two artifacts, kept 14 days: …" with: "When a job of `.github/workflows/verify.yml` fails, it uploads what the browser tests left, kept 14 days: the server log (`byway-e2e-server.log`, from the system temp folder) once `pnpm e2e` has started byway, and Playwright's traces and screenshots (`apps/web/test-results`) when a browser test failed. Each is one artifact per operating system, and an upload that finds no file is skipped. A job that fails before the browser tests (the install, the typecheck or the unit tests) uploads nothing, and a run that passes uploads nothing." Keep the SHA-pin and nothing-secret sentences.
  
  4. README.md:196. Say the same: "A failed job also keeps, for 14 days, what the browser tests left: their server log (… once the suite's server started) and Playwright's traces and screenshots of the browser tests that failed (…). A job that failed earlier, in the typecheck or the unit tests, keeps nothing."
  
  5. Optionally, ADR-0106:88 (Consequences): "A CI run whose browser tests fail keeps byway's server log and Playwright's traces…". The freeze rule covers only Context and Decision, but the sentence has the same overbreadth.
  
  Afterwards, run scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts and scripts/test/ci.test.ts on the branch to confirm both still pass. Commit before merging, so the corrected text is what reaches main.

#### Lens: ui (blocking findings)

What it checked: My own clone of fix/fx-d2 at 0da7bf0, at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-web-ux\repo; pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline exited 0. I ran `pnpm demo` with its own data directory (...\check-web-ux\data) and ports 3917 and 7917. The start-up line said "Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox". I drove the app with Playwright probe specs (apps/web/e2e/zz-probe.spec.ts, in the clone only). I set the start-up check record with a small store script (zz-setcheck.ts, in the clone only). Afterwards I stopped every process I started (taskkill /T on the serve tree); no process or port 3917/7917 was left. Nothing was pushed. The worktree, main's checkout and byway_review were not touched.

What I verified:
- **Settings switch**
  - It is a role=switch named by the h2 and described by the help text.
  - Space opens the confirmation and "Keep it on" takes focus. Enter on "Keep it on" closes it, leaves the sandbox on and puts focus back on the switch.
  - Shift+Tab, then Enter on "Turn it off" turns it off. Focus returns to the switch and the status line says "The OS sandbox is off."
  - Turning it on asks nothing.
  - The "Turn the sandbox back on" offer appears only while off with a 'starts' check. Pressing it turns the sandbox on, removes the offer and puts focus on the switch.
  - Error path (PATCH routed to 503): a role=alert appears, focus stays on "Turn it off", the confirmation stays open and the switch stays on. A retry works.
  - Escape does nothing in the confirmation. It is inline rather than a dialog, which matches the existing cancel-run confirmation, and no README rule requires Escape. Not reported.
- **Origin guard**
  - PATCH /api/renderer is refused with 403 for Origin null, for a foreign origin, and for sec-fetch-site cross-site.
  - The OPTIONS preflight sends no CORS header, so a sandboxed generated page cannot switch the sandbox off.
  - SettingsPatch strips osSandbox.
- **Banner**
  - Present while off on /, /history, /library, /settings, an unknown route (404), /recommend/&lt;id>, /runs/&lt;id>, /runs/&lt;id>/result and /view/&lt;id>.
  - Gone when the sandbox is on. Its link goes to /settings#renderer.
  - The 'cannot-start' banner appears when the setting is on and the check found 'fails'. When the setting is off, the off banner shows instead, with why and fix in Settings.
  - Checked at 375 px: no horizontal scroll, including a long Windows Chromium path in the fix text.
- **Settings findings**, written into the store for: on+fails (Windows), off+fails (Windows and Linux AppArmor), off+timeout, off+unknown. In each case the copy matched rendererFindings. The Linux fix names sh scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh. No message suggests switching the sandbox off.
- **Run note**: a C1 run in demo mode with the sandbox off wrote renderer-sandbox-off events for the render, asset-verify, cut-render, tells-render, compare-render, voice and final-render steps. The note showed on 7 timeline rows and 7 "How this was made" rows. Failed steps hide it: see WEB-2.
- **Tests**
  - The new e2e (renderer-sandbox.spec.ts) passed against my demo. It uses no wall-clock waits.
  - `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web`: 43 files, 432 tests passed.
  - The e2e does not cover the run note or the cannot-start banner; unit tests do.

Not reported because B3 already disclosed it: if the sandbox probe hangs while the setting is off, Settings would say "the page renderer could not start: …had not started 30 s…" although the renderer runs without the sandbox (rendererFindings, apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:53). B3 named this for the start-up line, and it is unlikely.

Copy checked and found true: OFF_RISK matches the owner's words; the help text, the banner text, the run note, and the start-up line text for the off state. The README's "The OS sandbox" section matches the UI behaviour, except for the failed-step case in WEB-2.

- **WEB-1** (blocking, a11y / wrong behaviour), at apps/web/src/components/PageRendererSettings.tsx:70-83 (the confirmation group, its risk paragraph and the autoFocus on "Keep it on"); apps/web/e2e/renderer-sandbox.spec.ts:37-56

  Screen readers never hear the risk wording in the turn-off confirmation. When the switch is pressed, focus jumps to "Keep it on", inside a group named only "Turn the OS sandbox off?". The risk text (OFF_RISK) sits in a plain &lt;p> that neither the group nor either button references. A screen-reader user hears "Turn the OS sandbox off? grouping, Keep it on, button", can Shift+Tab to "Turn it off" and press it without ever hearing what it risks. The owner's design requires the confirmation to 'explain the risk plainly', and ADR-0105 item 6 says the confirmation is correct for keyboard and screen reader.

  Evidence: I ran byway in demo mode in my clone (PORT 3917) and drove it with a Playwright probe. The probe read Chromium's accessibility tree over CDP (DOM.describeNode, then Accessibility.getFullAXTree) for the focused element after pressing Space on the switch:
    KEEP: button name="Keep it on" desc="" | none | group name="Turn the OS sandbox off?" desc=""
    TURN OFF (after Shift+Tab): button name="Turn it off" desc="" | none | group name="Turn the OS sandbox off?" desc=""
    group aria-describedby: null
  The switch itself does get a description (its help paragraph, through describedBy), so the gap is only in the confirmation. The e2e checks the risk only with toContainText on the group's visible text, so it cannot catch this.

  Suggested fix: Give the risk paragraph an id (useId) and put aria-describedby={riskId} on the role="group" div. Also put it on the "Turn it off" ActionButton, or on both buttons, so that landing on the dangerous button reads the risk. In renderer-sandbox.spec.ts, assert `await expect(confirm.getByRole('button', { name: 'Turn it off' })).toHaveAccessibleDescription(RISK_WITHOUT_QUESTION)`, and the same for the group or for "Keep it on", so the e2e fails if the description is lost.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I reproduced WEB-1 in my own clone of fix/fx-d2 at 0da7bf0, and I could not refute it. When focus lands on either button in the turn-off confirmation, Chromium exposes no description on the button or on its group. A screen-reader user who presses Space, then Shift+Tab, then Enter (the same keyboard path renderer-sandbox.spec.ts drives) only hears "Turn the OS sandbox off? grouping, Keep it on" and then "Turn it off". They never hear the risk the owner asked for: "a malicious page or image ... could run as you ... next to your API keys and spending records ... banner on every screen".
  
  I tried four ways to argue it is not blocking. None holds:
  (a) WCAG: the risk &lt;p> sits next to the buttons in reading order, and a browse-mode user can arrow up to it. So there is arguably no strict A/AA failure. But nothing tells that user the paragraph is there, and focus mode skips it.
  (b) ADR-0105 line 47 only promises "a named group whose 'Keep it on' takes focus", and the code matches that literally. But the same ADR quotes the owner's requirement that the confirmation "explains the risk plainly", and it presents the confirmation as correct for keyboard and screen reader. Informed consent to turning off a security control is the confirmation's whole job.
  (c) The switch's own description is announced before Space is pressed. It only says, in positive terms, that the sandbox shuts in an exploit. It does not mention API keys, spending records or the banner.
  (d) RunScreen's "Cancel this run?" group has the same gap and was accepted. But its extra text ("What it made so far is kept.") is harmless. The codebase's own yes/no pattern, ConfirmPanel.tsx:40, already uses role="group" with aria-describedby on the prompt, so the convention exists and this confirmation departs from it.
  
  Conclusion: the defect is real, it defeats the purpose of this confirmation for screen-reader users, and the fix is three attributes. I rate it blocking for D2.

  Evidence: Setup: clone at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-ui-WEB-1\repo (fix/fx-d2, 0da7bf0), pnpm install --frozen-lockfile. I ran Playwright's own demo webServer on PORT 3961 / Temporal 7361; the start-up line read "renderer: Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox". The probe spec is apps/web/e2e/probe-web1.spec.ts in that clone. It reads the focused node's accessibility-tree chain over CDP (DOM.describeNode + Accessibility.getFullAXTree) and runs soft toHaveAccessibleDescription checks.
  
  Unfixed (log run2.log):
    SWITCH: switch name="Page renderer's OS sandbox" desc="byway draws pages ... With the sandbox on, a page or image that takes advantage of a bug in Chromium is still shut in by the sandbox. ..."
    KEEP: button name="Keep it on" desc="" < group name="Turn the OS sandbox off?" desc=""
    TURN OFF (after Shift+Tab): button name="Turn it off" desc="" < group name="Turn the OS sandbox off?" desc=""
    group aria-describedby: null
    toHaveAccessibleDescription(OFF_RISK) FAILED on all three: the group, "Keep it on" and "Turn it off".
  The existing e2e passes regardless, because it checks only `confirm.toContainText(RISK)` (renderer-sandbox.spec.ts:38). That reads visible text, not what is announced.
  
  With the fix applied in my clone only (log run3.log), both buttons and the group expose desc = OFF_RISK, all three probe assertions pass, and the unchanged renderer-sandbox.spec.ts still passes (2 passed, 27.5s). Nothing outside my clone was touched: no worktree or main edits, no paid calls, no push. The demo servers exited with the run.

  Fix: PageRendererSettings.tsx:70-86:
  (1) Wrap only the risk sentence in an element with an id, so the description does not repeat the question already in the group's name: `<p>Turn the sandbox off? <span id={`${ids}-renderer-risk`}>{OFF_RISK}</span></p>`. Reuse the component's existing `ids` from useId.
  (2) Put `aria-describedby={`${ids}-renderer-risk`}` on BOTH buttons: the autoFocus "Keep it on" &lt;button>, and the "Turn it off" ActionButton. ActionButton's NativeProps forwards aria-describedby, and actionAttributes does not override it.
  Prefer the buttons over the group. NVDA also speaks the description of a container that focus enters, so describing both the group and the button would read the risk twice on first focus. If you want the group described for other ATs, accept that duplication knowingly.
  
  renderer-sandbox.spec.ts: after line 39, add `await expect(confirm.getByRole('button', { name: 'Keep it on' })).toHaveAccessibleDescription(OFF_RISK_TEXT)`. After line 51, add the same assertion for 'Turn it off'. OFF_RISK_TEXT is the risk without the "Turn the sandbox off? " prefix; import OFF_RISK from apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts or copy the string. Keep the existing toContainText(RISK).
  
  I applied and verified exactly this in my clone (with the group also described), and it passes. Out of scope for D2 and pre-existing: RunScreen.tsx:220's "Cancel this run?" group has the same pattern ("What it made so far is kept." is not announced). That is a candidate for a separate small follow-up, not a blocker here.

- **WEB-2** (non-blocking, behaviour / doc accuracy), at apps/web/src/components/run/Timeline.tsx:89; apps/web/src/view/run.ts:97; claims in docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:49 and apps/web/README.md:148-149

  A step that draws pages with the sandbox off and then fails loses its sandbox-off note from the Run timeline. Timeline.tsx shows `step.error` OR `step.note`, never both, so the row shows only the error. "How this was made" lists only rows with status 'done', and a failed run has no Result screen. Take a model step whose cards were drawn without the sandbox before the provider call failed: the run's timeline then carries no sandbox note anywhere, and the note is visible only after opening the step (StepDetail.tsx:38 shows both). ADR-0105 item 6 says the Run screen's timeline shows the note, and the web README says such a step 'has a note on the Run and Result screens'. Neither holds for a failed step. The owner's design asks for a note on each run drawn without the sandbox.

  Evidence: A throwaway vitest in my clone (since deleted) used the events step-started 'critic', renderer-sandbox-off {note}, step-failed {error}:
    VIEW MODEL {"status":"failed","error":"The model provider did not answer.","note":"Pages in this step were drawn with the page renderer's OS sandbox off (it was switched off in Settings)."}
    TIMELINE HTML has note: false | has error: true   (renderToStaticMarkup(&lt;Timeline/>))
    HOW IT WAS MADE rows: []
  timeline.test.ts:299 checks only the view model on a 'done' step, so it does not catch this. For comparison, a finished C1 run in demo mode with the sandbox off showed the note on 7 timeline rows and 7 Result rows, so the happy path works.

  Suggested fix: In Timeline.tsx render the note alongside the error, for example `{step.error ? <p className="tl-error">…</p> : null}{step.note ? <p className="tl-note">…</p> : null}`, or at least keep the sandbox note when an error is shown. Add a component test (renderToStaticMarkup) for a failed step that carries a renderer-sandbox-off note. Alternatively, narrow the ADR-0105 and README wording to say a failed step's note is in its step detail.

**Status page, spec and backlog**
