**Guard mutations.** Two mutations show the sandbox guard is weaker than claimed (DOC-1). With the second one, launch.test.ts failed 4 tests (the run log is ../mut-default-launch.log). Both files restored, and the unmutated launch and guard tests pass again.

- **DOC-1** (blocking, test-does-not-catch-what-it-claims), at apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:60 (claim in its header; apps/web/README.md:146-150; docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:31, Decision 1)

  The guard is described as failing if anything else in the product switches the sandbox or gives the renderer a setting of its own (the header says: `osSandbox:` with a function, anywhere but the worker and the start-up check). It does not catch that. It only matches the literal forms `osSandbox: ()`, `osSandbox: async ()` and `osSandbox: storeOsSandbox`, and it never looks at calls of `playwrightPages(`, `launchChromium(` or `renderBrowsers(`. A second production renderer whose setting is always off passes it.

  Evidence: In my clone, I added these lines to packages/runner/src/worker.ts above line 65: `const off = (): boolean => false; const previews = playwrightPages({ osSandbox: off }); void previews;`. Then `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts` gave '4 passed (4)'. A second change, making packages/runner/src/pages.ts:424 read `const launch = opts.launch ?? ((_s: LaunchSetting) => launchChromium({ osSandbox: false }));`, also left the guard at '4 passed (4)'. launch.test.ts did catch that one (4 failures, ../mut-default-launch.log), but nothing catches the first. I restored both files and checked with git status.

  Suggested fix: Pin the whole surface in the guard, as it already does for setRendererSandbox. Every product-source line that calls `playwrightPages(`, `launchChromium(`, `renderBrowsers(` or `chromiumLaunchOptions(`, and every line containing `osSandbox:` or `osSandbox =`, should equal an exact allowlist: pages.ts's launch default and checkRenderer's attempt, and worker.ts's storeOsSandbox line. If that is not wanted, narrow the claims in the guard header, apps/web/README.md:149-150 and ADR-0105 Decision 1 to the literal patterns actually checked.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim is real, and the gap is wider than reported. The guard's fourth test (apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:59-65) only matches the literal text `osSandbox: ()`, `osSandbox: async ()` or `osSandbox: storeOsSandbox`. It never looks at who builds a renderer or who launches Chromium. Three places say otherwise: the guard's own header (line 8: "`osSandbox:` with a function, anywhere but the worker and the start-up check"), apps/web/README.md:149-150 ("fails if anything else in the product switches it or gives the renderer a setting of its own") and ADR-0105 Decision 1 ("holds the sources to that: ... renderers given a setting only by the worker (the store's) and by the start-up check"). That is an overclaim in an Accepted ADR and in the README, about the check that enforces the owner's "Never automatic" rule. Nothing else covers the gap either: tsc passes, no other guard pins callers of playwrightPages, launchChromium or chromium.launch, and launch.test.ts only checks how the existing launch is configured. Shipped product behavior is correct today, since no second renderer exists, so this is about what the guard enforces and what the docs say it enforces. I still count it as blocking: the ADR states a guarantee that is false, and either fix is small. I found nothing that refutes the claim. The only strict-pattern case that works is the literal arrow: `playwrightPages({ osSandbox: () => false })` does make the guard fail (1 failed, 3 passed).

  Evidence: Clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-docs-DOC-1\repo at 0da7bf0 (fix/fx-d2), with deps installed offline. On the unmodified tree, `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts` gave 4 passed (4). I made each mutation in packages/runner/src/worker.ts above line 63, then restored it with git checkout. git status was clean at the end.
  (1) The checker's own case, `const off = (): boolean => false; const previews = playwrightPages({ osSandbox: off }); void previews;`: guard 4 passed (4), and the root `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` exited 0.
  (2) Shorthand with a named function, `const osSandbox = async function () { return false; }; const cardsRenderer = playwrightPages({ osSandbox });`: guard 4 passed (4).
  (3) A direct production launch with the sandbox off, `import { launchChromium, ... }` plus `const warm = (): Promise<unknown> => launchChromium({ osSandbox: false });`: guard 4 passed (4).
  (4) A direct Playwright launch with no sandbox option, `import { chromium } from 'playwright'` plus `const warm = () => chromium.launch({ headless: true });`: guard 4 passed (4) and tsc exited 0. Playwright's chromiumSandbox defaults to false, so this launch runs unsandboxed. The guard only looks for the word `chromiumSandbox`, so it misses this, and nothing pins ADR-0105 item 2's "launchChromium({ osSandbox }), the only production launch".
  (5) Sanity check, `playwrightPages({ osSandbox: () => false })`: guard 1 failed | 3 passed. So only the literal arrow form is caught.
  A grep of every */test/** file found no other test or guard that pins callers of playwrightPages(, launchChromium(, renderBrowsers( or chromium.launch( in product sources.

  Fix: Preferred fix: pin the whole construction and launch surface in apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts, the same way the guard already pins setRendererSandbox. Match identifier mentions rather than calls, so that imports, aliases, namespace access and multi-line calls also change the list. The exact current output of the guard's own uses() helper is below; I reproduced it with a copy of that helper. Add to the fourth test, keeping the existing checkRenderer({ pin:
  
  expect(uses(/\b(playwrightPages|launchChromium|renderBrowsers|chromiumLaunchOptions)\b/)).toEqual([
   'packages/runner/src/index.ts: export { checkRenderer, chromiumLaunchOptions, launchChromium, launchFailure, playwrightPages, RENDER_ALLOWLIST, RENDERER_OS_SANDBOX_DEFAULT, rendererCheckWithin, rendererLine, storeOsSandbox, type ArtifactResolver, type LaunchSetting, type PageRenderer, type PlaywrightPagesOptions, type RenderOptions, type RendererCheck, type RendererCheckOptions, type SurfaceShots } from \'./pages.ts\';',
   "packages/runner/src/pages.ts: import { renderBrowsers } from './render-browsers.ts';",
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: export function chromiumLaunchOptions(setting: LaunchSetting, env: Env = process.env): LaunchOptions {',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: export function playwrightPages(opts: PlaywrightPagesOptions = {}): PageRenderer {',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: const launch = opts.launch ?? launchChromium;',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: const browsers = renderBrowsers((osSandbox) => launch({ osSandbox }), { timeoutMs: timeout, closeTimeoutMs: closeTimeout, ...(opts.osSandbox ? { osSandbox: opts.osSandbox } : {}) });',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: export async function launchChromium(setting: LaunchSetting): Promise&lt;Browser> {',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: return await chromium.launch(chromiumLaunchOptions(setting));',
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: const pages = playwrightPages({ osSandbox: () => osSandbox, host, ...(opts.launch ? { launch: opts.launch } : {}) });',
   'packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts: export function renderBrowsers&lt;B extends KeptBrowser>(launch: (osSandbox: boolean) => Promise&lt;B>, { timeoutMs, closeTimeoutMs, osSandbox }: RenderBrowsersOptions): RenderBrowsers&lt;B> {',
   "packages/runner/src/worker.ts: import { playwrightPages, storeOsSandbox, type PageRenderer } from './pages.ts';",
   'packages/runner/src/worker.ts: const pages = opts.pages ?? playwrightPages({ osSandbox: storeOsSandbox(opts.store) });',
  ]);
  // Playwright itself is loaded, and Chromium launched, only in pages.ts (Playwright's own default is no sandbox).
  expect(uses(/\bchromium\.launch\b|['"]playwright['"]/)).toEqual([
   "packages/runner/src/pages.ts: import type { Browser, BrowserContext, LaunchOptions, Page, Route } from 'playwright';",
   "packages/runner/src/pages.ts: let chromium: typeof import('playwright').chromium | undefined;",
   "packages/runner/src/pages.ts: ({ chromium } = await import('playwright'));",
   'packages/runner/src/pages.ts: return await chromium.launch(chromiumLaunchOptions(setting));',
   "packages/runner/src/pages.ts: executable = executableOf((await import('playwright')).chromium);",
  ]);
  
  The existing `osSandbox: (...)` assertion can stay as a second check, or be dropped because the allowlist above covers it. Then make the header (line 8) say what is now true: a renderer is built (playwrightPages/renderBrowsers) and Chromium is launched (launchChromium/chromium.launch, or Playwright loaded) only at these listed lines. README:149-150 and ADR-0105 Decision 1 can then stand as written. Optionally add "and Chromium launched only by launchChromium" to Decision 1, since that is now pinned too. Rerun the mutations above: each one should then fail the guard.
  
  Proportionate alternative, if the stronger guard is not wanted: narrow the three claims to what is actually matched, and say plainly that a renderer built with a named or shorthand setting function, or a new launchChromium or chromium.launch caller, is not caught. This means the guard header line 8, apps/web/README.md:149-150 and ADR-0105 Decision 1, where "renderers given a setting only by the worker ... and by the start-up check" becomes "`osSandbox:` given as an inline function only in the worker and the start-up check". Since the ADR is Accepted, record the correction with honest provenance, as the project does for ADR edits.

- **DOC-2** (non-blocking, backlog-row-closed-unsettled), at docs/backlog.md:166 ("What D2 left (ADR-0105)"), compared with the removed D2 row (7eabcfe docs/backlog.md, "Owner decisions from the external-review verification")

  The closed D2 row set a gate: "Test the AppArmor steps the README is to give on a real Ubuntu 24.04 or later machine (not WSL…) before the sandbox is switched on for Linux: only CI's runner has run such a profile". The sandbox is now on for Linux, the steps have run only on CI's runner, and the replacement row moves the gate to "before Linux is announced". No record says who relaxed the gate or why.

  Evidence: git diff 7eabcfe..HEAD -- docs/backlog.md: the old row's gate is removed, and new row item (1) reads "has run on CI's fresh Ubuntu 24.04 machine only, not on a person's own Ubuntu install … try it on the first real Linux machine byway runs on, and before Linux is announced". ADR-0105's Consequences repeat the limit but not the waived gate.

  Suggested fix: Add one sentence to ADR-0105 (Decision 8 or Consequences) saying the gate in the D2 row was not met, and why. For example: the owner's "Windows and Linux now" plus CI's ubuntu-24.04 VM with kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1 (run 39137471023) was taken as enough, with who decided that. Or put the gate back to the owner.

- **DOC-3** (non-blocking, adr-provenance-and-alternatives), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:6 (Decided by) and :72 (Alternatives)

  'Decided by: User' also covers choices the owner did not make. B3 itself lists two as 'for the owner to review': one setting per data directory instead of the per-workspace Preferences the brief named, and an automatic start-up launch of Chromium without the sandbox, on byway's test page, when the sandbox fails for no stated reason. The Alternatives entry 'Probe without the sandbox only when Chromium gave no reason. Kept' describes the decision, not an alternative. The real alternative, never launching Chromium unsandboxed unless the person switched it off (closest to the owner's 'Never automatic'), is not weighed.

  Evidence: pages.ts checkRenderer: `const without = await attempt(false);` runs at every start while the sandbox is on and failing without a marker. The owner's words quoted in ADR-0105's Context are "5. Never automatic: byway never turns the sandbox off by itself." The memory rule on ADR provenance is "Never mark Claude's recommendations as user decisions."

  Suggested fix: In Decided-by or the Decision, name the items that were delegated choices (the per-data-directory scope, reading before each load, the unsandboxed probe). Replace the 'Kept' entry with the real alternative ('No unsandboxed probe: say only that the cause is unknown') and the reason it was rejected.

- **DOC-4** (non-blocking, backlog-accuracy), at docs/backlog.md:160 ("Bring the decision evidence kept only in %TEMP% into the repository")

  The row lists every Accepted ADR whose evidence lives only in %TEMP% (0081, 0092, 0093, 0097, 0098, 0102, 0103, and now 0106) but leaves out ADR-0105. ADR-0105 cites owner-decisions.json, scratch/decisions/D2-renderer-sandbox, D2-skeptic/probe-ready.mts and the red runs in scratch/d2/b3.

  Evidence: `grep -l -E 'AppData[\\/]+Local[\\/]+Temp' docs/adr/*.md` gives 0081 0092 0093 0097 0098 0102 0103 0105 0106. The row names all of these except 0105.

  Suggested fix: Add a sentence such as: "ADR-0105 cites the D2 brief and skeptic there (owner-decisions.json, scratch/decisions/D2-*), and its red runs (scratch/d2/b3)."

- **DOC-5** (non-blocking, unrecorded-known-limit), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2320 (checkRenderer probe) and docs/backlog.md:166 item (4)

  B3 reported a known limit that no document records. With the sandbox off, the check draws the test page without the sandbox and then probes with it. If the probe hangs rather than failing fast, rendererCheckWithin's 30 s ends first. Both the start-up line and the record Settings reads then say the renderer is 'not available' / 'could not start', although it has just drawn the test page. Backlog item (4) mentions only the second launch.

  Evidence: checkRenderer awaits `attempt(true)` inside the promise that serve.ts races against RENDERER_CHECK_MS. On timeout, rendererCheckWithin resolves `{ osSandbox, ready: false, sandbox: 'unknown', message: "Playwright's Chromium had not started 30 s after …" }`. serve.ts records that, and rendererFindings shows "At byway's last start-up, the page renderer could not start: …". In contributor mode the 30 s start from engine-up with no build first, so a test-page hang of up to timeoutMs (30 s) is enough to trigger it.

  Suggested fix: Record it in item (4) of 'What D2 left'. Better: give the probe its own bound in checkRenderer, so that once the unsandboxed page has been drawn, a probe that does not answer returns `{ osSandbox: false, ready: true, sandbox: 'unknown' }`.

- **DOC-6** (non-blocking, adr-statement-incomplete), at docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md, Decision 5

  Decision 5 reads as a complete list: every browser close byway makes gives a reason, either '… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close' or '… as it shut down'. ADR-0105 (render-browsers.ts:69) adds a third close, 'byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed', on the same retire path. ADR-0106 was already edited on this branch for ADR-0105 (item 3), but item 5 was not.

  Evidence: render-browsers.ts:68-70 defines retiredReason, changedReason and shutdownReason. ADR-0106 Decision 5 lists only the first and third.

  Suggested fix: While ADR-0106 is still a draft on the branch, add "or '… after its OS sandbox setting changed' (ADR-0105)" to item 5's list.

#### Lens: integration (blocking findings)

What it checked: I cloned fix/fx-d2 at 0da7bf0 (merge base with main: 7eabcfe) into C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-whole\repo and ran pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline (exit 0).

**Full gate in my clone.** All four commands passed.
- `pnpm -s typecheck`: exit 0.
- `pnpm exec vitest run`: 198 files, 4,940 tests passed (140 s).
- `pnpm --filter ./apps/web run build`: exit 0.
- `pnpm e2e`: 54 passed (4.7 min). It printed "e2e: renderer Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox", the "checked … complete" line, and no stand-in key under apps/web/.next-e2e.
- `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test apps/web/test/guard-` (CI, ADR log, README, guards): 12 files, 138 tests passed.

**Branch CI (read-only `gh`).** Run 39137471023 ran at e36a0aa and succeeded on both jobs. The only later commit, 0da7bf0, adds one line of evidence to ADR-0105.
- Linux (ubuntu-24.04): the README's script printed `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1` and added the profile. 198 files and 4,940 tests passed, 54 e2e passed in 5.6 min, and the line said "inside its OS sandbox".
- Windows (windows-2025): 54 e2e passed in 17.9 min, with the same line.
- The new upload-on-failure steps have never run, because no job has failed. The workflow uses `defaults.run.shell: bash`, so `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` also resolves on Windows.

**Workflow bundle.** build.test.ts still pins 38afef53… and passes, so the bundle is unchanged and no waiting run ends (ADR-0078). ADR-0105 says so.

**Store migration.** Migration 8 only creates `renderer_settings`, so it is additive. A probe built a version-7 database (migrations 1–7 with their checksums plus a workspace row) and opened it with this branch's store. The workspace was kept, getRendererSettings() returned {osSandbox:true}, and recordRendererCheck left the sandbox on. The probe file was removed.

**serve.ts end to end in demo mode, sandbox off.** I set it off in a scratch data directory with Store.setRendererSandbox(false) and started on ports 3941 and 7941. Ports 3917 and 7917 were already held by another process, which I did not touch.
- The start-up line said "Playwright's Chromium, ready, with its OS sandbox off (switched off in Settings). The sandbox can start on this computer now: Settings offers to turn it back on."
- `/` and `/history` carried the role=note "off" banner linking to /settings#renderer.
- `/settings` showed "the OS sandbox could start on this computer" and the "Turn the sandbox back on" button.
- I then killed the process tree (node serve.ts, Temporal, next) and confirmed no process from my clone remains.

**Combination review of render-browsers.ts and pages.ts.** I checked these by reading the code; I found no defect in them.
- When the setting changes, the browser launched under the other setting is retired, the loads already on it finish there, and an idle one closes at once.
- Microtask ordering counts the launching load before a concurrent mismatched load can retire the browser.
- ready() uses find(), then a counted test-page load. A late close of the test page retires the browser, and the next ready() draws the test page on the new one.
- A setting that cannot be read counts as on.
- The backstop cannot pre-empt a load retired by a setting change.
- Every page-drawing activity input (renderView, the critic, openCheckpoint, finishHumanStep) has run and call, so the sandbox-off note is written. The readiness test page never counts.
- PATCH /api/renderer is a strict {osSandbox} body. A cross-origin PATCH needs a preflight, so a CSRF write is not possible.

**ADR-0103.** Index rows and "changed by" links are present: ADR-0084, 0103 and 0081 for ADR-0105, and 0103 for ADR-0106. The counts match the table: 106 rows, 99 accepted, 15 by continuation, 38 delegated, 7 proposed. ADR-0084 and ADR-0104 only gained "Later notes" sections, and adr-records.test passes.

**Fact checks.**
- Ubuntu 23.10 release notes: the restriction "is not currently enabled by default". So B3's correction of ADR-0084's "default on Ubuntu 23.10 and later" is right.
- I probed ADR-0104's Later-notes facts in node and they are right: shad is not Sentence_Terminal, U+037E normalises to U+003B, « “ ‘ are Pi, and ℹ is a letter.
- next.config.ts has no images config, which supports the sharp licence note.

**Wall-clock.** The new tests use fake timers, held promises or source reads. pages.test.ts passes `deadline: Date.now() + 1_500` only to make a hung load end through its deadline, and it asserts the message, not elapsed time.

Not done: no macOS check, and no run of the AppArmor script on a real (non-CI) Ubuntu.

- **W1** (blocking, false-statement / combination), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2333-2336 (rendererCheckWithin message), shown via rendererLine (pages.ts:2349), apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:53 (Settings: "the page renderer could not start: …"), quoted in docs/adr/0084-renderer-launch-without-secrets.md:79

  B1 worded the time-limit message for B3's predecessor check, which only launched Chromium: "Playwright's Chromium had not started 30 s after …". B3's checkRenderer now does more after the launch. It draws byway's test page (with its own 30 s limit, pages.ts:421/583). It may also make a second full attempt with the other setting: always when the setting is off, and when it is on and the failure carries no marker. So the limit can now fire after Chromium has started, while the test page is drawing or the second probe runs. The start-up line then says "not available: Playwright's Chromium had not started …", the store records ready:false, and Settings says "the page renderer could not start". All three are false. With the sandbox off and the probe slow, the line says "not available, with its OS sandbox off" although the renderer is ready. The inner test-page limit (30 s) equals the outer limit (RENDERER_CHECK_MS, scripts/serve.ts:62). So when a sandboxed test page hangs (the Windows security-software case the design is meant to explain), the first attempt alone uses up the limit. In contributor mode, whose limit counts only from the engine being up, the person can get this false line instead of the Windows why and fix.

  Evidence: Probe (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\check-whole\zz-probe-check-timeout.test.ts, run with vitest in packages/runner/test). It uses a real launchChromium({osSandbox:true}) whose newContext waits briefly, then calls rendererCheckWithin(checkRenderer(...), 500, {built:true, osSandbox:true}). Output: LAUNCHED+CONNECTED: [true]; RECORD: {"osSandbox":true,"ready":false,"sandbox":"unknown","message":"Playwright's Chromium had not started 0.5 s after the app was built and the engine was up."}; LINE: renderer   not available: Playwright's Chromium had not started 0.5 s after …; the check itself then resolved {"osSandbox":true,"ready":true,"sandbox":"starts"}. B3's own report names the off-mode case ("the line says the renderer is 'not available' even though it is ready"), but backlog row "What D2 left (ADR-0105)" item (4) leaves out that consequence.

  Suggested fix: Say only what is known when the limit fires. For example: "byway's check of the page renderer had not finished 30 s after …, so byway cannot say yet whether it can draw pages." Better, have checkRenderer report its progress so rendererCheckWithin can return what is already known. When the first attempt has succeeded and only the probe is outstanding, return ready:true with sandbox:'unknown', so the line says ready (sandbox off) and "could not tell whether the sandbox can start". Give the test page inside checkRenderer a limit well under RENDERER_CHECK_MS, or the outer wait room for two attempts. Update the renderer-check.test.ts expectations and add a dated later-note bullet to ADR-0084 for the new wording.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I could not refute it. One part of the claim overstates the case: "the first attempt alone uses up the limit" is wrong. The outer 30 s starts when the engine is up (E, measured at 3.2 to 6.6 s here). The inner 30 s test-page limit starts right after Chromium launches (about 0.2 s). So the outer limit only wins when the work around the hang (closing the first browser, a second launch and draw, or in off mode the first attempt plus the probe's launch) takes longer than E minus L1. In start mode, next build gives the check a long head start, so the default `pnpm start` path is mostly safe. The issue is reachable in contributor mode, or in start mode on a machine where the launch waits for the build.
  
  On this machine, with the sandboxed renderer hanging and launches at normal speed, the race had only 1.3 to 3 s to spare over 5 runs. Slowing each launch by 2 s, which security software scanning a new chrome.exe can plausibly cause, made both modes print the false line in the real serve.ts order, with the real engine and the real 30 s limit.
  
  The harm lands in the exact case ADR-0105 is built for. ADR-0105 line 42 says "Either way the check says why and the fix", and here it says neither. With the sandbox on, the record is `sandbox:'unknown'`, so rendererBanner shows no "cannot-start" banner. Settings says "could not start: Playwright's Chromium had not started …", and runs fail with readyFailure's plain "did not draw byway's test page within 30 s" (a StepError passes through unchanged), which has no why and no fix either. On that machine the timing repeats, so it happens on every start-up.
  
  With the sandbox off, the line says "not available" although the check itself found the renderer ready.
  
  This branch caused it: B1's wording was true for the launch-only check of c31f107, and B3's checkRenderer made it false. The fix is small and local.

  Evidence: Where I ran it: my own clone at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W1\repo (pnpm install). The probe script is packages/runner/scratch-timing.mts. It follows serve.ts's contributor-mode order: startDevEngine on port 7955 with a fresh data dir in demo mode (no keys loaded, no paid calls), checkRenderer starting meanwhile, and once the engine is up, rendererCheckWithin(checking, 30_000, {built:false}). Only the launch is a stand-in. When `osSandbox` is true it adds Chromium's `--wait-for-debugger-children=renderer`, which holds each renderer about 60 s and plays the hanging sandboxed renderer. It can also wait a given time before each launch.
  
  Results:
  - Sandbox off, hang, normal launch speed (4 runs): the line was correct, but the margin was thin. Cold run: probe launched at 3.9 s, engine up at 5.3 s, check finished at 34.0 s against an outer limit at 35.3 s. Warm runs: margin about 3 s.
  - Sandbox on, hang (1 run): the line was correct. E was 3.2 s, the check finished at 30.7 s with sandbox:'fails' and the Windows why and fix.
  - Sandbox on, hang, launches 2 s slower: launched 2.2 s, engine up 3.5 s, LINE at 33.5 s: `renderer   not available: Playwright's Chromium had not started 30 s after the engine was up.` The record was {"osSandbox":true,"ready":false,"sandbox":"unknown",…}. The check's own answer at 34.5 s was sandbox:'fails' with the Windows why and fix.
  - Sandbox off, hang, launches 2 s slower: first attempt drew at 2.4 to 2.6 s, engine up 3.9 s, probe launched 4.7 s, LINE at 33.9 s: `not available, with its OS sandbox off (switched off in Settings): Playwright's Chromium had not started 30 s after the engine was up.` The record was ready:false. The check's own answer at 34.8 s was {"osSandbox":false,"ready":true,"sandbox":"fails",…}.
  
  Code and docs:
  - c31f107 checkRenderer only called ready(), which was browsers.find(), so it only launched.
  - Now ready() draws READY_PAGE under `timeout` (30 s, pages.ts:421, late() at 583). checkRenderer (pages.ts:2308-2321) makes a second full attempt: always when the setting is off, and when it is on and the error carries no marker.
  - serve.ts:191 starts the check before the build and the engine; serve.ts:211 starts the 30 s only after both.
  - apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:26 shows the banner only when `sandbox === 'fails'`, and :53 shows the false text in Settings.
  - The old wording is also asserted in renderer-check.test.ts:35-36 and quoted in ADR-0084:79.
  - The worktree, main and byway_review were not touched.

  Fix: 1) Have the limit report what is already known instead of a fixed claim. Give checkRenderer a progress sink, e.g. `opts.progress?: (known: RendererCheck) => void`, or a shared `{ latest?: RendererCheck }`. rendererCheckWithin resolves to `latest` when the limit fires:
     (a) Setting off and the first attempt drew: report {osSandbox:false, ready:true, sandbox:'unknown'}. rendererLine prints "ready, with its OS sandbox off …". Optionally add "byway could not tell yet whether the sandbox can start".
     (b) Setting on, the first attempt failed without a marker, and the no-sandbox attempt is still running: report {osSandbox:true, ready:false, sandbox:'unknown', message: possibleSandboxCause(…) or plain(true, first.error)}. That keeps the why and fix from sandboxAdvice instead of losing them.
     (c) Nothing known yet: report a message that claims nothing about Chromium, e.g. "byway's check of the page renderer had not finished 30 s after the engine was up, so byway cannot say yet whether it can draw pages."
  2) Size the budget so the normal hang case finishes inside the limit: in checkRenderer's attempt(), pass `timeoutMs` to playwrightPages for the test page, about 12 s (RendererCheckOptions.testPageMs). Two attempts plus launches and closes then fit inside RENDERER_CHECK_MS = 30 s. Run-time ready() keeps its 30 s. Alternatively, give the outer wait room for two attempts. This needs a matching line in late()'s "within N s" wording, since it reads `timeout`.
  3) Tests:
     - Update renderer-check.test.ts:31-37 to the new wording for (c).
     - Add real-Chromium tests through the `launch` seam, with the sandboxed browser's newContext held, a short outer limit, and expectations: setting off gives ready:true/sandbox:'unknown', and a newContext hang with the setting on gives a message that names the test page and keeps the sandbox why and fix.
     - Keep scripts/test/serve.test.ts's order test.
  4) Docs:
     - Append a dated later-note bullet to ADR-0084 for the new wording. Do not edit the 0c40ec8 bullet (ADR-0103 rule 1).
     - In ADR-0105, note that the limit reports what the check found so far, which keeps "Either way the check says why and the fix" true.
     - If the budget split is not done, add the consequence to backlog row "What D2 left (ADR-0105)" item (4).
  5) Separate small point, may be out of scope: during a run, readyFailure passes the test-page late() StepError through unchanged. So a sandbox hang during a run gives "did not draw byway's test page within 30 s" with no "one possible cause is the sandbox" hint. Consider routing that case through possibleSandboxCause when the browser was sandboxed.

- **W2** (blocking, test-does-not-catch-what-it-claims), at apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:59-66 (and the claims at apps/web/README.md:149 and ADR-0105 Decision 1, docs/adr/0105-…:31)

  The apps/web README says guard-sandbox.test.ts "fails if anything else in the product switches it or gives the renderer a setting of its own". ADR-0105 Decision 1 says it holds the sources to "renderers given a setting only by the worker (the store's) and by the start-up check". The guard only matches `osSandbox:` followed by `()`, `async ()` or `storeOsSandbox`, plus `chromiumSandbox` and `setRendererSandbox(`. Product code that draws pages without the sandbox and without the person passes it in three ways: the test-only `launch` seam, a named function, or chromiumLaunchOptions passed straight to Playwright.

  Evidence: Mutation: a new product file scripts/zz-mutation.ts holding `playwrightPages({ launch: () => launchChromium({ osSandbox: false }) })` gave `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts` 4 passed. A second mutation holding `const off = () => false; playwrightPages({ osSandbox: off })` and `chromium.launch(chromiumLaunchOptions({ osSandbox: false }))` also gave 4 passed. Both files were removed afterwards; git status was clean.

  Suggested fix: Extend the guard to list, as exact expected sets, every product use of `launchChromium(` (only pages.ts's default `opts.launch ?? launchChromium`), `chromiumLaunchOptions(` (only inside launchChromium), any `launch:` option (only checkRenderer's pass-through) and any `osSandbox:` property in product code (not only function forms). Alternatively, remove the test seams from the production options type (a test-only factory), so the guarantee is structural. Otherwise narrow the README and ADR-0105 wording to what the guard checks.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim holds. In my own clone of fix/fx-d2 (0da7bf0), apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts passes 4/4 when product code gives the renderer a setting of its own, or launches Chromium without the sandbox, in any form other than `osSandbox: ()`, `osSandbox: async ()` or `storeOsSandbox`. The README (apps/web/README.md:148-149) says the guard "fails if anything else in the product switches it or gives the renderer a setting of its own". ADR-0105 Decision 1 (line 31) says it "holds the sources to" renderers getting a setting only from the worker and the start-up check. Both claims are false for ordinary, non-adversarial code: `launchChromium({ osSandbox: false })` in a new script, or a plain `chromium.launch()`, which Playwright runs with --no-sandbox by default. The owner's item 5 ("Never automatic") has no other check for code outside the renderer.
  
  Two things limit how serious this is. First, today's product code is correct: launchChromium is reached only through playwrightPages' default, chromium.launch is called only inside launchChromium, and playwrightPages and checkRenderer have only the pinned callers. Second, the most likely regression, an automatic fallback inside the renderer, is caught by behavioural tests rather than by this guard: 8 runner tests fail.
  
  So the gap is a guarantee written in an ADR and a README that the test does not give, for new callers anywhere else. It is blocking because the ADR and README state it as enforced. The fix is cheap: I wrote and checked a stronger guard that passes on the clean tree and fails on every mutation below. Narrowing the wording would also be acceptable.

  Evidence: Clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W2\repo, installed with `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline`. On the clean tree the guard passes 4/4.
  
  Mutations: I added one file at a time as scripts/zz.ts, ran `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts`, then removed the file. The current guard result is shown for each.
  - M1 `playwrightPages({ launch: () => launchChromium({ osSandbox: false }) })`: 4 passed
  - M2 `const off = () => false; playwrightPages({ osSandbox: off })`: 4 passed
  - M3 `chromium.launch(chromiumLaunchOptions({ osSandbox: false }))`: 4 passed
  - M4 `launchChromium({ osSandbox: false })`: 4 passed
  - M5 `import { chromium } from 'playwright'; chromium.launch({ headless: true })` (Playwright's default is no sandbox): 4 passed
  - M6 `osSandbox: function () { return false; }`: 4 passed
  - M8 `osSandbox:() => false` (no space after the colon): 4 passed
  - M9 `osSandbox: (_?: unknown) => false`: 4 passed
  - M10 `const s = { osSandbox: false }; checkRenderer(s)`: 4 passed
  - M13 `const make = r.playwrightPages; const off = () => false; make({ osSandbox: off })`: 4 passed
  - Control M7 `playwrightPages({ osSandbox: () => false })`: 1 failed. The guard does catch the literal arrow form.
  
  Fallback inside the renderer: I changed pages.ts:429 to `launch({ osSandbox }).catch(() => launch({ osSandbox: false }))`. The guard still passed 4/4. launch/pages/render-browsers/renderer-check/sandbox tests gave 8 failed / 100 passed (launch.test, pages.test ENG-14, renderer-check), so behavioural tests cover that path. I reverted it.
  
  Stronger guard (prototype in my clone): C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W2\repo\apps\web\test\guard-sandbox-proto.test.ts. It passes 2/2 on the clean tree and fails on M1-M6, M8, M10, M12 and M13 (I did not re-run M9 against it).
  
  No importer outside packages/runner uses launchChromium or chromiumLaunchOptions from index.ts; the runner tests import ../src/pages.ts directly.
  
  The worktree C:\Users\arvik\code\byway\.worktrees\fx-d2 is untouched (git status clean). I did not push and made no paid calls.

  Fix: Strengthen the guard. Do not try to list the forms an `osSandbox:` value can take; that cannot be closed. Instead, pin every product mention of each way to a launch as an exact set, whatever the argument looks like. This is checked in the prototype at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W2\repo\apps\web\test\guard-sandbox-proto.test.ts.
  
  (a) Count an import or re-export list once per file. Map lines matching `^\s*(import|export)\s+(type\s+)?(\{|\*)` to `${file}: (import or re-export)`, so editing serve.ts's import list causes no churn but a new importer anywhere fails.
  
  (b) Pin `uses(/\bplaywrightPages\b/)` to exactly:
  - the index.ts re-export
  - the definition at pages.ts:413
  - checkRenderer's call at pages.ts:2286
  - worker.ts's import
  - worker.ts:64
  
  (c) Pin `uses(/\bcheckRenderer\b/)` to:
  - the index.ts re-export
  - the definition at pages.ts:2282
  - serve.ts's import

Also state the remaining gap: a change to the launch wiring inside pages.ts itself (for example a no-sandbox retry) is caught by launch.test.ts, pages.test.ts and renderer-check.test.ts, not by the guard.

- **W3** (blocking, false-statement (ADR, still editable on the branch)), at docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md:46 (Decision 5) vs packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:69

  ADR-0106 Decision 5 lists byway's close reasons as either "… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close" (a retired browser, at its last load or its backstop) or "… as it shut down" (pages.close()). B3 added a third reason to render-browsers.ts. A browser retired because the OS sandbox setting changed is closed, at its last load or its backstop, with "byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed". B3 extended ADR-0106 item 3 on this branch for ADR-0105 but did not update item 5. Under ADR-0103, the list freezes as Decision text once ADR-0106 reaches main, and from then on it is untrue.

  Evidence: render-browsers.ts:69 `const changedReason = `${BYWAY_CLOSED} after its OS sandbox setting changed`;`, used by retireWith in get() for a setting mismatch. ADR-0105 Decision 2 documents that reason; ADR-0106 Decision 5 does not.

  Suggested fix: Before merging, add the third reason to ADR-0106 Decision 5 on the branch (ADR-0106 is not on main yet, so ADR-0103 allows this). For example: "… or '… after its OS sandbox setting changed' (a browser retired by a change of the setting, ADR-0105)". Mention the edit in ADR-0105's "What this changes in earlier records" bullet on ADR-0106, beside the item 3 extension.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim is real. ADR-0106 Decision 5 lists two reasons for byway's browser closes, and it says a retired browser is closed "at its last load or its backstop" with "… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close". Since 72a9a53 (B3), a browser retired because the OS sandbox setting changed is closed with a third reason, "byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed", both at its last load and at its backstop. So the list is incomplete and the parenthetical is false for that case.
  
  Item 5 was true when ADR-0106 was written. In c31f107 and its code 957da70 there were only two reasons; B3 added the third later. The ADR-0105 commit (f6f978e) edited only item 3 of ADR-0106. ADR-0105's "What this changes in earlier records" also says ADR-0106 "is used as it stands" apart from item 3, so that bullet is incomplete as well.
  
  Attempts to refute it, and why they fail:
  (a) ADR-0105 Decision 2 does name the third reason, and both records merge together. But nothing points a reader of ADR-0106 item 5 to it. ADR-0106's Related row and its index row have no ADR-0105 link, and ADR-0105 says ADR-0106 is "used as it stands".
  (b) The list could be read as examples. It reads as exhaustive: "X or Y", with a parenthetical saying which browsers get X.
  (c) A note could be appended after merge (ADR-0103 rule 1 allows notes). True, but the Decision text itself would stay wrong for good. That is the "false on arrival" case (ADR-0100 item 7) that ADR-0103 was written to stop, and the user's standing rule asks for honest records.
  
  It is blocking in the narrow sense of a merge gate. It is doc-only and has no runtime effect. But fixing it in place is possible only before merge, because ADR-0106 is not on origin/main yet (confirmed with git cat-file), and the fix is a small edit. The decision-log check will not catch it, because it checks edits and links, not whether the text is accurate.

  Evidence: Fresh clone at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W3\repo (fix/fx-d2 @ 0da7bf0).
  
  - docs/adr/0106-a-late-close-retires-the-renderers-browser.md:46 lists only "… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close" (a retired browser, at its last load or its backstop) and "… as it shut down".
  - packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:69 defines `const changedReason = `${BYWAY_CLOSED} after its OS sandbox setting changed`;`. get() calls retireWith(b, changedReason) at :127. retireWith stores k.reason, and both the backstop (:108) and release() (:161, `k.reason ?? retiredReason`) close with it.
  - `git show 957da70:packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts` has only retiredReason and shutdownReason; 72a9a53 added changedReason. `git log` on ADR-0106 shows c31f107 (created) and f6f978e (item 3 only).
  - Runtime probe C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2\refute-integration-W3\probe\probe.mts (node 24, strip-types, stand-in browsers). It printed "A at last load -> byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed" and "B with no loads -> byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed". Playwright's close() received that reason for both b1(os=true) and b2(os=false); only pages.close() gave "as it shut down".
  - packages/runner/test/render-browsers.test.ts:214/257 already pins CHANGED. pages.ts:645 turns closedWith() into the step failure, so a load stopped at a setting-retired browser's backstop would read "The page could not be drawn: byway closed the page renderer after its OS sandbox setting changed."
  - ADR-0105:59 says ADR-0106 "is used as it stands" except item 3.
  - ADR-0106 is absent from origin/main, so it is still editable under ADR-0103 rule 1. scripts/test/adr-log.ts:352-361 flags only a "changed by" link that reaches main after its record, so an in-place edit on the branch, like the one item 3 got, passes.

  Fix: Before merging, on the branch:
  
  1. In ADR-0106 Decision 5 (line 46), list three reasons and narrow the first parenthetical. For example: Every browser close byway makes gives Playwright a reason that starts "byway closed the page renderer": "… after a page in it took more than 5 s to close" (a browser retired for a late close or a context that would not open, at its last load or its backstop), "… after its OS sandbox setting changed" (a browser retired because the person changed the setting, likewise; [ADR-0105](0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md)) or "… as it shut down" (`pages.close()`).
  
  2. In ADR-0105, "What this changes in earlier records" (line 59), replace "is used as it stands. Its item 3 … was extended" with: "is used as it stands, except that two items were extended on this branch before it reached main: item 3 (the test page `ready()` draws is a load like any other) and item 5 (a browser retired by a change of the setting is closed with its own reason, item 2 above)."
  
  3. Optional: add ADR-0105 to ADR-0106's Related row. The header table can change at any time.
  
  No "changed by" index link is needed, because both records reach main in the same --no-ff merge. Merge with --no-ff so the revised draft counts as one first-parent arrival. Then run `pnpm vitest run scripts/test apps/web/test/guard-`. Leave ADR-0106 item 1 as it is: it is phrased as a condition, not as the only way a browser is retired, so it stays true.

## Round 2 (2026-10-18)

*From `d2-r2-result.json` (the session's scratchpad under Temp). Two builders (ba the fixes of round 1's findings; bb five items, among them the security review's Linux folder that only root can change), five lenses.*

### What the builders reported

#### ba (head commit 6c77426)

All six round-1 findings assigned to builder A are fixed on fix/fx-d2. There are four commits on top of 0da7bf0: 60dc2b7 (renderer: S1, S2, W1), b34accc (Settings and Run screen: W1 wording, WEB-1, WEB-2), 2504e85 (the guard: DOC-1/W2) and 6c77426 (docs).

- **S1:** the Windows fix now names the headless shell byway actually launches, chrome-headless-shell.exe. byway finds it from chromium.executablePath() and checks the file is there, which also follows PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH. When the file is missing it names Playwright's browsers folder.
- **S2:** a launch with the sandbox on that dies without saying why (and is not a missing Chromium) now names the sandbox as one possible cause, with the Windows reason and fix.
- **S2, the probe's finding:** once the start-up check has found the sandbox cannot start, a refused run gives the start-up message word for word. I reproduced this on this machine with a real Chromium inside a job object that denies the sandbox its desktop.
- **W1/DOC-5:**
  - The probe now has its own 15 s limit.
  - The start-up line's 30 s limit reports what the check had already found. When nothing is known yet it says "not checked in time", and the stored record then has no `ready` field.
  - Settings now says the renderer "was not ready" or "was not checked in time", never "could not start" for a check that had not finished.
- **WEB-1:** both answers in the turn-off confirmation now carry the risk as their screen-reader description. The e2e spec checks this in a real browser.
- **WEB-2:** a failed step now shows its sandbox-off note under its error in the Run timeline.
- **DOC-1/W2:** the guard now pins every product mention of the ways to switch the setting, build a renderer or launch Chromium, as exact lists. It also checks itself against 14 known ways round it. The three bypasses the W2 refuter found, plus Playwright's own launch, now fail it.

Items:

- **1. S1: the Windows fix names the binary byway launches** (done): sandbox.ts headlessShell() takes the full Chromium path from chromium.executablePath() and finds &lt;folder>/chromium_headless_shell-&lt;rev>/chrome-headless-shell-&lt;platform>/chrome-headless-shell[.exe] beside it. It names that file only if it exists, otherwise Playwright's browsers folder ({ folder }), and nothing for an unrecognised layout. This follows PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH because Playwright builds the path from it. sandboxAdvice takes executable or folder. Tests: sandbox.test.ts covers the four layouts, the folder fallback, and a child process with PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH moved to a temp folder. The drift test in pages.test.ts compares the named file with argv[0] of a real launch made with chromiumLaunchOptions(STATED) plus --enable-automation. launch.test.ts now mocks executablePath to return the full-Chromium path, as Playwright does, and expects the derived shell or the folder. The job-object probe named chromium_headless_shell-1243\chrome-headless-shell-win64\chrome-headless-shell.exe.

A new LaunchFailed subclass keeps the launch's setting. The worker gives the renderer storeLastCheck(store). When the last start-up check found sandbox 'fails', any sandboxed readiness failure (other than a missing Chromium or byway's own close) gives sandboxFailureMessage, the start-up line's message. When both attempts fail, checkRenderer now reports the message of the attempt without the sandbox. The probe was kept, as the owner chose. Agreement tests in renderer-check.test.ts:

- **3. W1 + DOC-5: the start-up limit reports what is known; the probe has its own bound** (done): checkRenderer takes progress, a shared { known }, set before either probe. It also takes probeMs, default PROBE_MS = 15 s, under the test page's own 30 s. A late probe is closed at once, and the check answers:
  - sandbox off: ready, sandbox unknown;

With nothing known it returns { osSandbox, sandbox: 'unknown', message: 'The check had not finished 30 s after …, so byway could not tell whether it can draw pages.' }, with no ready field (contracts: ready is optional; the store accepts it absent). The start-up line says "not checked in time: …", or "…, ready, with its OS sandbox off … byway could not tell whether the sandbox can start here now." Settings says "was not checked in time", "was not ready", or "could not tell whether the OS sandbox can start". serve.ts wires the progress, and serve.test.ts pins that wiring. No wall-clock assertions: fake timers, held launches (released at the end, with a check that the probe's browser was closed before any page opened), and an outer limit started only after the probe began. #### bb (head commit d082335)

All five items are built on fix/fx-d2. There are five commits on top of 6c77426, and the branch is pushed.

- **Security (d387960):**
  - `scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh` now installs Playwright's Chromium and its system libraries into `/opt/byway/ms-playwright` as root. It uses byway's own Node and the Playwright CLI that `packages/runner` resolves, then runs `chown -R root:root` and `chmod -R go-w` on `/opt/byway`.
  - It writes one profile, `playwright-chromium`, attached to that folder only, with `unconfined` and `userns`. It says what the profile allows and why the home folder is not used, prints the export line, and says to run it again after every Playwright upgrade.
  - The home-folder profile is gone. S3 (`XDG_CACHE_HOME`) no longer applies, because nothing depends on the default folder.
  - How byway gets the folder: the person exports `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. Playwright reads it in the engine process, whose environment byway does not scrub; `e2e.ts` and the web process pass it on. `withoutSecrets` only shapes Chromium's own environment, which does not need the variable.
  - The renderer:
    - The AppArmor fix text names the script and the export.
    - A Chromium that cannot start from `/opt/byway/ms-playwright` names the script instead of `playwright install`, which cannot write there.
    - New `browsersFolder()` / `browsersFolderOf()`, and the start-up output prints "Playwright's browsers folder: …" under the renderer line.
  - CI's Linux job runs the same script instead of Playwright's install, then writes `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` to `$GITHUB_ENV`. The matrix entries lost their install argument.
- **ADR-0106 (b1b062e):**
  - Decision 5 lists the three close reasons as the reasons when it was built; a later cause adds its own reason with the same start.
  - Decision 6 and the README say a failed job uploads 0, 1 or 2 artifacts.
  - Decisions 2 and 7 and the Consequences include Playwright's 30 s kill (up to about 65 s after retirement).
  - Re-checked against the code: the backstop sentence was softened ("at most with the count").
- **ADR-0105 (1dc0249):**
  - A "Who decided what" section and the Decided-by line separate the owner's decisions (the 2026-10-16 design; the 2026-10-18 answers as the coordinator relayed them) from Claude's delegated choices.
  - The real "no probe" alternative replaces the "Kept" entry. Item 8 and the alternatives record the move to the folder only root can change.
  - Item 1 now matches exactly what the guard pins.
- **Backlog, the two READMEs and the guard header:** updated to match.
- **Evidence and the CI flake (f46726f, d082335):** run 1's evidence is in ADR-0105. The C5 browser test that failed in run 2 is named in the backlog. ADR-0106 records that run 2 was the first to exercise the upload steps.

Items:

- **1. SECURITY: root-owned browsers folder, profile attached there only, PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH, home-folder variant removed, S3, CI runs the same steps, start-up line names the folder** (done): d387960. The script installs as root into /opt/byway/ms-playwright and makes /opt/byway root's alone. It writes one profile on $folder/chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell}, says what it allows, prints `export PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/byway/ms-playwright`, and says to rerun after upgrades. Its old checks for PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH and a space in $HOME are gone; it still refuses root. Mechanism: the person exports the variable (README and script output); the start-up line shows the folder in use. Runner: sandbox.ts has ROOT_BROWSERS_FOLDER, browsersFolderOf and the new AppArmor fix text; pages.ts has installHint (the script when Chromium comes from the root folder) and browsersFolder(); serve.ts prints the folder line. CI: the Linux step runs the script then writes PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH to $GITHUB_ENV, and Playwright's own install runs on non-Linux only. On CI the script printed restrict = 1, installed Chromium and the headless shell into /opt, and every Linux test passed.
- **2. ADR-0106 text (retire-2, W3, DOC-6 blocking; retire-1)** (done): b1b062e. Decision 5 has all three close reasons in a dated, extensible form. Decision 6, the README CI section and the workflow comment give 0, 1 or 2 artifacts depending on where the job failed. Decisions 2 and 7 and the Consequences include Playwright's 30 s kill (closeOrKill with DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT checked in coreBundle.js), so about 65 s after retirement. Re-check against the code: the backstop sentence corrected to 'at most with the count'; ADR-0105 added to Related; Evidence names round 1's corrections. d082335 replaced 'no run has exercised the uploads' with run 39152075629.
- **3. ADR-0105 (DOC-2, DOC-3, security change, guard statements)** (done): 1dc0249. 'Who decided what' and Decided by: the owner's 2026-10-16 design. - **Small consequences not recorded in any doc:**
  - Playwright writes its DEPENDENCIES_VALIDATED marker into the root-owned folder as root during the install. After 30 days it cannot refresh it as the person, so it re-checks system libraries at each browser launch. It fails silently and only costs time.
  - The install runs byway's own (user-writable) code as root. This one is recorded in ADR-0105's Consequences.
- **Design choice:** byway does not set PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH itself; the person exports it. If they forget, the sandbox fails with the AppArmor message, which names the variable, and the start-up line shows which folder is in use.

### The checks

Confirmed by the refuters (real and blocking): SBX2-1, SBX2-2, LNX-1, WEB-R2-1, DOC-R2-1, WB-1.

#### Lens: sandbox (blocking findings)

What it checked: I worked in my own clone, C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\check-sandbox\repo, at fix/fx-d2 d082335, installed offline.
