Processes: none of mine left. Three orphaned chrome-headless-shell renderers created on 2026-10-17 by other sessions were left alone. The clone is clean; my probe files are in …\check-sandbox\probe.

- **SBX2-1** (blocking, false-adr-statement / test-cannot-catch), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2397-2408 (checkRenderer's probe: on 'late' it only does `void p.pages.close()`), with ready() at pages.ts:740-770 and loaded()'s `browsers.take()` at pages.ts:627; packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:166-171 (close() keeps no closed state, so a later get() launches again). Claims: docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:65 ("A probe that has not answered by then is closed") and :124 ("Each draws only byway's own test page, within 15 s"); checkRenderer's doc comment. Test: packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts:33-58 (held()) and :241, :279

  A probe that misses its 15 s is not stopped. Its renderer is closed, but its ready() keeps going. When the held launch finally arrives, ready() passes find() and loaded() calls browsers.take(). close() has already cleared `current`, so take() launches Chromium again with the probe's setting and draws byway's test page in it. Only the attempt's own finally closes that browser, after the draw. With the sandbox on and a launch that died with no marker, this is an extra Chromium without the sandbox, started and drawing after the check has answered and the start-up line has printed. ADR-0105 says such a probe is closed and draws within 15 s. The test's held() helper rejects any second launch, so the test cannot see this happen.

  Evidence: Experiment in my clone (copy: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\check-sandbox\probe\keep\zz-check-lateprobe.test.ts). Setup: real launchChromium; setting on; the sandboxed launch throws launchFailure(DIED) for the Windows host; the first unsandboxed launch is held; probeMs 50. The check answered {osSandbox:true, ready:false, sandbox:'unknown', message: the possible-cause wording}. I then released the held launch and waited 8 s.
  
  Output:
  - LAUNCHES: [{"setting":true,"at":"before-bound"},{"setting":false,"at":"before-bound"},{"setting":false,"at":"after-bound"}]
  - PAGES OPENED: ["browser 2 newContext (after-bound)"]
  - CONNECTED AT END: [false, false]
  
  So a third Chromium, unsandboxed, launched and opened a context after the bound.
  
  The builders' own helper describes this case. renderer-check.test.ts:35 says "A second call (the renderer launching again after its close) fails at once", and :46 rejects it with 'launched once already'. So `expect(await probe.closedOnceReleased()).not.toContain('newContext')` (:241, :279) checks only the first browser.
  
  In production nothing rejects the second launch. It is bounded only by Playwright's launch timeout plus the test page's 30 s. It draws only byway's test page, so no person's content and no setting change.

  Suggested fix: Make a closed renderer stay closed. In render-browsers.ts, close() sets a flag, and get() then rejects with the shutdown reason instead of launching. That also covers a load between find() and take() at engine shutdown.
  
  Alternative inside checkRenderer: give each attempt a launch that refuses once the probe is late. The guard's pin for the `launch:` line in pages.ts would change with it.
  
  Change held() to record a second launch instead of rejecting it, and assert that nothing is launched and no context is opened after the bound.
  
  If the behaviour is kept instead, reword ADR-0105 :65 and :124 and checkRenderer's comment to say a late probe may still launch and draw the test page after the check has answered.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I could not refute it. I reproduced it in my own clone of fix/fx-d2 at d082335, using the real launchChromium and real Chromium, in both directions.
  
  When a probe misses its bound, checkRenderer only calls `void p.pages.close()`. That clears renderBrowsers' `current`. The attempt's ready() is still waiting in find(). When the held launch arrives, find() returns that browser, which close() then shuts. ready() goes on into loaded(), whose browsers.take() finds `current` undefined and launches Chromium again with the probe's setting. That third Chromium opens a context and draws byway's test page after the check has answered. Only the attempt's own `finally { pages.close() }` closes it, after the draw. Nothing stops it in production. Its time is bounded only by Playwright's default launch timeout (no timeout is set in chromiumLaunchOptions), twice over, plus the test page's 30 s.
  
  So two statements are false:
  - ADR-0105:124 says "Each draws only byway's own test page, within 15 s". The late probe draws after the bound.
  - ADR-0105:65 says "A probe that has not answered by then is closed", and checkRenderer's doc comment says the same. Only its renderer is closed. The probe goes on and launches again.
  
  The owner kept the probe on 2026-10-18 on that description. The test cannot catch this. held()'s own doc (renderer-check.test.ts:35) says "A second call (the renderer launching again after its close) fails at once", and it rejects that call. So `not.toContain('newContext')` (:241, :279) looks only at the browser that is closed before any page. The two tests pass unchanged (33/33 with render-browsers.test.ts).
  
  What holds, and limits the severity: the extra Chromium draws only READY_PAGE, a static page with no artifacts, so none of a person's content is drawn without the sandbox. The check's answer and `progress` are not changed. The browser is closed in the end. It happens only when the probe's launch takes more than 15 s, which the code itself says happens on a busy machine at start-up. With the sandbox on and failing (Windows), the extra Chromium has no sandbox and starts after the start-up line has printed.
  
  It is blocking because the ADR's stated bound on the unsandboxed probe is false, the owner decided on that statement, and the test is built so it cannot see the relaunch.

  Evidence: Scratch test: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-sandbox-SBX2-1\repo\packages\runner\test\zz-refute-lateprobe.test.ts. Output: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-sandbox-SBX2-1\out.txt. The probe's first launch is held, probeMs is 50, and the launch is released after the check answers.
  
  Sandbox on, sandboxed launch dies (DIED, Windows host). The check answered {osSandbox:true, ready:false, sandbox:'unknown', ...}. Events:
  - launch osSandbox=false at 2 ms (before the answer)
  - held launch released at 67 ms (after)
  - browser 1 up, close asked at 372 ms
  - launch osSandbox=false at 372 ms (AFTER the answer)
  - browser 2 up at 442 ms
  - browser 2 newContext at 442 ms
  - browser 2 test page loaded at 517 ms
  - browser 2 close asked at 523 ms
  
  Sandbox off: the same pattern. Browser 3 is launched with osSandbox=true after the answer, opens a context, draws the test page, and is then closed.
  
  Microtask order: get()'s `await launching` reaction is registered before close()'s `c.catch`. So find() resolves and loaded() calls take() while `current` is already undefined. Any order would launch again, because close() clears `current` before it awaits anything.
  
  The suggested fix works. I added to render-browsers.ts a `closed` flag, set first in close(), and `if (closed) throw ...` at the top of get() and again just before `const launching`. With it, my repro shows no launch after the bound in either direction, renderer-check.test.ts passes, and render-browsers.test.ts fails only at :207. Diff saved at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-sandbox-SBX2-1\sticky-close.diff.
  
  That test, from 957da70, "close() closes ... after a launch on its way; the next load launches again", asserts relaunch after close() on purpose. Nothing in production depends on that: worker.ts:92 closes pages only at shutdown, checkRenderer's attempt closes its own renderer, and the shared test renderers close in afterAll.
  
  I left the clone with only the scratch test untracked. The worktree, main and byway_review were not touched.

  Fix: 1. Fix the cause in packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts: a closed renderer stays closed.
     - Add `let closed = false`; close() sets it to true before anything else.
     - get() rejects at entry, and again after its look-up loop just before it creates `launching`, with an error that pages.ts words as byway's own close. For example, export a BrowsersClosed error carrying shutdownReason from render-browsers.ts, and have loaded() map it to RendererClosed, so drawFailure says "The page could not be drawn: byway closed the page renderer as it shut down." and readyFailure wraps it as its RendererClosed case.
     - Do not add a check inside `reading.then`. That would change the tested case of a take() made before close() whose launch is already on its way; keep it so close() still closes that launch.
     - Update render-browsers.test.ts:188-208: the next take() after close() now rejects with the shutdown reason and launches nothing (launched.length stays 1).
     - Update ADR-0106 decision 1 or 3 to say a closed renderer launches nothing more.
     - This also covers a render that reaches take() after the worker's shutdown close (worker.ts:92). Today that one launches a browser that only the process's exit ends.
  2. Fix renderer-check.test.ts held() (:33-58) so it records every launch instead of rejecting the second. In both late-probe tests (:226-242 and :249-268), after closedOnceReleased(), assert that `settings` is still [true, false] (or [false, true]) and that no browser ever got newContext. The test then fails on the current code and passes with step 1.
  3. ADR-0105:65 and :124 and checkRenderer's doc comment can then stay as written. Optionally make them exact: "its renderer is closed, and launches nothing more". If the behaviour is kept instead, those three places must say that a late probe may still launch Chromium under the probe's setting and draw byway's test page after the check has answered and the start-up line has printed, bounded only by Playwright's launch timeout plus 30 s. With the sandbox on, that browser is without the sandbox. The owner should be told, since the probe was kept on the description of a 15 s bound.

- **SBX2-2** (blocking, false-adr-statement), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:65 ("on, not ready with the sandboxed attempt's message (one possible cause, why and the fix)"); cause in packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2331 (readyFailure returns a StepError as it is, before the possible-cause branch) and checkRenderer's plain() at pages.ts:2413

  ADR-0105 item 5 says that when the probe misses its limit with the sandbox on, the check answers with the sandboxed attempt's message, "(one possible cause, why and the fix)". That holds only when the sandboxed launch or test page failed. When the sandboxed test page ran out its own 30 s (security software that stalls the sandboxed renderer rather than killing it), that message is the bare deadline line, with no word of the sandbox. The start-up line, the stored check and Settings ("was not ready: …") then never mention the sandbox, its why or its fix. The ADR's Consequences admit this for a run's refusal (the 30 s bullet, backlog item 7), but item 5 claims the check covers it.

  Evidence: Fake-timer run in my clone (copy: …\check-sandbox\probe\keep\zz-check-faketimers.test.ts, test 'on, the sandboxed test page hangs…'). Setup: the sandboxed browser's newContext never resolves, the probe's launch never resolves, the Windows host.
  - At 35 s (30 s deadline plus the 5 s close limit), progress.known = {"osSandbox":true,"ready":false,"sandbox":"unknown","message":"The page renderer did not draw byway's test page within 30 s."}.
  - At 50 s, the check answered that same record.
  - rendererLine gave "not available: The page renderer did not draw byway's test page within 30 s."
  
  The same file's other cases behave as built, under fake timers only:
  - A launch that died, probe never answering: unresolved at 14,999 ms, answered at 15,000 ms with the possible cause naming chrome-headless-shell.exe.
  - Sandbox off: {ready:true, sandbox:'unknown'} at 15 s, and the line's 30 s limit returned the same.
  - Nothing known at 30 s: no `ready`, and "not checked in time: …".

  Suggested fix: ADR-0105 is not on main yet, so reword :65 in place, for example: "on, not ready with the sandboxed attempt's message: for a launch or test page that failed with no reason given, one possible cause, why and the fix; for a test page past its own 30 s, only that (see Consequences)".
  
  Or build round 1's W1 point 5: in readyFailure, route a sandboxed test-page deadline through maybeTheSandbox. That fixes the check and backlog item 7 together. Add a fake-timer test for the hung sandboxed test page in renderer-check.test.ts.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim is real, but only as a false statement in the ADR. The scenario it uses to show harm is wrong. ADR-0105:65 says that when the probe misses its 15 s with the sandbox on, the check answers with the sandboxed attempt's message "(one possible cause, why and the fix)". That is false on one path the code can reach. If the sandboxed test page runs out its own 30 s, pages.ts:621 makes a bare deadline StepError. Both readyFailure (pages.ts:2331) and checkRenderer's plain() (pages.ts:2413) pass it on unchanged, so the line, the stored record and Settings say nothing of the sandbox.
  
  The claim's motivating case does not go that way. Take security software that stalls only the sandboxed renderer: the probe without the sandbox draws the page, and the check falls through to sandboxFails (pages.ts:2429). The result is sandbox 'fails' with why and the fix. The bare message reaches the record only in two cases. (a) The probe without the sandbox also fails to answer within 15 s, so nothing points at the sandbox and the bare message is honest. (b) A narrow race: the start-up line's 30 s limit lands while the probe is still running, and progress.known holds the bare message.
  
  So the code is defensible and the defect is the record. Item 4's :57 has the same overclaim: "A test page that fails with the sandbox on and no marker names the sandbox as one possible cause" has no deadline exception, and :126 qualifies it for runs only. I count it as blocking because the project holds ADR statements to the code. This ADR is not on main yet, so it can be edited in place now; after merge it would need a later note. The fix is a few clauses.

  Evidence: My own clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-sandbox-SBX2-2\repo (fix/fx-d2 at d082335). Test: packages\runner\test\zz-refute-sbx2-2.test.ts, using fake browsers, fake timers and a Windows host.
  
  - A (the claimant's setup): the sandboxed newContext never resolves and the probe's launch never resolves. The check answers at 50 s with {osSandbox:true, ready:false, sandbox:'unknown', message:"The page renderer did not draw byway's test page within 30 s."}. rendererLine gives "not available: The page renderer did not draw byway's test page within 30 s." This confirms the claim's result.
  - B (the claim's motivating case): only the sandboxed renderer stalls; the browser without the sandbox draws the test page. The check gives sandbox 'fails', with sandboxFailureMessage and the Windows why and fix naming chrome-headless-shell.exe. Launch settings were [true, false]. So for that scenario the claim's "the start-up line, the stored check and Settings then never mention the sandbox" is false.
  - C: case B, contributor mode, engine up 1, 3, 5 or 10 s after the check began. For 1 to 5 s the line says "not checked in time", because in my fake the first attempt ends at 35 s with the 5 s close limit. At 10 s the line says sandbox 'fails' with the fix.
  - D (the race): the sandboxed context opens but its page never loads, the probe without the sandbox draws after 2 s, and the line's limit lands at 30.5 s. The line, and so the store record (serve.ts:213-216 records the line's answer), gets the bare deadline message. The check itself finishes about 1.5 s later with sandbox 'fails', but nothing records that answer.
  
  Code path: pages.ts:621 (the check's deadline message), :2331 (`if (err instanceof StepError) return err;` comes before the possible-cause branch at :2334), :2413 (plain passes a StepError's message through), :2424-2426 (`sandboxed` returned when the probe is late), :2429 (a probe that draws makes the sandbox 'fails').
  
  ADR text: :57 and :65 overclaim. :126 and backlog row "What D2 left" item 7 admit only the run case. The existing test renderer-check.test.ts ("on, and the probe without the sandbox does not answer…") covers only a launch that died (DIED), so no test pins the deadline sub-case. I did not touch the worktree, main or byway_review. No paid calls, no push.

  Fix: Proportionate fix, docs plus one test, no behaviour change. ADR-0105 is not on main, so edit in place:
  1. :65: replace "on, not ready with the sandboxed attempt's message (one possible cause, why and the fix)" with "on, not ready with the sandboxed attempt's message: for a launch that died, or a test page that failed, with no reason given, the sandbox as one possible cause, with why and the fix; for a test page past its own 30 s, only that it was not drawn in time (see Consequences)".
  2. :57: after "A test page that fails with the sandbox on and no marker", add ", other than one past its own 30 s,".
  3. :126: widen it to the check. "A sandboxed test page slower than 30 s is said as 'did not draw byway's test page within 30 s', with no word of the sandbox: in a run's refusal unless the start-up check found the sandbox cannot start; and in the start-up check when the probe without the sandbox does not answer within its 15 s either, or when the start-up line's 30 s comes while that probe runs. When the probe draws the page in time, the check finds the sandbox the cause, with why and the fix." Say the same in backlog row "What D2 left" item 7.
  4. Pin the wording in packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts with fake-browser cases under fake timers:
     (a) The sandboxed context never opens and the probe draws: expect sandbox 'fails' with sandboxFailureMessage(sandboxAdvice(windows, onDisk)).
     (b) The sandboxed context never opens and the probe is late: expect the bare deadline message.
     chromiumOnDisk does real I/O, so advance the fake clock in steps rather than in one jump.
  
  Alternative, a code change that needs the coordinator's approval under the fix-loop rule (round 1's W1 point 5): mark the check's deadline with its own StepError subclass from late() for screen 'check'. In readyFailure, when osSandbox is on and the error is that subclass, return possibleSandboxCause(said, advice) before the `instanceof StepError` pass-through at :2331. That makes :57 and :65 true as written, closes backlog item 7 for runs, and fits item 5's handling of a launch that died while the probe is late. It changes what a refused run says, so :126, item 7 and renderer-check.test.ts would all need updating, plus a refusal test for a sandboxed test page past its deadline. Either way, don't word the fix as the claim does ("security software that stalls the sandboxed renderer" leads to no mention of the sandbox). In that case the check does find the sandbox the cause.

#### Lens: security (blocking findings)

What it checked: I worked in my own clone at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\check-linux-apparmor\repo, on fix/fx-d2 at d082335, after pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline. The worktree, main's checkout and byway_review were not touched. I did not open .env.agents, push, stash or make a paid call. My only gh use was read-only: run list, run view and job log. No processes of mine are left running.

**Ownership and the glob (holds):**
- /opt is the distribution's own folder. The script creates /opt/byway and /opt/byway/ms-playwright as root:root 755, then runs chown -R root:root and chmod -R go-w.
- Playwright 1.63 extracts with `getExtractedMode(mode) & 511` (coreBundle.js:10467), so the download cannot carry setuid or setgid bits. Root's umask applies when it writes files.
- chown -R does not follow symlinks.
- AppArmor attaches on the resolved path. A symlink from a person's folder into /opt runs the root-owned binary; a link in /opt pointing at a person's file would not attach. protected_hardlinks stops the hardlink trick.
- The glob `chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell}` matches Playwright 1.63's linux64 and arm64 layouts for full Chromium and the headless shell, and nothing else that the download contains (ffmpeg is not matched).
- If PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH points elsewhere, nothing attaches: Chromium fails with the AppArmor message and gains no privilege.
- The script writes no sysctl, and the workflow does not touch the restriction.
- The only other privilege use is Playwright's --with-deps, which runs apt-get as root.

**Runtime use of the folder (holds):**
- The engine and its worker run inside serve.ts's own process (startDevEngine; nothing in packages/runner/src spawns a process). Playwright reads PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH from that process's environment.
- The web child gets `{...env}`, and e2e.ts passes `...process.env` on.
- withoutSecrets applies only to Chromium's own environment and to next build.
- serve.ts prints "Playwright's browsers folder: …" after the renderer line.
- installHint names the script when the folder is /opt/byway/ms-playwright, so after a Playwright bump a missing revision gives the script, both at a run's launch and in the start-up check's second attempt.

**CI (read-only):**
- Run 39149469179 at d387960, Linux job 117739965263:
  - "kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1";
  - Chrome for Testing and the headless shell downloaded into /opt/byway/ms-playwright;
  - PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH shown in every later step's environment;
  - 198 files and 4,989 tests passed, including pages.test.ts (62 tests) with builder A's drift test, now also run on Linux;
  - 54 browser tests passed, and the e2e line said "Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox".
- Run 39152075629 at f46726f: Linux passed; Windows failed only in the C5 browser test's 120 s wait.
- d082335 differs from d387960 only in comments, docs and one guard test's description, so the branch head has no green run of its own, as builder B said.
- CI runs the same script as the README and sets the same variable through $GITHUB_ENV. ci.test.ts pins the script, the workflow, the README and sandbox.ts to the same path and folder.
- apps/web's @playwright/test and packages/runner's playwright are both 1.63.0, so one install serves both.

**Tests run locally:** ci, serve, sandbox, launch, render-browsers and renderer-check: 6 files, 125 tests passed.

**Round 1's S3:** fixed. The script no longer uses $HOME or XDG_CACHE_HOME, and ci.test.ts forbids both.

**SEC-5 after round 2 (holds):**
- render-browsers.ts is unchanged since round 1.
- Round 2's pages.ts changes add no unbounded wait:
  - The probe is bounded by PROBE_MS (15 s). Its renderer is closed without the check waiting, and `done`'s finally closes it again once ready() settles. ready() is bounded by Playwright's launch timeout and the load's limits.
  - ready()'s new failure path awaits only a store read (errors caught) and an import of Playwright.
  - rendererCheckWithin still bounds the start-up line.
- ADR-0106 items 2, 5 and 7 now match the code: the three close reasons, and Playwright's 30 s kill after byway's bounded close.
- Retired browsers are still always closed at their last load or at the backstop, and pages.close() closes the current browser and every retired one.

**Sources for LNX-1:**
- [Qualys advisory](https://www.qualys.com/2025/three-bypasses-of-Ubuntu-unprivileged-user-namespace-restrictions.txt)
- [Ubuntu Discourse: Understanding AppArmor User Namespace Restriction](https://discourse.ubuntu.com/t/understanding-apparmor-user-namespace-restriction/58007)
- [AppArmor's nautilus profile](https://gitlab.com/apparmor/apparmor/-/raw/master/profiles/apparmor.d/nautilus)
- [BleepingComputer coverage](https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/new-ubuntu-linux-security-bypasses-require-manual-mitigations/)

- **LNX-1** (blocking, false-security-statement), at scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:3-4, 11-13, 67-68 (comment and the message it prints); README.md:157; docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:75 (Decision 8: "Nothing the person can write is covered, and the rest of the machine stays restricted") and :105 (the rejected home-folder alternative); packages/runner/src/sandbox.ts:182-184 (ROOT_BROWSERS_FOLDER doc) and :213 (the AppArmor fix shown in Settings and the start-up line: "Allow them for Playwright's Chromium alone"); .github/workflows/verify.yml:73-75; docs/backlog.md:167; scripts/test/ci.test.ts:107 (test name)

  Round 2's Linux change says its profile lets the Chromium programs in /opt/byway/ms-playwright "and nothing else" create user namespaces. The script says it does this "without giving those namespaces to any program you can change", and the docs say "the rest of the machine stays restricted". That is false. The profile is `flags=(unconfined) { userns, }`, the same template as Ubuntu's own nautilus, chrome and trinity profiles. Any program running as the person can use it to create user namespaces in two ways:
  (a) Start one of those root-owned binaries with a library of its own: `LD_PRELOAD=./x.so /opt/byway/ms-playwright/chromium_headless_shell-1243/chrome-headless-shell-linux64/chrome-headless-shell`. An attachment from unconfined sets no secure-exec, so the library's constructor runs inside playwright-chromium. Programs it starts stay in that profile.
  (b) While kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, which is Ubuntu 24.04's default, enter the profile directly: `aa-exec -p playwright-chromium -- unshare -U -r sh`.
  The ADR rejects the home-folder profile because any program running as the person "could put a file there and get the user namespaces … which would undo the restriction for that person". The chosen profile does the same through (a) on every Ubuntu, and through (b) on a default 24.04. The root folder removes only the file-drop route. So the security reason recorded for this delegated choice is overstated. Against it, the ADR weighs real costs: sudo runs the person's Node and byway's Playwright as root at install and after every upgrade, a variable must be exported, and the renderer breaks after each upgrade until the script runs again. The ADR's Consequences also understate the root step: it trusts "what is in the byway folder", but the Node it runs as root is whatever `command -v node` finds, often under ~/.nvm, which any program running as the person can write. The machine is no weaker than a stock 24.04, which already ships such profiles. The finding is the false security claims, in the README, ADR-0105, the UI fix text and the script output, on which this decision rests.

  Evidence: - Qualys advisory, 2025-03 (https://www.qualys.com/2025/three-bypasses-of-Ubuntu-unprivileged-user-namespace-restrictions.txt), shown on Ubuntu 24.04:
    - `aa-exec -p trinity -- unshare -U -r -m /bin/sh` from an unprivileged shell gives a namespace where `mount --bind` succeeds. aa-exec and the profile are installed by default, and the advisory says the sysctl fix "was never enabled by default".
    - `LD_PRELOAD=./shell.so /usr/bin/nautilus`, then `unshare -U -r -m /bin/sh` from the injected shell, also works.
  - Ubuntu's own post (https://discourse.ubuntu.com/t/understanding-apparmor-user-namespace-restriction/58007) calls unconfined profiles that allow userns a "trivial bypass" for an unconfined user. It says only kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined=1 stops the aa-exec route, and gives no default-on release.
  - AppArmor's nautilus profile (gitlab.com/apparmor/apparmor profiles/apparmor.d/nautilus) is `flags=(unconfined)` with `userns,` attached to a root-owned /usr/bin path. It has the same shape as the profile the script writes: `"profile playwright-chromium $folder/chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell} flags=(unconfined) {" '  userns,' '}'` (script line 63).
  - CI run 39149469179 (Linux job 117739965263, log line 460) printed the false sentence: "the AppArmor profile /etc/apparmor.d/playwright-chromium lets the Chromium programs there, and nothing else, create user namespaces".
  - I ran no Linux probe: this machine is Windows, and pushing to CI was not allowed. The mechanism is the published, reproduced Qualys one, applied to an identical profile.

  Suggested fix: Keep the design. Correct every statement listed above to the property that holds, and let the owner confirm the trade-off, because the security review's premise does not hold as written. Suggested README / ADR-0105 Decision 8 wording:
  "The profile is attached only to the Chromium programs in that folder, which only root can change, and confines them in no other way, as Ubuntu's own profile does for Google Chrome in /opt/google/chrome. Like Ubuntu's own profiles of this kind, it does not stop a program running as you from using it to create user namespaces. Such a program can start one of those Chromium programs with a library of its own (LD_PRELOAD). While kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, Ubuntu 24.04's default, it can also enter the profile with aa-exec, as it already can with Ubuntu's own chrome, nautilus or trinity profiles. What the folder adds is that the profile covers no file such a program could put there or replace; on ~/.cache/ms-playwright it would."
  In sandbox.ts:213, drop "alone". Fix the script's header and its final echo, the verify.yml comment, the backlog row and the ci.test.ts test name to match. In ADR-0105's Alternatives, rewrite the home-folder entry: its extra exposure is the file route only. In Consequences, say that the root step also runs the `node` found on the person's PATH. Then ask the owner to confirm the root folder against its costs.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I could not refute it. The profile the script writes (scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:63) has the same shape as the profiles Qualys bypassed: one `flags=(unconfined) { userns, }` profile attached by path. The kernel source confirms the LD_PRELOAD route. So any program running as the person can still create user namespaces through this profile, on every Ubuntu with the restriction. On 24.04 it can also enter the profile with aa-exec. Several statements are therefore false: "and nothing else" (README:157, the script's echo at :67-68, verify.yml:74), "the rest of the machine stays restricted" (README:157, script:11, ADR-0105:75, verify.yml:74-75), "without giving those namespaces to any program you can change" (script:3-4), and the fix text "Allow them for Playwright's Chromium alone" (sandbox.ts:213). The ADR rejects the home-folder profile because a program "could … get the user namespaces … which would undo the restriction for that person" (ADR-0105:105). The chosen profile does the same, through a library instead of a dropped file. That reason is the security argument recorded for a delegated choice whose costs are real: sudo runs the person's Node as root, a variable must be exported, and the script must run again after every upgrade. False security claims in the README, the in-app fix text, the script's output and an ADR's rationale must be corrected before merge under this repo's honest-provenance rule. The design itself does not need to block. On a stock 24.04 the machine is no weaker, and Ubuntu desktops already ship profiles of the same kind.

  Evidence: Clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-security-LNX-1\repo (fix/fx-d2 at d082335). Probe scripts: ...\refute-security-LNX-1\probe\.
  - Profile text, script:63: `profile playwright-chromium $folder/chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell} flags=(unconfined) {  userns, }`.
  - Kernel source, torvalds/linux master, security/apparmor/domain.c: in profile_transition(), the branch `if (profile_unconfined(profile))` calls find_attach and returns, either with the attached label or with `aa_get_newest_label(&profile->label)`. It never sets *secure_exec. apparmor_bprm_creds_for_exec() sets `bprm->secureexec = 1` only `if (unsafe)`. So an exec from an unconfined task into playwright-chromium has no AT_SECURE, and ld.so honours LD_PRELOAD (route a).
  - policy_unpack.c: PACKED_MODE_UNCONFINED sets `profile->label.flags |= FLAG_UNCONFINED`. A task inside playwright-chromium therefore takes the same unconfined branch: a program it starts that matches no other attachment stays in playwright-chromium. The preloaded library's shell, and anything Chromium itself starts, can create namespaces. Not reproduced: Chromium's own subprocess switches (e.g. --renderer-cmd-prefix) probably give the same result with no compiled library.
  - The sysctl kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined only touches change_profile (priv_restricted_transition, "change_profile unprivileged unconfined converted to stacking"). It does not stop route (a).
  - Route (b), aa-exec: Qualys (tested on 24.04) says the sysctl was "never enabled by default". Canonical's discourse post 58007 calls such profiles "a trivial bypass" and gives the sysctl only as manual hardening. A devco.re write-up (2025-06-26) says it has been on by default since 25.04. WSL Ubuntu 26.04 on this machine (apparmor 5.0.0~beta1-0ubuntu7) has /usr/lib/sysctl.d/10-apparmor.conf with both `apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1` and `apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined = 1`. Route (b) therefore applies to 24.04's default only. Route (a) applies to every release.
  - Ubuntu ships the same template for other programs: /etc/apparmor.d/chrome, trinity, loupe and epiphany in that WSL Ubuntu are `flags=(unconfined) { userns, @{exec_path} mr, ... }`.
  - I ran no live AppArmor probe either. The WSL kernel (6.18.33.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2) has AppArmor off (/sys/module/apparmor/parameters/enabled = N; no /sys/kernel/security/lsm), there is no passwordless sudo, and pushing to CI was not allowed. The conclusion rests on the kernel source plus Qualys's reproduction on an identical profile shape.
  - Consequences understatement confirmed. Script:46 runs `node=$(command -v node)`, and :58 runs `sudo env ... "$node" "$cli" install`. Root therefore runs whatever node is first on the person's PATH (e.g. ~/.nvm), not only "what is in the byway folder" (ADR-0105:121).
  - Text pinned in tests: packages/runner/test/sandbox.test.ts:104 pins the "Chromium alone" fix string; scripts/test/ci.test.ts:107 (test name) and :132-133 (comment) repeat the claim.

  Fix: Keep the design and fix the words. Then put the trade-off to the owner, because the security reason recorded for the root folder is much weaker than stated.
  1. README:157, ADR-0105 Decision 8 (line 75), and the script's header (lines 2-13) and final echo (67-68). Say what holds: the profile is attached only to the Chromium programs in /opt/byway/ms-playwright, which only root can change, and confines them in no other way, as Ubuntu's own profiles do for Google Chrome, Loupe or Epiphany. Like those profiles, it does not stop a program running as you from creating user namespaces, in two ways. First, a program can start one of those Chromium programs with a library of its own (LD_PRELOAD), and anything started from there stays in the profile; this works on every release. Second, on Ubuntu 24.04, where kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0 by default, a program can enter the profile with aa-exec, as it can with Ubuntu's own profiles; 25.04 and later set it to 1. What the root folder adds is that no file such a program writes is covered. On ~/.cache/ms-playwright one would be. Do not say "and nothing else" or "the rest of the machine stays restricted". The claimant's "no weaker than stock" holds on 24.04 and on desktops that already have such a program installed. On a 25.04+ machine with none installed, this profile can be the one way around the restriction, so say "as installing Google Chrome does" rather than "no weaker".
  2. sandbox.ts:213: drop "alone", e.g. "Allow them for Playwright's Chromium, installed in a folder only root can change: ...", and update the pinned string in packages/runner/test/sandbox.test.ts:104 in the same commit. sandbox.ts:182-184: "the only folder its AppArmor profile is attached to (a profile on a folder the person can write would also cover any file a program they run put there)".
  3. Correct the same wording in verify.yml:73-75, docs/backlog.md:167 ("lets only the Chromium there create user namespaces"), the ci.test.ts:107 test name and the :132-133 comment.
  4. ADR-0105 Alternatives (line 105): the home-folder profile's extra exposure is the file route only. Both designs let a program running as the person get user namespaces. Record that the root folder was kept, or dropped, for that narrower gain, against its costs.
  5. ADR-0105 Consequences (line 121): the root step also runs, as root, the `node` first on the person's PATH, which is often under ~/.nvm and writable by the person. This is the same exposure as any sudo typed in a shell that a program running as the person can already change.
  6. Optional: have the script print kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined next to the userns line, so CI records which case route (b) is in.
  7. Ask the owner to confirm the root folder against its costs (sudo running Node and Playwright as root at install and after every upgrade, the exported variable, a broken renderer after an upgrade until the script runs again). Do not start another fix round without that approval.

- **LNX-2** (non-blocking, script-destructive-edge), at scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:57, 60-61

  The script hard-codes /opt/byway as the parent of its root-only folder. It then changes owner and mode on the whole of /opt/byway recursively, without checking what is already there:
  - `sudo install -d -o root -g root -m 755 /opt/byway "$folder"`. GNU install -d also resets the owner and mode of a directory that already exists.
  - `sudo chown -R root:root /opt/byway` and `sudo chmod -R go-w /opt/byway`.
  /opt/byway is a natural place to clone or deploy an app called byway, and the script must be run from the byway checkout. If the checkout is /opt/byway, the script does three things:
  - installs Chromium inside the checkout, at /opt/byway/ms-playwright;
  - makes the person's whole checkout root-owned and not writable, including .git, node_modules and apps/web's build folder, so git pull, pnpm install and pnpm start then fail with EACCES or git's "dubious ownership";
  - does all this silently, and the script ends by reporting success.
  The obvious repair, `sudo chown -R $USER /opt/byway`, then hands the profiled folder back to the person. The same happens to anything else a person keeps in /opt/byway.

  Evidence: Script lines 57, 60 and 61 as quoted. The only precondition the script checks is `[ ! -f packages/runner/package.json ]` ("Run this in the byway folder"), which passes when the current folder is /opt/byway. Nothing compares /opt/byway's existing owner or contents. The README ("Run this once, as yourself, in the byway folder") names no clone location. Not run here: there is no sudo or Linux on this machine. This is a reading of the script and of GNU install's documented behaviour on existing directories.

  Suggested fix: Before any sudo step, refuse when /opt/byway exists and is not owned by root, or holds anything other than ms-playwright. Also refuse when the current folder is inside /opt/byway, for example: `case "$(pwd -P)/" in /opt/byway/*) echo "Run this from a byway folder outside /opt/byway" >&2; exit 1;; esac`. Change owner and mode recursively only on "$folder", and keep install -d for /opt/byway itself. Pin the guard in ci.test.ts beside the existing ownership pins.

Round 1's web findings, rechecked in the real app:
- **WEB-1 is fixed**, read from Chromium's own accessibility tree over CDP (DOM.describeNode, then Accessibility.getFullAXTree):

- **W1 and DOC-5 wording in Settings is fixed.** I wrote 10 start-up check records into the store and read Settings and the banner for each:
  - not checked in time, on and off;
  - not ready, with the possible-cause message;
  - off, ready, sandbox unknown;
  - fails, with the setting on, off and changed since;
  - starts.
  Each text matched rendererFindings and rendererBanner. None says "could not start" for a check that had not finished. The cannot-start banner appears only when the setting is on and the check found 'fails'. No message suggests switching the sandbox off.
  At 375 px, nothing scrolls sideways, with the confirmation open or with the longest message.

- **WEB-R2-1** (blocking, wrong-behaviour / false-ui-copy), at apps/web/app/layout.tsx:40-63: the banner comes from rendererBanner(), computed only in the root layout's server render. Compare apps/web/src/components/Nav.tsx:24-38, whose comment says "the header outlives page changes", which is why the waiting count polls. Claims that become untrue: OFF_RISK in apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:14 ("byway shows a banner on every screen while it is off", shown in the confirmation), README.md:147-148, apps/web/README.md:148, docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:70 (Decision 6), and apps/web/e2e/renderer-sandbox.spec.ts:63-70, which checks only after full page.goto loads.

  Only the tab where the switch was pressed is sure to show the banner.
  - The banner is drawn only by the root layout's server render. Next's App Router keeps the root layout across client-side navigations.
  - The tab where the person presses the switch gets the banner through PageRendererSettings' router.refresh().
  - Any other byway tab that was open at that moment never gets it. Every screen it reaches by its own links shows no banner, until a full reload: Start, Library, History, Settings (whose switch there already reads off), a new recommendation, and a whole run whose pages are drawn without the sandbox.
  - The owner's design asks for a persistent banner on every screen while the sandbox is off, so the person cannot forget it. The confirmation the person has just read says the same. Neither holds in that tab.
  - The same mechanism should also leave a stale "off" banner after the sandbox is turned back on in another tab, and no cannot-start banner in a tab opened before a restart. Both are inferred, not run.

  Evidence: Demo mode in my clone (own data directory, ports 3927 and 7927), driven by the Playwright probe C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\check-web\repo\apps\web\e2e\zz-check.spec.ts. Logs are in ...\check-web\.
  
  probe-tabs.log, test "two tabs": the sandbox on; tab B opens /; tab A turns the sandbox off through Settings' confirmation; tab B then clicks its Main nav links.
    B start banner: 0
    A banner after off: 1
    B after clicking History (/history): banner=0
    B after clicking Library (/library): banner=0
    B after clicking Start (/): banner=0
    B after clicking Settings (/settings): banner=0 switch=false
    B after clicking History (/history): banner=0
    B after reload: banner=1
  
  probe-tabs2.log, test "a run started in the other tab": tab B, opened before the switch, types a problem on Start, gets the recommendation and starts a C1 run.
    B recommend (/recommend/be7d…): banner=0
    B run 1c3c1004… at its first turn (confirm): banner=0 notes=1
  The one note is "Pages in this step were drawn with the page renderer's OS sandbox off". Screenshot tab-b-run.png shows the demo banner and no sandbox banner over that run.

  Suggested fix: Keep the banner up to date the way the waiting count is kept:
  - Make the banner a client component in the layout. The server render gives it its first state.
  - On every pathname change, and every few seconds while the tab is visible, it reads the renderer's state again through useRefresher and usePolling, as Nav does with /api/waiting.
  - The read can be a GET /api/renderer that returns only rendererBanner()'s input, or the renderer's state added to /api/waiting's answer.
  - If a GET /api/renderer is added, update guard-sandbox.test.ts's "/api/renderer" pin and its description, which today say only Settings' switch calls it.
  
  Test it in apps/web/e2e/renderer-sandbox.spec.ts:
  - Open a second page before turning the sandbox off.
  - Navigate it by the Main nav links, not page.goto, and expect the banner.
  - Expect the banner gone there after the sandbox is back on.
  
  Until this is built, the copy cannot say "every screen" without a qualifier.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The main claim is real, and I reproduced it in my own clone. Only the server render of the root layout draws the sandbox banner. Next's App Router keeps that layout across client-side navigation. Nothing re-reads the renderer state except the router.refresh() in the tab where the switch was pressed. Repo-wide grep finds only three router.refresh() calls, in PageRendererSettings, SettingsView and SpendingRecords. There is no revalidatePath, BroadcastChannel or storage listener for this state. Nav polls only /api/waiting.
  
  So any byway tab that was open when the sandbox went off shows no banner on any screen it reaches by its own links, and Nav's 5 s polling does not change that. The reverse also happens. After the sandbox goes back on in another tab, an old tab keeps the "off" banner on every screen, including Settings, where the switch it renders reads on. That screen contradicts itself.
  
  The copy is false in a state a person can reach with two tabs of a local app. That copy is the confirmation shown at the moment of consent, plus the READMEs and ADR-0105's Decision 6. This is the mitigation the owner chose for turning off a security boundary. The codebase already knows the header outlives page changes, which is why Nav polls, and the banner does not use that pattern. The e2e test cannot catch this because it moves between screens only with page.goto.
  
  What softens it, though not enough to make it non-blocking:
  - The tab where the switch was pressed is correct.
  - A tab opened after the switch keeps the banner across client navigation.
  - A reload fixes the tab.
  - Each step drawn without the sandbox still writes its run note, per the checker's probe-tabs2 evidence.
  
  One inferred sub-claim is wrong. A tab opened before a `pnpm start` restart does not stay stale. serve.ts runs `next build` on every start, so the build ID changes. Next's fetch-server-response.js then does a full page load (doMpaNavigation) on that tab's first navigation. The fresh layout carries the banner. The claim should drop the restart case, at least for `pnpm start`. It is untested for `pnpm dev`, whose build ID does not change.

  Evidence: My own clone: C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-ui-WEB-R2-1\repo at d082335. Demo mode on ports 3937 and 7937 with its own data folder. Probes: apps\web\e2e\zz-refute.spec.ts and zz-restart.spec.ts. Logs: ...\refute-ui-WEB-R2-1\probe.log and probe-restart.log.
  
  probe.log, two pages in one context. Tab B opens /, then tab A turns the sandbox off through Settings' switch and its confirmation.
    A after off: banner=1 kind=off
    B immediately: 0
    B Main nav History / Library / Start / Settings: 0 each; on Settings, B's switch shows aria-checked=false
    B Settings after waiting 12 s while visible (two of Nav's polls): 0
    B back, and the wordmark link: 0
    B reload: 1
  
  Reverse: tab A turns it back on.
    A: 0
    B History: banner=1 kind=off
    B Settings: switch aria-checked=true and banner=1 kind=off
    B reload: 0
  
  Control: a tab opened while the sandbox is off keeps the banner on /, /history and /library through client navigation.
  
  probe-restart.log: tab B opens while the sandbox is on, the sandbox goes off over the API (B History: banner 0), and the server is stopped and started again. The start rebuilds, and its start-up line says the sandbox is off. B's next click on Library gives "banner 1, same document false": the build ID changed, so Next loaded the whole page.
  
  Code:
  - apps/web/app/layout.tsx:40-63 computes rendererBanner() only in the server render.
  - apps/web/src/components/Nav.tsx:24-38 polls only /api/waiting.
  - node_modules/next/dist/client/components/router-reducer/fetch-server-response.js:175-177 falls back to a full load when the build ID differs.
  - test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:92 pins every /\/api\/renderer\b/ line to PageRendererSettings' PATCH alone.
  
  The worktree, main and byway_review were not touched. I made no paid calls and pushed nothing. My demo server is stopped and its ports are free.

  Fix: Keep the banner up to date in the browser, the way Nav keeps the waiting count.
  
  1. Add a client component, SandboxBanner, to the root layout.
     - The server's rendererBanner(renderer) gives it its first state.
     - It re-reads the renderer's state with useRefresher(pathname, ...) plus usePolling(refresher, 5000).
     - Polling while the tab is visible is required, not only re-reading on pathname changes. A person sitting on one screen, such as a running run's page or Settings, must see the banner appear and go without navigating.
     - rendererBanner() in src/view/renderer.ts imports only a type, so the client can call it on what it reads.
  2. Choose where the read comes from.
     - Option (a), less churn: add the renderer's state to /api/waiting's answer, e.g. { count, renderer: { osSandbox, lastCheck: { sandbox } } }. Let one client component own a single refresher that feeds both Nav's count and the banner. This leaves guard-sandbox.test.ts' /api/renderer pin as it is.
     - Option (b): add GET to app/api/renderer/route.ts. It returns only the inputs rendererBanner() needs, never anything that switches. Update guard-sandbox.test.ts:92's pinned lines, and its description "called only by Settings' switch", so the PATCH stays the only writer.
  3. In the tab where the switch is pressed, keep the router.refresh(), or replace it with the refresher's now(), so that tab updates at once.
  4. Test it in apps/web/e2e/renderer-sandbox.spec.ts:
     - Open a second page with context.newPage() before turning the sandbox off.
     - On that page, expect the banner without navigating. Use Playwright's expect polling, which covers the 5 s poll inside the 30 s expect timeout, so the test sets no wall-clock limit of its own.
     - Then move it through the Main nav links, not page.goto, and expect the banner on each screen.
     - Turn the sandbox back on in the first page, and expect the banner gone in the second, both on the screen it is showing and after a nav click.
  5. Correct the claim: drop "no cannot-start banner in a tab opened before a restart". With `pnpm start`, the rebuild changes the build ID and the first navigation reloads the whole page. Polling would also cover the screen the tab is sitting on.
  6. Until the fix lands, OFF_RISK, README.md:147-148, apps/web/README.md:148 and ADR-0105 Decision 6 overstate "every screen".

- **DOC-R2-2** (non-blocking, overstated security statement), at README.md:157 ('lets the Chromium programs in that folder, and nothing else, create user namespaces'); scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:8-13 and :67-68; .github/workflows/verify.yml:74-75; docs/adr/0105-…md:75 ('Nothing the person can write is covered, and the rest of the machine stays restricted'), :105, :121

  The docs say only the Chromium programs in /opt/byway/ms-playwright can create user namespaces. The script writes a named profile, `profile playwright-chromium … flags=(unconfined) { userns, }` (script:63). On a kernel where `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined` is 0, an unconfined program running as the person can enter that profile by name, e.g. `aa-exec -p playwright-chromium -- unshare -U -r sh`, and get user namespaces whatever its path. Qualys' March 2025 advisory 'Three bypasses of Ubuntu's unprivileged user namespace restrictions' did exactly this with Ubuntu's own trinity and chrome profiles, which have the same shape. Stock 24.04 already ships such profiles, so the root folder is still the right call and adds no new way round the restriction. But 'and nothing else' is not true. The reason given at ADR :105 for rejecting the home-folder profile ('would undo the restriction for that person') applies just as well to any named unconfined+userns profile under aa-exec. Separately, ADR :121 says the script 'trusts what is in the byway folder'. It also runs `command -v node` as root (script:46,58), and that Node is often under the person's home folder (nvm), outside the byway folder.

  Evidence: script:63: `printf … "profile playwright-chromium $folder/chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell} flags=(unconfined) {" '  userns,' '}' | sudo tee "$profile"`. The profile has a name, and the script sets nothing that restricts changing into it. The script only reads `apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns`, and ci.test.ts forbids touching sysctls. I did not reproduce this, because this machine has no Ubuntu. It rests on the published advisory and on Ubuntu 24.04's own `chrome` profile, which has the same unconfined+userns shape. script:46: `node=$(command -v node)`; script:58: `sudo env PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH="$folder" "$node" "$cli" install --with-deps chromium`.

  Suggested fix: Change wording only; no code change is needed.
  - README :157, the script header and the verify.yml comment: replace 'and nothing else' with 'attached to the Chromium programs in that folder alone (like Ubuntu's own profiles for browsers, a program running as you can also enter it by name with aa-exec unless kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 1, so it adds no way round the restriction that Ubuntu's own profiles do not already give)'.
  - ADR-0105 :75 and :105: say the same, and narrow the home-folder rejection to what the root folder does gain (no profile on a path the person can write, which still holds where unconfined profile changes are restricted).
  - ADR-0105 :121: 'trusts the Node on the PATH and what is in the byway folder at that moment'.

**Combination checks that held**
- **Setting read before each load, with retire.** ready()'s `on` follows the browser the test page was drawn on, and proven is kept per browser.

- **PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH.** The engine runs in-process (startDevEngine calls createWorker), so the start-up check and the worker see the same environment. browsersFolderOf, the root-folder install hint and the AppArmor fix text all match the script and ci.test.ts.

**Blocking finding (WB-1).** I reproduced it against real Chromium. A late probe relaunches Chromium after checkRenderer has answered, which contradicts ADR-0105's "closed" and "within 15 s", and the test helper masks it.

**Not re-checked by hand.** Each builder's mutation logs. Instead, I checked that each claimed test exists and passes.

- **WB-1** (blocking, false-adr-statement / test-masks-behaviour), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:2397-2407 (checkRenderer's probe: on 'late' it calls p.pages.close() but ready() keeps running); packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts:166-171 (close() sets current = undefined, so a get() still in flight launches again) and 113-152; docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:65 (item 5: 'A probe that has not answered by then is closed') and :124 (Consequences: 'Each draws only byway's own test page, within 15 s'); packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts held() ('A second call (the renderer launching again after its close) fails at once')

  A probe that runs past its 15 s bound while its launch is still pending is not stopped. The check closes the probe's renderer and answers. The probe's ready() then gets its browser from find(), which resolves just before close() shuts that browser. loaded() calls take(), sees no current browser (close() cleared it), and launches a new Chromium with the probe's setting. It draws byway's test page there after the check has answered, and the browser closes only when that attempt's finally runs. With the setting on (the default), that new Chromium runs without the sandbox. So ADR-0105's 'closed' and 'within 15 s' are false in this case. The tests cannot see it, because held() makes every second launch reject at once, and the ADR-0105 tests assert only that the first browser opened no page. The only page drawn is byway's own test page, so the runtime impact is one extra Chromium that can live for up to Playwright's launch timeout plus 30 s.

  Evidence: I put a probe test in my clone (a copy is at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\check-whole\probe-relaunch.test.ts). It runs checkRenderer({ osSandbox: false }, { probeMs: 100 }) with a launch that delays only the probe's sandboxed launch by 400 ms, against real Chromium. Its log:
  - launch#1 sandbox=false
  - newContext on launch#1
  - launch#1 closed
  - launch#2 sandbox=true
  - answer {"osSandbox":false,"ready":true,"sandbox":"unknown"}
  - launch#3 sandbox=true afterAnswer=true
  - launch#2 closed afterAnswer=true
  - newContext on launch#3 afterAnswer=true
  - launch#3 closed afterAnswer=true
  With the setting on, the same path relaunches with osSandbox false.

  Suggested fix: Make the renderer's close final. In renderBrowsers, have close() set a closed flag. A get() after it, or one still waiting when it ran, then rejects with the shutdown reason and never calls launch. This also stops a load in flight at worker shutdown from relaunching. Add a render-browsers.test.ts case with stand-in browsers: close() while find() waits on a held launch; release it; take() rejects and launch was called once. In renderer-check.test.ts, let held()'s second launch succeed, or record it, and assert that the probe launched once (settings stays [false, true] or [true, false]). If the code is not changed, reword ADR-0105 item 5 and the Consequences bullet: the ADR is not on main yet.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim is real, and I reproduced it in my own clone of fix/fx-d2 at d082335. When a probe runs past probeMs while its launch is still pending, checkRenderer calls p.pages.close() and answers. close() in render-browsers.ts sets current = undefined and does not stop anything. When the slow launch arrives, find() in ready() returns its browser. loaded() then calls take(), which finds no current browser and launches a new Chromium with the probe's setting. It draws byway's test page there after the check has answered. That browser closes only when the attempt's finally calls close() a second time. With the setting on (the default), the extra Chromium runs without the sandbox.
  
  What limits it: the extra browser draws only byway's own test page, and that probe is meant to draw that page without the sandbox anyway. So ADR-0105's "draws nothing but byway's own test page" holds, and this is not a security hole. It needs a launch slower than 15 s, which is rare. The extra Chromium lives at most for Playwright's launch timeout (180 s by default) plus the test page's 30 s, plus the close.
  
  Why I still call it blocking: ADR-0105 is not on main yet. It promises that a late probe "is closed" (item 5) and that each probe draws "within 15 s" (Consequences), and the code does not keep either promise in this case. The test seam hides it on purpose. held()'s own comment, quoted in full: "A second call (the renderer launching again after its close) fails at once". The tests assert `settings` before releasing the launch, so the relaunch is never seen. The fix is three lines, smaller than the one the claim proposes.

  Evidence: My reproduction is packages/runner/test/zz-refute-wb1.test.ts, in my clone at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r2\refute-integration-WB-1\repo. It runs against real Chromium.
  
  1. Setting on, sandboxed launch failing with a plain error, probe launch delayed 1.5 s, probeMs 200:
     - launch#1 sandbox=true
     - launch#2 sandbox=false
     - answer {osSandbox:true, ready:false, sandbox:unknown}
     - launch#3 sandbox=false afterAnswer=true
     - launch#2 closed +1659ms
     - newContext on launch#3 (sandbox=false) afterAnswer=true
     - launch#3 closed +1811ms
     So an unsandboxed Chromium was launched after the answer and drew there.
  2. Setting off: the same pattern. launch#3 sandbox=true after the answer, with newContext on it.
  3. Stand-in browsers, no real Chromium: renderBrowsers with find() waiting on a held launch, then close(), then release, then take(). launches = [false, false]; the second browser stays connected.
  4. The existing tests hide it. I added `expect(settings).toEqual([true,false])` and `([false,true])` after `probe.closedOnceReleased()` in renderer-check.test.ts's two late-probe tests. Both fail 3 times out of 3 on the branch, with [true,false,false] and [false,true,true]. No time-based wait is needed: the relaunch happens within microtasks, long before the first browser's disconnect.
  
  Fix tried in the clone: a closed flag in render-browsers.ts, set first in close(), and `if (closed) throw new Error(shutdownReason);` just before `const launching`.
  - My repro tests no longer show launch#3. The late browser is closed as its launch arrives, and no page is drawn after the answer.
  - The two tightened assertions pass 3 times out of 3.
  - These all pass: pages, launch, renderer-check, cards, page-arbiter, voice, apps/web guard-sandbox and scripts serve tests (164 + 106 tests).
  - Only one assertion fails: render-browsers.test.ts:188-210, "the next load launches again" (line 207, `await browsers.take()`). That one is meant to flip.
  - The claim's stronger version also rejects a get() that was still waiting when close() ran. I tried it, and it also breaks `expect(await taking).toBe(late)` and loses ADR-0106's "byway closed the page renderer as it shut down" message.
  
  I did not modify the worktree, main or byway_review, made no paid calls and pushed nothing.

  Fix: 1. packages/runner/src/render-browsers.ts: add `let closed = false;`. Set `closed = true;` as the first line of close(). In get(), after the for-loop and before `const launching = ...`, add `if (closed) throw new Error(shutdownReason);`. This guards only the launch. A load still waiting on a launch when close() ran keeps getting that browser, which close() then closes, and fails with the reason, as ADR-0106 says. Do not reject those waiters as the claim suggests. Update the header comment and the doc comments of close() and take(): nothing is launched after close(). This also stops a ready() still in find() at worker shutdown from relaunching.
  
  2. packages/runner/test/render-browsers.test.ts:188-210: retitle the test (it currently ends "the next load launches again"). After close(), `browsers.take()` rejects with `${BYWAY_CLOSED} as it shut down` and launch is not called again. Add a stand-in case: find() waits on a held launch, close() runs, the launch is released, find() resolves to that browser and it is closed with SHUTDOWN, a following take() rejects, and launch was called once.
  
  3. packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts: in the two late-probe tests (on, about line 224; off, about line 264), assert `settings` again after `await probe.closedOnceReleased()`: `[true, false]` and `[false, true]`. This fails deterministically without the fix and passes with it, with no wall-clock wait. Reword held()'s comment: a second call would be a relaunch after the close, and the tests assert there is none.
  
  4. docs/adr/0105 item 5 (line 65) and the Consequences bullet (line 124): say that a late probe's renderer is closed and launches nothing more. A launch still on its way cannot be cut short; Playwright's launch timeout bounds it, and its browser is closed as it arrives, before any page. So any drawing happens within 15 s. Name the render-browsers test and the renderer-check assertions as the evidence. If the code is not changed, the two ADR sentences must instead be reworded to describe the relaunch.

- **WB-2** (non-blocking, guard-gap), at apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts (PINS); claim at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:42-43 ('The only writer of the setting is Store.setRendererSandbox … guard-sandbox.test.ts holds the product sources to that')

  Product code can write the setting's column directly and turn the sandbox off without naming any pinned identifier. The guard does not notice. Its 'does not see a use that names none of these' disclaimer covers this in general terms, but the most direct way round the store's only-writer rule is unpinned.

  Evidence: I added scripts/zz-sql.ts to my clone, containing `db.exec('UPDATE renderer_settings SET os_sandbox = 0 WHERE id = 1')`. `pnpm exec vitest run apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts` still passed 28 of 28. Only seven product lines name the table or column today:
  - packages/store/src/migrations.ts:291, 294, 296
  - packages/store/src/queries.ts:50, 52-54
  - packages/store/src/renderer.ts:46

  Suggested fix: Add a pin for /\bos_sandbox\b|\brenderer_settings\b/ with exactly those lines, and add the raw-SQL form to the self-test's list of ways round the guard.

- **WB-3** (non-blocking, ui-copy (Linux)), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:323-326 (installHint) used at 359-362 (launchFailure); README.md Renderer, OS sandbox Linux bullet

  On a Linux where AppArmor restricts user namespaces, Chromium can be missing outside /opt/byway/ms-playwright. For example, the person ran the script but PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH is not set in the shell byway starts in, because a line added to ~/.profile takes effect only at the next login. byway then says 'If Playwright's Chromium is not installed: pnpm --filter ./apps/web exec playwright install chromium'. The README says the opposite for Ubuntu 24.04 and later: run the script 'in place of Playwright's own install'. Following byway's advice installs Chromium into ~/.cache/ms-playwright, which the profile does not cover, so the next start fails with the AppArmor message. That message does name the export, so the person gets there in the end, one wrong step later.

  Evidence: installHint chooses the script only when browsers === ROOT_BROWSERS_FOLDER, and never looks at host.appArmorRestricts. For a missing Chromium, launchFailure returns `${couldNot} ${install}`, whatever the sandbox setting.

  Suggested fix: Pass the host to installHint. When host.os is 'linux', host.appArmorRestricts is true and the folder is not /opt/byway/ms-playwright, name the script and say that PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/byway/ms-playwright is not set in this shell. Add a launch.test.ts case for it.

5. **Gate in my clone** (logs in check-code\out\gate-*.log):

The only processes still running are three chrome-headless-shell processes from 2026-10-17, which are not mine. No node processes from my clone remain.

- **LNX-R3-1** (non-blocking), at scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:35 (folder="${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-$HOME/.cache}/ms-playwright"), :61 (the profile line uses $folder as typed), :65 (the success message)

  The profile is attached to the folder's path as typed, without resolving symlinks. AppArmor matches a profile against the path of the file actually executed, after symlinks are resolved (bprm->file->f_path in security/apparmor/domain.c). So if ~/.cache, $XDG_CACHE_HOME or $HOME is a symlink (for example ~/.cache moved to another disk or a tmpfs), the profile never attaches to Playwright's Chromium. The script still prints "Playwright's Chromium in &lt;folder> may start its OS sandbox now", which is then false. byway's AppArmor message then says to run the script again, which changes nothing.

  Evidence: In WSL Ubuntu, my clone's script ran with sudo stood in, the kernel files faked, and HOME/.cache a symlink to another folder (C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r3\check-code\wsl\symlink.sh and symlink.out). The script exited 0. It wrote `profile playwright-chromium /tmp/…/home/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell} …`, but `readlink -f` of that folder is /tmp/…/disk/cache/ms-playwright. It printed "… may start its OS sandbox now.". Playwright 1.63 builds the folder as `process.env.XDG_CACHE_HOME || path.join(os.homedir(), '.cache')` + ms-playwright (coreBundle.js:32664) and never resolves it, so the path Chromium is launched from keeps the symlink, and the kernel sees the resolved one. Not run on a kernel with AppArmor loaded, because WSL has none. This is a narrow configuration, and the README already says the steps have not been tried on a desktop.

  Suggested fix: After the full-path check, when the folder exists, resolve it with `real=$(cd "$folder" && pwd -P)`. Run the same character check on `real`, and write the profile on `real`, or on both paths, as two profiles or one alternation. Alternatively, refuse with a plain message when `real` differs from `folder`. Have ci.test.ts run that case with a symlinked XDG_CACHE_HOME in a temp folder: it exits before any sudo. If the folder does not exist yet, say to run Playwright's install first (the README's Renderer paragraph already gives it first).

- **DOC-R3-1** (blocking), at docs/backlog.md:139 (row "macOS support (deferred by the owner, 2026-10-16)")

Drop "Playwright's own install runs on every system but Linux". This is main's wording again, apart from the entry's form. Optionally pin it: have ci.test.ts check that the backlog's macOS entry equals the commented entry in verify.yml.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: The claim holds; I could not refute it. Round 3 (115e9db) put back the `playwright:` argument on every matrix entry, and the install step now runs on every system, Linux included. docs/backlog.md:139 still has round 2's text and is false on two points. First, it gives the CI entry as `{ name: macos, os: macos-15 }` and says this is "as the comment in verify.yml says", but the comment says `{ name: macos, os: macos-15, playwright: chromium }`. Second, it says "Playwright's own install runs on every system but Linux". The step at verify.yml:65-66 has no `if:` and runs `--with-deps chromium` on Linux. On main the row is correct ("macos-15, with `playwright install chromium`"), so merging this branch would put false text on main where today it is true. I count that as blocking for merge under this repo's documentation rules, though the fix is one line. One part of the claim is overstated. The platform-claim test (ci.test.ts:348-350) hard-codes "; macOS later", so it must be edited when macOS lands whatever form the entry takes. That failure does not come from the stale row. Following the row does cause something worse, shown below.

  Evidence: All of this ran in my own clone, C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r3\refute-docs-DOC-R3-1\repo, on branch fix/fx-d2 at 115e9db, after an offline pnpm install. I restored the clone afterwards and it is clean.
  1. verify.yml:40 reads `#   - { name: macos, os: macos-15, playwright: chromium }`. Lines 65-66 hold the install step, `run: pnpm --filter ./apps/web exec playwright install ${{ matrix.playwright }}`, with no `if:`. Line 43 is the Linux entry, with `playwright: --with-deps chromium`.

The row matched that workflow. `git diff d082335..HEAD -- docs/backlog.md` changes only the bump-policy, C5, "What D2 left" and late-close rows; the macOS row is unchanged.

YAML reads three entries, the macOS one with no `playwright` key. scripts/test/ci.test.ts still passed all 54 tests. Its matrix regex at line 20 requires `playwright:`, so it skips the new entry. As a result:
     - the "one job per OS: Windows and Linux" toEqual still passes with three jobs;
     - the README checks never require macos-15 in the CI section or a macOS install command;
     - the README can keep saying "Windows and Linux; macOS later" while a macOS job runs.

  5. On the claim's point about ci.test.ts:348: line 350 checks for `${systems.join(' and ')}; macOS later`. Adding macOS in any form means editing that test, so its failure is not caused by the stale row.

In docs/backlog.md:139, replace "one more CI matrix entry (`{ name: macos, os: macos-15 }`, as the comment in verify.yml says; Playwright's own install runs on every system but Linux)" with "one more CI matrix entry (`{ name: macos, os: macos-15, playwright: chromium }`, as the comment in verify.yml says)". If the install should still be described, say instead that Playwright's own install runs on every system, with `--with-deps` on Linux only.

In scripts/test/ci.test.ts, read the matrix from the parsed YAML (`parse(workflow).jobs.verify.strategy.matrix.include`) instead of the line regex. Then check that every entry has a `name`, an `os` and a non-empty `playwright` string. An entry without `playwright` should fail the check rather than be skipped.
