It would also require the row not to say "every system but Linux". On the branch as it is, this check fails, as step 3 of the evidence shows. After fix 1 it would pass.

- **DOC-R3-2** (blocking), at README.md:158 ("The alternative is to leave the restriction whole and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings"); scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:14-15 (header, same sentence) and :72 (printed: "To keep the restriction whole instead, remove the profile …"); docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:77 (item 8: "The alternative it names is to leave the restriction whole")

  The README and the script say that the sandbox-off alternative keeps Ubuntu's restriction "whole". The README tells the person to make this choice for each machine. On a default Ubuntu 24.04 the restriction is not whole without byway's profile. Ubuntu's own profiles that are unconfined and allow user namespaces are loaded whether or not their programs are installed, and they can be entered by name with aa-exec while kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, which is 24.04's default. So the wording overstates what switching byway's sandbox off protects, in the very trade-off the owner asked to be stated plainly ("honest wording … the alternative is to switch byway's sandbox off"). A person could give up Chromium's sandbox believing this keeps the machine fully restricted.

  Evidence: - The project's own text already says the restriction is open without byway's profile:
    - README.md:158 says that on 24.04 a program "can enter the profile by name with aa-exec" and that "Ubuntu's own profile for Google Chrome can be used in the first two ways too".
    - ADR-0105:122 says "On a machine with no such program that sets kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined to 1, this profile can be the only way round the restriction". Read the other way: on a default 24.04 it is not the only way.
  - Round 2's confirmed LNX-1 evidence (d2-r2-result.json) cites Qualys's March 2025 advisory: `aa-exec -p trinity -- unshare -U -r -m /bin/sh` works on Ubuntu 24.04. Trinity is a syscall fuzzer that is not installed by default, so these profiles are loaded whatever is installed. Ubuntu's chrome profile has the same shape.
  - ADR-0105 Evidence, round 2's bullet, relies on that advisory.
  - ADR-0103's later note corrects the same kind of overstatement ("the rest of the machine stays restricted").
  - The phrase "restriction whole" is new in 115e9db, and ci.test.ts:171 does not forbid it.

  Suggested fix: Say what the alternative actually keeps.
  - README and script header: "The alternative is to add no profile and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which has its own risk (…). The restriction then stays as Ubuntu set it; on Ubuntu 24.04, while kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, Ubuntu's own profiles, such as Google Chrome's, already let a program running as you get round it with aa-exec."
  - Script's last message: "To add nothing to the restriction instead, remove the profile (…) and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which …"
  - ADR-0105 item 8: the same words.
  - Add /restriction whole/ to the phrases ci.test.ts:171 forbids.
  - Then run pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test apps/web/test/guard-.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, blocking: yes.

  Reason: I could not refute it. On a default Ubuntu 24.04 the restriction is not "whole" without byway's profile. Ubuntu's apparmor package ships about a hundred profiles marked unconfined that allow user namespaces, chrome and trinity among them. They are loaded whether or not those programs are installed. While kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, which is still noble's default, any program running as the user can enter one of them with aa-exec. That means byway's profile opens no new route on a default 24.04.
  
  The README's earlier sentence ("Ubuntu's own profile for Google Chrome can be used in the first two ways too") does not save the wording. A reader takes it to mean "only if I have Chrome", because the LD_PRELOAD route does need Chrome installed. The next sentence then says that switching byway's sandbox off would "leave the restriction whole". This is the same kind of overstatement round 2 blocked on ("the rest of the machine stays restricted"), seen from the other side. It sits in the sentence the owner asked to be honest, and it pushes a careful person to give up a real protection (Chromium's sandbox) to keep one that a default 24.04 does not provide.
  
  ADR-0105 contradicts itself here. Item 8 says "leave the restriction whole", while its residual bullet (line 122) says the profile can be the only way round only when the setting is 1.
  
  My best case for "not blocking" is to read "whole" as "not changed by byway". That reading is too weak for the trade-off text the owner asked for. It is a cheap wording fix, but under this round's own goal it blocks.

  Evidence: My clone is at C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r3\refute-docs-DOC-R3-2\repo (fix/fx-d2 at 115e9db). I modified nothing.
  
  1. The text, as the claim quotes it:
     - README.md:158: "The alternative is to leave the restriction whole and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings".
     - scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:14-15 (header) and :72 (printed): "To keep the restriction whole instead, remove the profile ...".
     - docs/adr/0105-...md:77.
     - `git log -S"restriction whole"` shows the phrase first appeared in 115e9db.
     - The forbidden-phrase regex at scripts/test/ci.test.ts:171 does not catch it.
  
  2. Ubuntu's profiles, checked read-only in WSL Ubuntu 26.04 (apparmor 5.0.0~beta1):
     - /etc/apparmor.d/chrome (`profile chrome /opt/google/chrome/chrome flags=(unconfined) { userns, ...}`) and /etc/apparmor.d/trinity have the same shape.
     - `dpkg -S` says both files and /usr/bin/aa-exec belong to the apparmor package itself.
     - /opt/google does not exist there, so these profiles ship and load whether or not Chrome or trinity is installed.
     - WSL's kernel has AppArmor off (/sys/module/apparmor/parameters/enabled = N), so I could not run the aa-exec bypass live.
  
  3. Qualys's March 2025 advisory (fetched) shows `aa-exec -p trinity -- unshare -U -r -m /bin/sh` working on Ubuntu 24.04. It names chrome, flatpak and trinity, and says aa-exec is installed by default.
  
  4. The noble (24.04) apparmor changelog on Launchpad, up to 0ubuntu0.24.04.9 of 2026-09-18, never sets kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined=1. Noble's only Qualys-related change removed the busybox and nautilus profiles (24.04.6, 2026-02). So aa-exec into the remaining profiles is still the default way round on 24.04.
  
  5. Ubuntu 25.04 and later differ. The plucky apparmor changelog (2025-02-11) says "debian/usr/lib/sysctl.d/10-apparmor.conf: Set sysctl kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined=1", and WSL 26.04's /usr/lib/sysctl.d/10-apparmor.conf sets both settings to 1. There, without byway's profile, the restriction is closer to whole: what remains is LD_PRELOAD into an installed program that has such a profile.
  
  6. ADR-0105:122 says: "On a machine with no such program that sets kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined to 1, this profile can be the only way round the restriction". That implies it is not the only way on a default 24.04, which contradicts item 8.
  
  7. The owner's words, quoted in ADR-0105:34, name the alternative as "switch byway's sandbox off". The "restriction whole" gloss was added beyond them.

  Fix: Keep the owner's alternative (switch the sandbox off). Remove "whole", and say what the restriction is without byway's profile, release by release. The words the ci.test.ts:137-144 pins require must stay in both the header and the printed text: "switch byway's sandbox off in Settings", "aa-exec", "LD_PRELOAD", "kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0", and /any program running as you can (then|now) create user namespaces through this profile/.
  
  1. README.md:158. Replace from "The alternative is to leave the restriction whole ..." to the end of that sentence with: "The alternative is to add no profile and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which has its own risk (above): a malicious page or image, together with a bug in Chromium, could then run as you. Without byway's profile the restriction is as Ubuntu set it, and on Ubuntu 24.04 that is not whole either: Ubuntu loads its own profiles of this kind, Google Chrome's among them, whether or not those programs are installed, and while `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined` is 0 any program running as you can enter one with `aa-exec`. (Ubuntu 25.04 and later set it to 1, which closes that way for Ubuntu's profiles and byway's alike.)" Keep "Choose for each machine."
     - In the sentence before, say that the aa-exec way works whether or not Chrome is installed, e.g. "Ubuntu's own profile for Google Chrome can be used in the first two ways too (with `aa-exec`, whether or not Chrome is installed)".
     - The 25.04 sentence is optional. Its evidence is the plucky apparmor changelog of 2025-02-11 and 26.04's /usr/lib/sysctl.d/10-apparmor.conf. If you add it, cite it in ADR-0105's Evidence.
  
  2. scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:14-16 (header). Use the same words as the README.
  
  3. scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:72-74 (printed). Replace with: "To take the profile away instead, remove it (sudo apparmor_parser --remove $profile, then sudo rm $profile) and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which has its own risk: a malicious page or image, together with a bug in Chromium, could then run as you. The restriction is then as Ubuntu set it: while kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0 (printed above), Ubuntu's own profiles, loaded whether or not their programs are installed, already let a program running as you round it with aa-exec."
  
  4. docs/adr/0105-...md:77 (item 8). Replace "The alternative it names is to leave the restriction whole and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which has its own risk" with "The alternative it names is to add no profile and switch byway's sandbox off in Settings, which has its own risk, and it says that the restriction is then as Ubuntu set it, which on Ubuntu 24.04, while kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined is 0, Ubuntu's own profiles (loaded whether or not their programs are installed) already open to aa-exec". This makes item 8 agree with line 122.
  
  5. scripts/test/ci.test.ts:171. Add `|restriction whole` to the forbidden regex. Its file list does not include ADRs, so ADR-0105 must be fixed by hand. Today the phrase occurs only in the README, the script and ADR-0105, so nothing else trips. Optionally pin that the README and the script both contain "whether or not".
  
  6. Then run `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test apps/web/test/guard-`. On Windows the script's refusals run only when Git's sh is on the PATH.
  
  Make no paid calls, do not push, and leave main and byway_review untouched.

- **DOC-R3-3** (non-blocking), at README.md:156 ("full Chromium, any version" … "A Playwright upgrade needs nothing more"); scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:4-8 ("any version", "the profile covers every version in that folder"); docs/adr/0105-…md:75 (item 8); docs/backlog.md:54 (bump-policy row, which no longer mentions Linux)

  The profile's pattern `chromium*/chrome-*/chrome{,-headless-shell}` covers Chromium only as Playwright 1.63 lays out its folders. "Any version" and "a Playwright upgrade needs nothing more" are promised without that condition. Earlier Playwright releases put the headless shell at chromium_headless_shell-&lt;rev>/chrome-linux/headless_shell, which the pattern does not match. A future upgrade that changes the layout would leave byway's Chromium outside the profile. The AppArmor message would then point to the same script, which writes the same pattern.

  Evidence: - Playwright 1.63's registry (node_modules/.pnpm/playwright-core@1.63.0/…/lib/coreBundle.js, EXECUTABLE_PATHS) names `chrome-linux64/chrome` and `chrome-headless-shell-linux64/chrome-headless-shell`.
  - The same file still passes `["chrome-linux"]` to `_validateHostRequirements`, the older folder name.
  - The well-known pre-CfT path was `chromium_headless_shell-<rev>/chrome-linux/headless_shell`.
  - CI's Linux job would show such a change, because the renderer tests run with the sandbox on. But no doc says that a Playwright bump should check this, and round 3 removed the only Linux line from the bump-policy row.

  Suggested fix: Limit the claim: "any version laid out as Playwright 1.63 lays it out". Add to the bump-policy row: "on Linux, CI's renderer tests with the sandbox on show whether the script's pattern still covers the new Chromium; if not, change the pattern in the script".

Yet a3d271f rewrote three passages of Decision item 8 (:75-77), because of DOC-R3-2 (blocking) and DOC-R3-3, and gave no provenance for it. The scratchpad report d2-r3-result.json exists and confirms those four IDs, its head being 115e9db. Run 39172144829 at a3d271f has now finished, both jobs green: Linux done 22:39:44Z, Windows 23:05:26Z. Linux printed userns = 1 and unconfined = 0, added the profile on /home/runner/.cache/ms-playwright, and passed 198 files (4,997 tests) and 55 browser tests, with the line "Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox". Windows passed the same with the same line. This is the only CI run of the script as merged (a34568f changed only the script's opening comment and ci.test.ts). The record's latest CI evidence is for d387960's root-folder script, which is now an Alternative. So a reader checking ADR-0103 §3's gate ("merges only when both jobs pass") against the record cannot find a run of the shipped script.
  
  Parts of the finding I weaken: (1) The ci.test.ts:169 comment is not false. "them" refers to the two claims in the test name, and ADR-0105 and ADR-0103's later note (:166) do quote those. The test name and item 8 (:80) undercount the regex, which since a3d271f/a34568f also bans "restriction whole", "every version in that folder" and "Chromium, any version". They are incomplete, not false. (2) a34568f did not touch ADR-0105, so "why item 8 changed in … a34568f" is wrong. (3) The proposed bullet blames LNX-R3-1 for "did not stop when Chromium was not installed yet". LNX-R3-1 was only about the symbolic link. The missing-folder stop came with the fix, because `pwd -P` needs the folder. (4) The proposed insertion point ("before 'Round 3's tests for the script'") would cut that paragraph off from the round-2 bullet it belongs to. Append at the end instead. (5) No test runs the missing-folder or symbolic-link paths (git grep), so the new bullet should not imply one does. It is minor because no behaviour is wrong and item 8 as it now reads is accurate. Only the provenance and the record of the gate run are missing.

- **leftovers:SWP-2** (minor, leftovers), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:70; apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:12; apps/web/test/renderer.test.ts:105

  ADR-0105 item 6 says "Turning it off asks first, with the risk in the owner's words: \"With the sandbox off, a malicious page or image, together with a bug in Chromium, could run as you …\"". renderer.ts:12 says "/** What turning the sandbox off risks, said before it is turned off (the owner's words, 2026-10-16). */", and renderer.test.ts:105 is named 'asks with the risk in plain words, as the owner put it'.

  Why: These words are not the owner's. ADR-0105's own Context (:23) says "The coordinator proposed: '… Turning it off asks you to confirm and explains the risk plainly: a malicious page or image plus a browser bug could then run as you, next to your API keys and spending records …'", and the owner's words were only "go with revised design but only Windows and Linux for now. MacOS later …". The shipped sentence also rewords the proposal. Round 1's DOC-3 corrected this same kind of slip in this record (Claude's choices marked as the owner's).

  Suggested fix: ADR-0105:70: '… with the risk as the design the owner approved put it (the coordinator's proposal, item 3, under Context): "With the sandbox off, …"'. renderer.ts:12: '/** What turning the sandbox off risks, said before it is turned off (from the design the owner approved on 2026-10-16; ADR-0105). */'. renderer.test.ts:105: "asks with the risk in plain words, as the design the owner approved put it".

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: Confirmed at fix/fx-d2 (a34568f). Three places say the risk sentence is the owner's words:
  - ADR-0105:70: "Turning it off asks first, with the risk in the owner's words: "With the sandbox off, a malicious page or image, together with a bug in Chromium, could run as you on this computer, …""
  - apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:12: "(the owner's words, 2026-10-16)"
  - apps/web/test/renderer.test.ts:105: it('asks with the risk in plain words, as the owner put it')
  
  The words are not the owner's.
  - **What the owner wrote.** In the session transcript (C:\Users\arvik\.claude\projects\C--Users-arvik-code-byway\67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422.jsonl), the owner's input on this point was a question at line 15021 (2026-10-16T16:17Z): "And tell them of the risks too (if any)?" The owner did not supply any wording.
  - **Who first wrote the risk.** Claude wrote it, in the assistant message at line 15093 (16:20Z), under "Design that follows from your requirements", item 3: "a malicious page or image plus a browser bug could then run as you, next to your API keys and spending records."
  - **What the owner approved.** At line 15331 the owner answered "go with revised design but only Windows and Linux for now. MacOS later …". The question the owner was answering there only said the switch "explains the risk", with no wording.
  - **The ADR agrees.** ADR-0105's own Context (:23) gives that sentence as the coordinator's proposal, and says the owner only answered "go with revised design …".
  - **The shipped text differs again.** It adds "together with a bug in Chromium", "on this computer" and the banner sentence, so it is not even the proposal word for word.
  
  Why it is not a false alarm:
  - The phrase appears only in these three places on the branch, so nothing else already corrects it.
  - No test or guard checks the ADR text or the test name.
  - It is the same kind of slip round 1's DOC-3 corrected in this record ("Claude's choices marked as the owner's"), against the owner's rule that ADRs record who decided what honestly.
  
  Why minor, not blocking: the owner did ask for the risk to be explained and did approve a design that included it, and the app's behaviour and copy are right. Only who wrote the words is wrong. A reader might take the copy as the owner's own wording and not change it without asking the owner.
  
  The Context/Decision edit is allowed under ADR-0103: ADR-0105 does not exist on main or origin/main yet, so it is new in this merge.

- **leftovers:SWP-4** (minor, leftovers), at scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:32

  echo "Run this as yourself, not as root: byway does not run as root, and the profile names your own folder." >&2

  Why: byway does run as root: nothing in scripts/serve.ts or the app refuses user id 0. Its own message for that case, sandbox.ts:196, says "byway is running as root, and Chromium does not start its sandbox for root." The README (:161) says the same: Chromium will not start its sandbox for root. Only the sandbox needs an ordinary user. So the script tells a person who runs it as root something untrue, and contradicts the message the app gives them.

  Suggested fix: echo "Run this as yourself, not as root: the profile names your own folder, and Chromium does not start its sandbox for root anyway (start byway as an ordinary user)." >&2

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: Confirmed at a34568f. scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:32 says "byway does not run as root", and nothing on the branch backs that up. scripts/serve.ts and the packages never check for user id 0 or refuse it; the only getuid calls are in dev-engine.ts and temporal-cli.ts, for file-trust checks, and in sandbox.ts, for diagnosis. sandbox.ts treats running as root as a supported state: it sets host.root = probe.uid === 0 (line 86) and gives the advice at :196, "byway is running as root, and Chromium does not start its sandbox for root." / "Start byway as an ordinary user." README:161 says the same, and so do ADR-0105:19, :55 and :123 ("Root and containers cannot draw pages with the sandbox on"). As root, then, byway starts, and with the Settings switch off it draws pages too. Only the sandbox needs an ordinary user, so the script's reason is false as stated and contradicts the app's own message. The wording is not a deliberate quote or a leftover of a reversed design: it has been there since round 1 (f6f978e, "...and the profile names your home folder"), and nothing elsewhere has fixed it. No test pins the text: ci.test.ts runs only the PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH, space and relative-path refusals, and skips its run block when uid is 0. guard-sandbox.test.ts scans only .ts/.js files. The new text also matches none of the phrases ci.test.ts forbids. Minor, not blocking: the refusal still sends the person to run as an ordinary user, which is the right action. The error is in the stated reason, which could make someone think byway refuses root.

  Fix: File: scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh, line 32.
  Old: echo "Run this as yourself, not as root: byway does not run as root, and the profile names your own folder." >&2
  New: echo "Run this as yourself, not as root: the profile names your own folder, and Chromium does not start its sandbox for root anyway (start byway as an ordinary user)." >&2
  The new wording matches sandbox.ts:196 and README:161. No test or other file needs to change.

The branch updated the other places that described D2 as pending:

So it disagrees with the status page and with line 181 in the same file. It is not literally false: the condition is a suggestion that has now been satisfied. - **consistency:SWEEP-1** (blocking, consistency), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:70 (Decision item 6); also apps/web/src/view/renderer.ts:12, apps/web/test/renderer.test.ts:105, apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:1-2

  ADR-0105:70: "Turning it off asks first, with the risk in the owner's words: "With the sandbox off, a malicious page or image, together with a bug in Chromium, could run as you on this computer, next to your API keys and spending records. byway shows a banner on every screen while it is off."". renderer.ts:12: "What turning the sandbox off risks, said before it is turned off (the owner's words, 2026-10-16)." renderer.test.ts:105: it('asks with the risk in plain words, as the owner put it'). guard-sandbox.test.ts:1-2: "(ADR-0105, the owner's "Never automatic: byway never turns the sandbox off by itself")".

  Why: These are not the owner's words. The risk sentence first appears in the coordinator's message of 2026-10-16T16:20:14Z in the session transcript ("Turning it off asks you to confirm and explains the risk plainly: a malicious page or image plus a browser bug could then run as you, next to your API keys and spending records."). ADR-0105's own Context quotes that text as "The coordinator proposed". The owner replied only "go with revised design but only Windows and Linux for now. MacOS later …". Before that, the owner had only asked "And tell them of the risks too (if any)?". The string quoted at line 70 is also not verbatim from anyone: "together with a bug in Chromium", "on this computer" and the banner sentence are new. "Never automatic" is likewise point 5 of the coordinator's proposal, which the owner approved. This is the same kind of error as round 1's DOC-3 (Claude's choices marked as the owner's). Item 6 is Decision text, so after the --no-ff merge it is fixed for good under ADR-0103 §1.

  Suggested fix: ADR-0105:70: replace "with the risk in the owner's words:" with "with the risk the design the owner approved on 2026-10-16 names (the coordinator's words, quoted under Context), said in the app as:". renderer.ts:12: replace "(the owner's words, 2026-10-16)" with "(the risk the design the owner approved on 2026-10-16 names; ADR-0105)". renderer.test.ts:105: rename to 'asks with the risk in plain words, as the approved design names it'. guard-sandbox.test.ts:1-2: replace "the owner's "Never automatic: …"" with "the design the owner approved: "Never automatic: …"". Optionally also ADR-0105:95: "The owner asked for the switch in Settings" → "The owner approved the switch in Settings (the coordinator's design)".

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: blocking.

  Reason: The finding is real. I checked it against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f and against the session transcript (C:\Users\arvik\.claude\projects\C--Users-arvik-code-byway\67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422.jsonl).
  
  1. The owner did not write the risk text. Every message the owner typed on D2 is in the transcript:
     - 2026-10-16T16:17:34Z: the owner asked for the risk to be told, without wording it ("And tell them of the risks too (if any)?").
     - 2026-10-17T02:53:54Z (16 Oct, local time): the owner approved the design ("go with revised design but only Windows and Linux for now. MacOS later …").
     - No message the owner typed in any transcript in that folder contains "together with a bug in Chromium", "plus a browser bug" or "Never automatic". I searched them all.
  
  2. The risk sentence is the coordinator's (Claude's). It is point 3 of the coordinator's message at 2026-10-16T16:20:14Z, and "Never automatic" is point 5 of the same message.
  
  3. ADR-0105 contradicts itself. Its Context (line 23) correctly says "The coordinator proposed: … explains the risk plainly: a malicious page or image plus a browser bug could then run as you, next to your API keys and spending records." Item 6 (line 70) then calls a different, longer string "the owner's words". That string is not word for word from anyone: "together with a bug in Chromium", "on this computer" and the banner sentence are new.
  
  4. The same claim is repeated in three other places, all present at a34568f:
     - renderer.ts:12: "(the owner's words, 2026-10-16)"
     - renderer.test.ts:105: "as the owner put it"
     - guard-sandbox.test.ts:2: the owner's "Never automatic: …"
  
  5. Round 1's DOC-3 was this kind of error (Claude's choices marked as the owner's). Commit 1dc0249 fixed it under "Who decided what" but missed these lines. They predate round 1.
  
  Why blocking, not minor:
  - No behaviour is wrong. But under the task's rule a reader would be misled about who decided and who wrote the words. In byway, "the owner's words" tells a maintainer the copy may not be changed without the owner (compare ADR-0081).
  - Item 6 is Decision text. ADR-0105 is not on main yet (`git ls-tree main docs/adr` shows only 0103 and 0104), so it can still be edited in place, as 1dc0249 did. After the --no-ff merge, ADR-0103 §1 forbids that edit, and only a later note could correct it.
  - No test pins any of these strings, so the fix touches no test assertions.
  
  Partial mitigations, which do not remove the finding:
  - The owner did ask for the risk to be told.
  - The owner did approve point 5 ("Never automatic"), so line 96, "The owner ruled it out", is defensible.
  - Line 95, "The owner asked for the switch in Settings", is loose rather than false: the owner asked for a switch that cannot be forgotten; putting it in Settings was the coordinator's proposal.

  Fix: Edit on fix/fx-d2 before the merge. ADR-0105 is not on main yet, so it can still be edited in place.

     NEW: it('asks with the risk in plain words, as the design the owner approved names it', () => {

- **consistency:SWEEP-2** (blocking, consistency), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:80 (Decision item 8) and :154 (Evidence, round 3's tests); scripts/test/ci.test.ts:153-166; scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh:69-79

  ADR-0105:80: "`scripts/test/ci.test.ts` holds the script, the workflow and the README to this: one profile on that folder, the two commands as root and no other, the refusals, which it runs, and the plain statement at the top and at the end". ADR-0105:154: "the refusals, run with Git's `sh` on Windows and with `sh` on Linux". Item 8's list of refusals (line 76) includes "when that folder … does not exist yet, and when its path, or the path a link leads to, has a space or a character AppArmor reads as a pattern".

  Why: ci.test.ts runs only three refusals: PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH set (line 156), a space in the folder's path (159) and a relative path (162). Commit a3d271f added two refusals to the script: the folder that "does not exist yet" (lines 70-73) and the link-target check (`pwd -P` then `plain_path "$real"`, lines 74-79). a3d271f changed ci.test.ts by one forbidden-phrase regex only. A grep of scripts/test finds no "does not exist yet", "leads to" or "pwd -P". So neither new refusal, and none of the symbolic-link handling, is run or pinned. On Windows the new refusals cannot be reached at all: Git's sh has no /proc/sys/kernel/apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns, so the script exits 0 at line 59-62, before line 70. Item 8 is Decision text, fixed once merged.

  Suggested fix: Either test them or say less. To test: in ci.test.ts, when /proc/sys/kernel/apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns is readable (CI's Linux), run the script (1) with XDG_CACHE_HOME pointing at a temp folder with no ms-playwright: expect status 1 and stderr "does not exist yet: install Playwright's Chromium first"; (2) with &lt;tmp>/cache/ms-playwright a symbolic link to &lt;tmp>/a b/ms-playwright: expect status 1, stdout "leads to", stderr "has a space or one of". Both stop before sudo. Never exercise the success path: CI's sudo is passwordless and would overwrite /etc/apparmor.d/playwright-chromium. Otherwise reword line 80 to "the refusals of a moved folder, a folder that is not a full path and a path with a space or a pattern character, which it runs (not the folder that does not exist yet, nor a link's target)" and line 154 to match, before the merge.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: blocking.

  Reason: The finding is real. I checked it against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f.
  
  **What the ADR says.** ADR-0105:76 (Decision item 8) lists these refusals:
  - PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH is set;
  - the folder is not a full path;
  - the folder "does not exist yet";
  - the folder's path, "or the path a link leads to", has a space or a pattern character.
  
  Line 80 then says `scripts/test/ci.test.ts` holds the script to "the refusals, which it runs".
  
  **What ci.test.ts runs.** At :155-164 it runs only three: a moved folder (/elsewhere), a space in the path (/home/a b/.cache) and a relative path (cache). Nothing on the branch tests the other two:
  - `git grep` finds "does not exist yet", "leads to" and "pwd -P" only in the script itself.
  - a3d271f added both refusals to the script (:69-79) and widened item 8's list. Its only change to ci.test.ts was one forbidden-phrase regex.
  - At 115e9db the script had just those three refusals, plus the root check. So "which it runs" was true when it was written and became false at a3d271f.
  
  **Windows cannot reach them.** Git's sh has no /proc/sys/kernel/apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns, so the script exits 0 at :59-62, before :70. Only a Linux with that setting, such as CI's Ubuntu 24.04, gets that far.
  
  **The script itself works.** I ran a copy in WSL Ubuntu with the setting file faked:
  - XDG_CACHE_HOME at an empty temp folder: status 1, stderr "does not exist yet: install Playwright's Chromium first".
  - cache/ms-playwright as a link to "&lt;tmp>/a b/ms-playwright": status 1, stdout "leads to …", stderr "has a space or one of".
  
  So the script behaves correctly. What is false is the ADR's claim that ci.test.ts runs these refusals: nothing guards them, and removing either would not fail any test.
  
  **Why blocking.** This is item 8's Decision text. Once merged, ADR-0103 forbids editing it on main. A maintainer reading it would believe CI guards the symbolic-link handling and the missing-folder stop, and it does not.
  
  **Line 154 is less serious.** It is in Evidence, written at 115e9db when "the refusals" meant the three that existed then. It is historically accurate; aligning it is optional.

  Fix: Use one option, before the merge.
  
  **Option A (recommended): test the two refusals, so item 8 stays true.**
  
  1. In `scripts/test/ci.test.ts:7` replace
  `import { existsSync, realpathSync } from 'node:fs';`
  with
  `import { existsSync, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, realpathSync, rmSync, symlinkSync } from 'node:fs';`
  
  2. In `scripts/test/ci.test.ts`, after line 164, `      expect(relative.stderr).toContain("Playwright's folder, cache/ms-playwright, is not a full path");`, and still inside the `if` block, insert:
  ```
        // Past the restriction setting, which only a Linux with AppArmor's restriction has (CI's Ubuntu 24.04): a folder that
        // does not exist yet, and a link to a path with a space. Both stop before sudo; the success path is never run here.
        if (existsSync('/proc/sys/kernel/apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns')) {
          const tmp = realpathSync(mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'byway-apparmor-')));
          try {
            const missing = run({ XDG_CACHE_HOME: path.join(tmp, 'empty') });
            expect(missing.status).toBe(1);
            expect(missing.stderr).toContain("does not exist yet: install Playwright's Chromium first");
            const target = path.join(tmp, 'a b', 'ms-playwright');
            mkdirSync(target, { recursive: true });
            mkdirSync(path.join(tmp, 'cache'));
            symlinkSync(target, path.join(tmp, 'cache', 'ms-playwright'));
            const linked = run({ XDG_CACHE_HOME: path.join(tmp, 'cache') });
            expect(linked.status).toBe(1);
            expect(linked.stdout).toContain(`leads to ${target}`);
            expect(linked.stderr).toContain(`Playwright's folder, ${target}, has a space or one of`);
          } finally {
            rmSync(tmp, { recursive: true, force: true });
          }
        }
  ```
  Never let the test reach the success path: CI's passwordless sudo would overwrite /etc/apparmor.d/playwright-chromium.
  
  **Option B: reword only.**
  
  1. In `docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:80` replace
  `the two commands as root and no other, the refusals, which it runs, and the plain statement at the top and at the end;`
  with
  `the two commands as root and no other, the refusals of a moved folder, a folder that is not a full path and a path with a space or a pattern character, which it runs (not the folder that does not exist yet, nor a link's target, which the script reaches only where the kernel has the restriction setting), and the plain statement at the top and at the end;`
  
  2. Optionally, at line 154 replace
  `the refusals, run with Git's \`sh\` on Windows and with \`sh\` on Linux;`
  with
  `the refusals of a moved folder, a relative folder and a path with a space, run with Git's \`sh\` on Windows and with \`sh\` on Linux;`

- **consistency:SWEEP-3** (blocking, consistency), at apps/web/README.md:161-164

  "It does not see a use that names none of these (SQL that builds the table's or the column's name from pieces, say), a browser driver it does not list, a browser started as a program of its own, or a change inside the lines it pins; the renderer's own tests check those."

  Why: This credits the renderer's tests with covering all four of the guard's blind spots. ADR-0105:49 and the guard's own header (apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:21-25) say they cover only the last: "a change to the launch wiring inside the lines it pins (a retry without the sandbox inside pages.ts): launch.test.ts, pages.test.ts and renderer-check.test.ts check that behaviour". No renderer test looks for SQL elsewhere that builds the table's or column's name, another browser driver, or a browser started as a program. This is the class round 1 fixed elsewhere (DOC-1/W2, the guard described beyond what it checks). The sentence survived 1dc0249's rewrite, which added the extra blind spots in front of the old "those". The README also leaves out "a file outside those it reads", which ADR-0105:49 lists.

  Suggested fix: Replace with: "It does not see a use that names none of these (SQL that builds the table's or the column's name from pieces, say), a browser driver it does not list, a browser started as a program of its own, a file outside those it reads, or a change inside the lines it pins. The renderer's own tests (launch.test.ts, pages.test.ts, renderer-check.test.ts) check the last of these; nothing checks the others."

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: blocking.

  Reason: Confirmed on fix/fx-d2 (a34568f). apps/web/README.md:161-164 lists four things the guard does not see (SQL that builds the table's or column's name from pieces, a browser driver it does not list, a browser started as a program of its own, a change inside the lines it pins) and then ends with "the renderer's own tests check those". "Those" is plural, so it covers all four. Two other places say the tests cover only the last one:
  - **ADR-0105:49** names only the retry without the sandbox in pages.ts: "such as a retry without the sandbox in `pages.ts`; `launch.test.ts`, `pages.test.ts` and `renderer-check.test.ts` check that behaviour".
  - **guard-sandbox.test.ts:21-25**, the guard's own header, says the same: "a change to the launch wiring inside the lines it pins (...): launch.test.ts, pages.test.ts and renderer-check.test.ts check that behaviour".
  
  Those three tests are in packages/runner/test and only test the runner's launch behaviour. I searched every *.test.ts and all the other guards (guard-*, scripts/test) for renderer_settings/os_sandbox, puppeteer/selenium/webdriver, and child_process or spawn used to start a browser. Nothing checks SQL outside the store, an unlisted driver, or a browser started as a program. The only renderer_settings test is packages/store/test/renderer-settings.test.ts, which covers the store package alone.
  
  History: the claim began in 6c77426 ("It does not see a use that names none of these, or a change inside the lines it pins; the renderer's own tests check those."). It was already too broad then. 1dc0249 and later commits added more blind spots in front of the same "those", so the claim grew.
  
  Round 1 fixed this same class of error (DOC-1/W2, "the guard described beyond what it checks"; ADR-0105:142 says it was corrected), but this sentence was missed. A README reader is told that a security guard's gaps are covered by tests when they are not, so I rate it blocking under the task's rule.
  
  Secondary, not wrong on its own: the README leaves out "a file outside those it reads", which ADR-0105:49 lists. No test pins the README wording; I grepped for the phrases, so the fix will not break a test.

  Fix: File: apps/web/README.md, lines 161-164 (the lines stay under 120 characters, as the README wraps).
  
  OLD:
  ```
    checks itself against each way round it that reviews have found, each of which must change one of its lists. It does
    not see a use that names none of these (SQL that builds the table's or the column's name from pieces, say), a browser
    driver it does not list, a browser started as a program of its own, or a change inside the lines it pins; the
    renderer's own tests check those.
  ```
  
  NEW:
  ```
    checks itself against each way round it that reviews have found, each of which must change one of its lists. It does
    not see a use that names none of these (SQL that builds the table's or the column's name from pieces, say), a browser
    driver it does not list, a browser started as a program of its own, a file outside those it reads, or a change inside
    the lines it pins, such as a retry without the sandbox in `pages.ts`; the renderer's own tests (`launch.test.ts`,
    `pages.test.ts`, `renderer-check.test.ts`) check that last one, and no test checks the others.
  ```
  
  This matches ADR-0105:49 and the guard's header at apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts:21-25. Neither of those needs a change.

- **consistency:SWEEP-4** (minor, consistency), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:85 (What this changes, ADR-0084 bullet); docs/adr/0084-renderer-launch-without-secrets.md:80 (later note of 2026-10-18, ADR-0105)

  ADR-0105:85 lists what changes in ADR-0084: the sandbox setting, `launchChromium()`'s parameter, "Readiness before spend", "What this does not decide" and the consequence "The OS sandbox stays off". ADR-0084's later note lists the same. Neither mentions ADR-0084's Decision bullet "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once. `launchFailure()` turns any failure of the production launch into one non-retryable `StepError` that names Playwright's first line and the install command".

  Why: That bullet no longer holds. Since ADR-0105 item 4, a sandbox that cannot start gives why on this computer and the fix, with no install command (pages.ts launchFailure → sandboxFailureMessage; launch.test.ts:198 "…not the install command"). A sandboxed launch that dies with no reason now also names the sandbox as one possible cause. Someone who reads ADR-0084 with its later notes is told that every launch failure gives Playwright's first line and the install command.

  Suggested fix: In ADR-0105:85, add: "; "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once" still holds, but a sandbox that cannot start now gives why on this computer and the fix in place of Playwright's first line and the install command, and a sandboxed launch that dies with no reason names the sandbox as one possible cause (item 4)". Add the same clause to ADR-0084's ADR-0105 later note.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: I checked this against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f, and the finding holds as a gap in completeness.
  
  **ADR-0084's Decision (line 30)** says: "`launchFailure()` turns any failure of the production launch into one non-retryable `StepError` that names Playwright's first line and the install command".
  
  **The code on the branch no longer does that for every failure.** In packages/runner/src/pages.ts:348-355, a failure that carries a sandbox marker gets `sandboxFailureMessage(advice())`. That message gives why on this computer and the fix, and has neither Playwright's first line nor the install command (launch.test.ts:198-205 checks `not.toMatch(/playwright install|Target page|.../)`). A sandboxed launch that dies with no reason given, other than a missing Chromium, now also says "One possible cause is that its OS sandbox cannot run on this computer…" (launch.test.ts:213-228). ADR-0105 item 4 (lines 53-59) describes both behaviours.
  
  **Neither summary of the changes mentions the bullet.** ADR-0105:85 lists the sandbox setting, `launchChromium()`'s parameter, "Readiness before spend", "What this does not decide" and the consequence. ADR-0084's ADR-0105 note (line 80) lists the same. A reader who takes those notes as the full list of what changed would still believe every launch failure names the first line and the install command.
  
  **Why minor and not blocking.**
  - The code and tests are right, and ADR-0105 item 4 states the behaviour in full.
  - ADR-0084's own "What this does not decide" (line 35) said "a sandbox-specific message … belong[s] with the decision", and both notes say that section is decided or replaced in ADR-0105, so a careful reader gets there.
  - In the case with no reason given, the message still keeps Playwright's first line and the install command, so the old bullet is wrong only for failures that carry a marker.
  
  **The fix is allowed under ADR-0103.** Editing the existing note does not break its rules. `git diff 7eabcfe fix/fx-d2` shows that ADR-0084's whole "Later notes" section is new on this branch and not yet on main. Context and Decision are untouched. ADR-0105 is not on main either.
  
  The new text uses none of the phrases ci.test.ts forbids.

  Fix: 1) docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:85
  OLD: its "What this does not decide" is decided here, with the 23.10 sentence corrected (item 9); its consequence "The OS sandbox stays off" no longer holds.
  NEW: its "What this does not decide" is decided here, with the 23.10 sentence corrected (item 9); "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once" still holds, but a sandbox that cannot start gives why on this computer and the fix in place of Playwright's first line and the install command, and a sandboxed launch that dies with no reason given also names the sandbox as one possible cause (item 4); its consequence "The OS sandbox stays off" no longer holds.
  
  2) docs/adr/0084-renderer-launch-without-secrets.md:80 (the note for ADR-0105, added on this branch)
  OLD: and `ready()` and the start-up check now draw a test page, so the sandboxed page renderer itself has started before anything is spent.
  NEW: and `ready()` and the start-up check now draw a test page, so the sandboxed page renderer itself has started before anything is spent. "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once" still holds, but a sandbox that cannot start now gives why on this computer and the fix in place of Playwright's first line and the install command, and a sandboxed launch that dies with no reason given also names the sandbox as one possible cause (ADR-0105 item 4).

- **consistency:SWEEP-5** (minor, consistency), at packages/runner/src/pages.ts:19-22 (module header)

  "A render whose page does not close in time retires that browser rather than closing it under the others: new renders launch another, the renders still on it finish there, and it is closed when the last of them ends, at the latest one render's limit plus the close limit after it was retired (render-browsers.ts; ADR-0106)."

  Why: Round 1's retire-1 corrected this claim in ADR-0106 (Consequences, items 2 and 7). byway only starts the close by then and waits for it at most closeTimeoutMs. Playwright kills a browser process that has not answered 30 s after the close began, so a retired browser can live about 65 s (65.3 s measured). render-browsers.ts:7-10 says so ("past that, Playwright's own close goes on"), but the overview in pages.ts still states the 35 s bound as the browser being closed.

  Suggested fix: Replace the clause with: "byway closes it when the last of them ends, and at the latest one render's limit plus the close limit after it was retired; a browser process that does not answer that close is killed by Playwright 30 s later (render-browsers.ts; ADR-0106)."

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real. pages.ts is the module overview. It says that, after the browser is retired, it "is closed when the last of them ends, at the latest one render's limit plus the close limit after it was retired". ADR-0106 used the same wording before round 1, and round 1 found it untrue (retire-1). Commit b1b062e then rewrote ADR-0106's item 2 ("A retired browser is closed no later than ...") and item 7 ("A hung Chromium is closed ... within the backstop") so they say "byway closes" and mention Playwright's kill 30 s later. The ADR's Consequences now give "up to about 65 s". The pages.ts header was written in 957da70 and no commit since has touched it, so it still has the corrected wording.
  
  The code supports the correction. In render-browsers.ts, shut() calls b.close({ reason }) and races it against sleep(closeTimeoutMs), so byway only starts the close and stops waiting for it. In Playwright 1.63.0, coreBundle.js:39848 closes through closeOrKill(DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT), and DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_TIMEOUT is 3e4 (coreBundle.js:4594). So a browser process that does not answer the close is killed only about 30 s later (65.3 s was measured in round 1).
  
  render-browsers.ts:7-10 uses the same "It is closed ... at the latest" wording but adds "Closing it is bounded by the close limit too; past that, Playwright's own close goes on". That makes it accurate enough; pages.ts has no such caveat.
  
  I found no other stale copy of the bound. ADR-0105:51 says only "the backstop applies" and gives no time. The README and backlog give no bound.
  
  Severity is minor: this is a code comment, the behaviour is correct, and the authoritative ADR and render-browsers.ts state it correctly. A reader of the overview alone would still believe a retired Chromium is gone by 35 s.

  Fix: packages/runner/src/pages.ts, lines 20-22 of the module header.
  
  OLD:
   * rather than closing it under the others: new renders launch another, the renders still on it finish there, and it is
   * closed when the last of them ends, at the latest one render's limit plus the close limit after it was retired
   * (render-browsers.ts; ADR-0106).
  
  NEW:
   * rather than closing it under the others: new renders launch another, the renders still on it finish there, and byway
   * closes it when the last of them ends, at the latest one render's limit plus the close limit after it was retired; a
   * browser process that does not answer that close is killed by Playwright 30 s later (render-browsers.ts; ADR-0106).
  
  The new lines are 119, 118 and 117 characters, inside the header's 120-column width. No code or test changes.

- **consistency:SWEEP-6** (minor, consistency), at docs/backlog.md:160 (row "Bring the decision evidence kept only in %TEMP% into the repository")

  "ADR-0106 cites the late-close investigation there: its report, both clones, the e2e and CI logs, the probe and the red and mutation logs, and round 1's report on the D2 wave with a refuter's clone. ADR-0105 cites … its red runs and mutation logs (scratch/d2/b3, scratch/d2-r2), the real Windows probe (scratch/d2-r2/builder-a/probe-s2) and round 1's report."

  Why: The row was written in round 1 (1dc0249) and not updated in rounds 2 and 3. It misses evidence both records now cite from Temp: round 2's report `…\scratchpad\d2-r2-result.json` (ADR-0105:146, ADR-0106:127), round 3's red runs and mutation logs `byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r3\` (ADR-0105:154), and the closed-renderer evidence `scratch\d2-r3\sticky-close\` (ADR-0106:127). This row exists to list what must be copied before Temp is cleared, so whatever it leaves out can be lost.

  Suggested fix: Append: "Both also cite round 2's report (the session scratchpad's d2-r2-result.json), and round 3's red runs and mutation logs (scratch/d2-r3, including sticky-close, which ADR-0106 cites)."

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: Confirmed at fix/fx-d2 (a34568f). The text in docs/backlog.md:160 was last edited in 1dc0249 (round 1). `git log -G` shows no later change. It lists the %TEMP% evidence that ADR-0105 and ADR-0106 cite, but leaves out what rounds 2 and 3 added:
  (a) Round 2's report, `...\Temp\claude\...\scratchpad\d2-r2-result.json`. ADR-0105:146 and ADR-0106:127 both cite it.
  (b) Round 3's red runs and mutation logs, `...\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\d2-r3\`, cited by ADR-0105:154.
  (c) The closed-renderer red and mutation logs, `scratch\d2-r3\sticky-close\`, cited by ADR-0106:127.
  All three paths exist on disk now.
  
  Every other Temp path the branch adds to docs is already covered by the row's wording:
  - scratch/d2/retire and its probe log: "the probe and the red and mutation logs"
  - inv1 and refute-retire-retire-1: "both clones" and "a refuter's clone"
  - the ci-39097581622 log: "the CI logs"
  - builder-a and builder-b: "scratch/d2-r2"
  
  The row names other ADRs' evidence only in general terms but lists these two ADRs' evidence item by item, so a reader takes that list as complete. Left as is, someone cleaning Temp could lose round 2's report and the d2-r3 logs.
  
  Severity is minor, not blocking:
  - The row's required minimum ("Bring in at least the D1 to D4 and held-question briefs...") does not depend on this list.
  - The ADRs themselves still give the exact paths.
  - No behaviour is affected.

- **main-side:SWEEP-2** (minor, main-side), at docs/backlog.md:165 (the only place the owner's CI answer is recorded)

  "The owner chose on 2026-10-18 to keep CI as it is, with timing flakes named and set aside ("Not now"), so the e2e waits are left as they are."

  Why: The question put to the owner (2026-10-18T19:12:05Z) was "Windows CI keeps failing on timing in tests, not in byway. Adopt the CI proposal?". The proposal was: merge on the local gate; Linux CI on every push; Windows CI weekly and by hand; unit and browser tests as parallel jobs; recorded as a new ADR amending ADR-0103. The owner answered "Not now", which defers it rather than rejecting it. It was raised after the owner's own complaint at 05:19:54Z: "over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical. We will never get anything done." The repository records that answer only as the reason C5's 120 s wait stays. No row parks the proposal, the CI-duration problem (Windows 28 to 40 min; this branch's run 39172144829 spent 19.7 min on the Windows browser tests alone), or a trigger to revisit. The write-up given to the owner lists the wall-clock limits still in the suite (`scratchpad/byway-windows-ci-writeup.md` §6, in Temp only): page-copy.test.ts BOUND_MS = 5000, flow-memo.test.ts:73, limits.test.ts:95, liveness.test.ts:175, spend-book.test.ts:235/255, memo.test.ts:175, and a state e2e's 30 s wait for "Sent" that failed once locally. Its recommendation to make those time-free "soon" is not filed either. The backlog's own rule (ADR-0062, code first) is that deferred items are parked with what brings them back. Supporting data point from this sweep: under load, a full unit run on the owner's machine failed link-view.test.ts "records the clip's last frame on its shot, beside the clip" at vitest's default 5 s test timeout; the test passed alone in 61 ms.

  Suggested fix: Add a Product follow-ups row: "| CI time, and the CI proposal the owner deferred ("Not now", 2026-10-18) | A Windows job takes 28 to 40 minutes (the browser tests alone 19.7 min in run 39172144829), Linux 14 to 18; the owner: "over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical" (2026-10-18). The proposal put to the owner, deferred with "Not now": merge on the local gate, Linux CI on every push, Windows CI weekly and by hand, unit and browser tests as parallel jobs (a new ADR amending ADR-0103). Also left: the wall-clock limits still in the tests (page-copy.test.ts BOUND_MS, flow-memo.test.ts, limits.test.ts, liveness.test.ts, spend-book.test.ts, memo.test.ts, a state e2e's 30 s wait, and vitest's default 5 s per test), any of which can make the next red Windows run. Revisit when Windows CI goes red on timing again, or when the owner raises CI time | .github/workflows/verify.yml, ADR-0103, tests |". Leave row 165 as it is.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real but minor, and its "why" overstates one point.
  
  What is true: row 165 is the only place in the repository where the owner's CI answer appears. Nothing on the branch or on main parks the deferred proposal, the CI-time problem or the time limits still in the tests. A grep for "Not now", "parallel jobs", "Windows CI weekly" and "CI proposal" across docs, README.md, the ADRs and .github turns up only backlog.md:165 and the existing weekly-trigger lines. main has no commits that the branch lacks.
  
  The transcript (67b5b9ac…jsonl) confirms the question and the answer:
  - Question: "Windows CI keeps failing on timing in tests, not in byway. Adopt the CI proposal?"
  - "Yes" option: merge on the local gate; Linux CI on every push to main; Windows CI weekly and by hand; unit and browser tests as parallel jobs; a new ADR amending ADR-0103.
  - "Not now" option: "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside."
  - The owner picked "Not now" (2026-10-18T21:02:28Z). The complaint "over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical. We will never get anything done." came at 05:19:54Z.
  
  Row 165 records that answer accurately: it repeats the option's own wording, so nothing in it is false. What is missing is the deferral itself. "Not now" means later, and the write-up the owner asked for says "Soon: do option 2" and "Revisit option 3". The project keeps parked items in the backlog. ADR-0062 says non-code decisions are parked there, and the owner's memory says to park off-path findings.
  
  The time limits listed exist on the branch:
  - page-copy.test.ts:1194 BOUND_MS = 5000
  - flow-memo.test.ts:73 (1,000 ms)
  - limits.test.ts:95 (500 ms)
  - liveness.test.ts:175 (10 s)
  - spend-book.test.ts:235 and :255 (2,000 ms and 900 ms)
  - memo.test.ts:175 (2,000 ms)
  - the "Sent" wait in state.spec.ts:336, which falls under playwright.config.ts's expect timeout of 30 s
  - vitest.config.ts sets no testTimeout, so the default 5 s applies.
  
  No backlog row names these. Row 145 covers only pages.test.ts, and separate rows cover Temporal's 5 s start and C5's 120 s wait.
  
  Where the "why" goes too far:
  1. ADR-0062 does not say parked items must carry a revisit trigger. That is the backlog's convention, not the ADR's rule.
  2. I could not check the "19.7 min on the Windows browser tests" figure. What gh run view does show for run 39172144829 is a Windows job of 40 min (22:25:23 to 23:05:26) and a Linux job of 14.4 min.
  
  It is minor rather than blocking: no statement is false, nothing behaves wrongly, and the owner knows the proposal. Without the row, though, a later session would not know the proposal exists or what would bring it back.

  Fix: docs/backlog.md, Product follow-ups table: add one new row right after the row that begins "| Known Windows timing flake: browser test C5 waits at most 120 s for each next turn |". Leave that row as it is. New row:
  
  | CI time, and the CI proposal the owner deferred ("Not now", 2026-10-18) | The owner (2026-10-18): "over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical. We will never get anything done." A Windows job takes 28 to 40 minutes (40 in run 39172144829, on fix/fx-d2) and a Linux job 14 to 18; `pnpm verify` takes about 7 minutes on the owner's machine. The proposal put to the owner, answered "Not now" (keep CI as it is; timing flakes named and set aside): merge on the local gate; Linux CI on every push to main; Windows CI weekly and by hand; unit and browser tests as parallel jobs; recorded as a new ADR amending ADR-0103. Also left, any of which can make the next red Windows run: the wall-clock limits in packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts (`BOUND_MS = 5000`), packages/validator/test/flow-memo.test.ts:73 (1,000 ms), packages/recipe/test/limits.test.ts:95 (500 ms), packages/runner/test/liveness.test.ts:175 (10 s), packages/gateway/test/spend-book.test.ts:235 and :255 (2,000 and 900 ms) and packages/runner/test/memo.test.ts:175 (2,000 ms); the 30 s wait for "Sent" in apps/web/e2e/state.spec.ts (it failed once locally under load); and vitest's default 5 s per test (vitest.config.ts sets none). The renderer tests' limits, Temporal's 5 s start and C5's 120 s wait have their own rows. Make them time-free as fx-ci-timing did when Windows CI next goes red on timing, and revisit the proposal then, or when the owner raises CI time | .github/workflows/verify.yml, ADR-0103, tests (runner, validator, recipe, gateway, apps/web e2e) |
  
  The row contains none of the phrases that scripts/test/ci.test.ts forbids in docs/backlog.md. Adding a backlog row does not touch the ADR index (ADR-0103's rule).

- **main-side:SWEEP-3** (minor, main-side), at docs/backlog.md:177 ("A hosted byway: a URL other people can open")

  "It must also revisit choices earlier records made for one local user: a separate renderer process or low-privilege account (ADR-0084: …), a token on every request against cross-site requests (ADR-0074 …), the per-process single flight for the first recommendation (ADR-0091), and the open decision "Who needs Lab access" (ADR index)."

  Why: D2 adds a new choice made for one local user, and this list does not name it. ADR-0105 item 1 makes the renderer's OS sandbox one setting for the whole data directory ("since the engine's one renderer serves every workspace"). Its only writer is Settings' `PATCH /api/renderer`, and nothing restricts who may call it: there is no sign-in, and the README says byway admits any loopback request. In a hosted byway any visitor could switch the sandbox off for every user's renders, which run in the server's engine next to the server's keys. ADR-0105 says nothing about hosting, so the hosted row is the only place a future design would find this.

  Suggested fix: Insert into the list after the ADR-0084 item: ", the renderer's OS sandbox as one setting for the whole data directory, which anyone who reaches Settings can switch off (ADR-0105)".

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real, and minor is the right severity. I checked it against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f. The hosted row is at docs/backlog.md:177. It came from 6cc36c7, on main before D2, and the branch does not touch it. Its list of "choices earlier records made for one local user" names ADR-0084, ADR-0074, ADR-0091 and the "Who needs Lab access" decision. It does not name ADR-0105.
  
  ADR-0105 is that same kind of choice:
  - Its title says "only the person switches it off, in Settings", and Decision 1 calls it "a setting the person owns".
  - Line 41 makes it "one for the data directory, since the engine's one renderer serves every workspace".
  - The only way to switch it is PATCH in apps/web/app/api/renderer/route.ts. That route has no check beyond the local guard in apps/web/proxy.ts (loopback hosts, no cross-site writes), and no route has sign-in.
  
  In a hosted byway, any visitor could therefore switch off a protection for the operator's machine. The setting is global, so that would cover every tenant's renders. Those renders run in the engine whose environment holds the server's keys. A sign-in design alone would not settle this, because the setting is one per data directory and is not owned by a tenant.
  
  None of the four listed records (ADR-0074, 0084 and 0091, plus the index's Lab access question) uses the words "hosted" or "multi-user" except ADR-0084's "Revisit". So the list was built by judgement, not by quoting the records, and ADR-0105 meets the same test.
  
  No other place records this. ADR-0105 never mentions hosting, and a search of docs/, the README and apps/web/README.md for "hosted" or "multi-user" finds nothing about the sandbox switch.
  
  Why it is less severe than the finding says:
  - Nothing behaves wrongly, and no statement is false. The list does not say it is complete, and hosting is not designed and is "Pick up only if the owner wants a hosted URL".
  - The finding says the backlog row is "the only place a future design would find this". That overstates it. The ADR-0084 item already in the list leads to ADR-0105 through ADR-0084's later note dated 2026-10-18. That note shows the switch exists, though it does not raise the hosted concern.
  - No test pins the row's text, so the edit breaks nothing.

  Fix: In docs/backlog.md, line 177, the row "A hosted byway: a URL other people can open", Why column.
  
  Old text:
  a separate renderer process or low-privilege account (ADR-0084: "Revisit if byway is ever hosted or multi-user"), a token on every request against cross-site requests
  
  New text:

(or the merge's own gate numbers).   The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real, but only minor. I checked it read-only against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f and against CI run 39172144829 (read with gh run view and its log).
  
  1. Status page, docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:56. D2 rewrote the page's opening paragraph, the F18 row and the D2 decision line so they say D2 is on main (git diff 7eabcfe...fix/fx-d2). It left the only gate statement under "Checks on main" unchanged: "With D1 and D4 merged (2026-10-17), the full gate passes on `main` on Windows: … 4,861 tests in 190 files … 53 of 53 browser tests." The sentence carries a date, so it is not false. It is stale next to the rest of the page, which now describes main after D2. The last time decisions were merged, commit 6cc36c7 ("the gate after the merges") updated this same line, so leaving it is a miss by the page's own practice. The line had already drifted on main before D2 (run 39079760987 counted 4,864 unit tests), and since it is dated, nobody is misled about behaviour. That is why this is minor, not blocking.
  
  2. ADR-0105 Evidence. Nothing on the branch names run 39172144829 (git grep finds nothing). The branch recorded every earlier CI run: 0da7bf0 recorded 39137471023, f46726f recorded 39149469179, and d082335 recorded the failed run 39152075629. This run is the one that tested the shipped script. It started at 22:25 UTC, and a34568f was committed at 22:49 UTC, before the run finished at 23:05 UTC. That explains why it is missing. The run's facts match the finding:
     - head a3d271f, both jobs successful;
     - on Linux the log shows "kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1", "kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_unconfined = 0" and "Added the AppArmor profile /etc/apparmor.d/playwright-chromium: Playwright's Chromium in /home/runner/.cache/ms-playwright …";
     - on each system, Test Files 198 passed, Tests 4997 passed, 55 browser tests passed, and "e2e: renderer Playwright's Chromium, ready, inside its OS sandbox".
  
     Between a3d271f and a34568f, the only changes are a comment in the script and a comment plus one regex in ci.test.ts. No test was added, so the counts still hold. Since e36a0aa the script's code changed a lot (XDG_CACHE_HOME, the pwd -P resolution, the folder-exists refusal, the unconfined print). The two runs ADR-0105 records therefore tested earlier versions of the script, not the one that ships.
  
  The finding overstates one point. The run did not trigger the symbolic-link branch (the log has no "leads to" line), the refusal, or a custom XDG_CACHE_HOME. It ran the shipped script only on its normal path. The fix below says so.

- **code:CODE-2** (minor, code), at apps/web/src/components/PageRendererSettings.tsx:17-27 (and :58)

  The doc comment that describes the component (`Settings' "Page renderer" (ADR-0105): the OS sandbox the renderer's Chromium runs inside, ... A screen reader hears the risk with whichever answer has focus (SandboxOffConfirm).`) sits directly above `interface ConfirmProps {` at line 27. `export function PageRendererSettings` at line 58 has no doc comment.

  Why: A JSDoc block attaches to the declaration that follows it. Editors and TypeScript tooling therefore show the description of the whole Settings section (the switch, the confirmation, the findings and the focus rules) on ConfirmProps, the confirmation's props. They show nothing on PageRendererSettings, the component it describes. A reader of the code sees the component explained on the wrong declaration. This most likely happened when SandboxOffConfirm and its props were split out above the component.

  Suggested fix: Move the block from lines 17-26 to sit directly above `export function PageRendererSettings({ settings: initial }: Props) {`, leaving `interface ConfirmProps` with its own one-line doc (e.g. `/** SandboxOffConfirm's props: the risk's id, whether a change is saving, and the two answers. */`).

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: Confirmed at fix/fx-d2 (a34568f), apps/web/src/components/PageRendererSettings.tsx. The block at lines 17-26 describes the whole component: the switch, the confirmation, the start-up check's findings and the focus rules (R5). It sits directly above `interface ConfirmProps {` at line 27. `export function PageRendererSettings({ settings: initial }: Props) {` at line 58 has no doc comment. A JSDoc block attaches to the declaration right after it, so hover and IntelliSense show the component's description on ConfirmProps and nothing on PageRendererSettings.
  
  `git log -L` shows how it happened. 093155f created the file with the block on the component. b34accc (round 1, the W1/WEB-1/WEB-2 checks, on this branch) inserted `interface ConfirmProps` and `SandboxOffConfirm` between the block and the component, and added the "(SandboxOffConfirm)" sentence to the block. The file does not exist on main, so the slip comes only from this branch.
  
  It is not deliberate: SandboxOffConfirm has its own block at lines 35-39, and ConfirmProps is the confirmation's props, not the Settings section. No test depends on where the comment sits. guard-sandbox.test.ts matches lines by their text, not by position: the one line it pins in this file is `setSettings(await patch<RendererSettings>('/api/renderer', { osSandbox }));`, which the move does not touch. renderer.test.ts only imports SandboxOffConfirm.
  
  It is minor, not blocking: runtime behaviour, UI copy and the wording are all correct. Only the editor tooltip is on the wrong declaration, and a reader of the source sees the block next to the right component anyway.

  - The substance is right, because the owner did approve a design that contains these items. Only the attribution is wrong.
  
  Why it is minor:
  - The Context section gives the true provenance.
  - No behaviour changes.
  - The fix is worth making before the merge. Under ADR-0103 §1, Decision item 6 (:70) is fixed in place once the record reaches main as Accepted. The Alternatives lines and the code comments could still change later.
  - The user's rule is that ADRs record provenance honestly, and round 1's DOC-3 (1dc0249) corrected the same kind of attribution in this record.

  - new: "Closest to the approved design's \"Never automatic\","

- **process:PR-2** (minor, process), at docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:85 and docs/adr/0084-renderer-launch-without-secrets.md:80 (later note), against ADR-0084:30

  ADR-0084 Decision (:30): "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once. `launchFailure()` turns any failure of the production launch into one non-retryable `StepError` that names Playwright's first line and the install command ... A browser that crashed or failed to launch is still launched again on the next render (ENG-14), always with the same options." ADR-0105's list of what it changes in ADR-0084 (:85), and ADR-0084's 2026-10-18 note for ADR-0105 (:80), name the sandbox constant, launchChromium's parameter, readiness and "What this does not decide". They do not name this bullet.

  Why: Since ADR-0105 item 4, a failure Chromium ties to the sandbox names neither Playwright's first line nor the install command. packages/runner/test/launch.test.ts:198, "whose OS sandbox could not start says so, why on this computer and the fix, not the install command", asserts `not.toMatch(/playwright install|Target page|.../)`. Since item 2, a relaunch takes the setting read for that load, so the options are no longer "always the same". Both lists name the other replaced parts one by one, so a reader of ADR-0084 takes this bullet as still in force.

  Suggested fix: Add to ADR-0105:85's ADR-0084 bullet (still a draft on the branch): "its 'A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once' no longer holds as written: a failure that is the sandbox's names why and the fix instead of Playwright's first line and the install command (item 4), and a relaunch uses the setting read for that load (item 2), not always the same options;". Add the same clause to ADR-0084's 2026-10-18 later note (line 80).

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real, and minor is the right severity. I checked it against fix/fx-d2 at a34568f.
  
  **What ADR-0084 still says.** Its Decision bullet at :30 says `launchFailure()` turns "any failure of the production launch" into a StepError "that names Playwright's first line and the install command". It also says a relaunch happens "always with the same options".
  
  **What the code does now.** In `packages/runner/src/pages.ts:348-355`, `launchFailure` returns `sandboxFailureMessage(...)` when the sandbox was not off and `isSandboxFailure(err)` is true. That message has neither Playwright's first line nor the install command. `launch.test.ts:198-201` asserts this with `not.toMatch(/playwright install|Target page|.../)`. A sandboxed launch that dies with no marker also adds a "one possible cause" sandbox sentence (ADR-0105 item 4, :58). Under item 2, a launch uses the setting read for the load that launches it, so after the setting changes a relaunch no longer has "the same options".
  
  **Why it is a real gap.** ADR-0105:85 and ADR-0084's later note at :80 both list the changed parts of ADR-0084 one by one. They name the sandbox constant, launchChromium's parameter, readiness, "What this does not decide" and the "stays off" consequence. Neither names this bullet. Both files are drafts on the branch: ADR-0105 is not on main 7eabcfe, and the 0084 note is new. So they can be edited without breaking ADR-0103. The index row already carries "changed by ADR-0105", so no process rule is broken.
  
  **Why only minor.** The core of the bullet still holds: one plain failure, not retried, nonRetryable. Also, ADR-0084's own "What this does not decide" (:35) says "Today's mapping would show that first line plainly and not retry; a sandbox-specific message ... belong with the decision". Both lists say that section is replaced. So a careful reader is pointed to the change of message. The "same options" change can be inferred from "launchChromium() now takes the sandbox setting". A reader would be only mildly misled, so it is not blocking.
  
  The fix the finding suggests is accurate. My wording below is tighter, and it keeps "once, not retried" so the edit does not read as that part changing.

  Fix: 1) docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:85 (draft on the branch)
  OLD: `"Readiness before spend" now draws a test page (item 3); its "What this does not decide" is decided here,`
  NEW: `"Readiness before spend" now draws a test page (item 3); "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once" still fails once and plainly, not retried, but a failure Chromium ties to the sandbox says why on this computer and the fix instead of Playwright's first line and the install command, a sandboxed launch that dies with no reason given also names the sandbox as one possible cause (item 4), and a relaunch takes the setting read for the load that launches it, not always the same options (item 2); its "What this does not decide" is decided here,`
  
  2) docs/adr/0084-renderer-launch-without-secrets.md:80 (the 2026-10-18 later note for ADR-0105, added on the branch; it is outside Context/Decision)
  OLD: `` `launchChromium()` now takes the sandbox setting and nothing else; and `ready()` ``
  NEW: `` `launchChromium()` now takes the sandbox setting and nothing else, so a relaunch uses the setting read for that load, not always the same options; "A Chromium that cannot start fails plainly, once" still holds as one plain failure, not retried, but a failure Chromium ties to the sandbox says why on this computer and the fix instead of Playwright's first line and the install command (ADR-0105 item 4); and `ready()` ``
  
  No change is needed to ADR-0084's Context or Decision (not allowed by ADR-0103 anyway), the index or the code.

- **process:PR-3** (minor, process), at scripts/test/ci.test.ts:175

  it('when a job fails, uploads the end-to-end server log and Playwright’s traces from where the suite writes them, one of each per operating system (ADR-0106)', ...

  Why: This test title keeps the claim that round 1's check retire-2 found untrue ("a failed job always uploads two artifacts"). ADR-0106 item 6 (:54) was corrected to "as at most two artifacts per operating system ... Each upload is skipped when it finds no file". The README says a job that fails before `pnpm e2e` keeps nothing, and the test body asserts `'if-no-files-found': 'ignore'`. The brief lists "two artifacts" among the reversed claims.

  Suggested fix: Rename to 'when a job fails, uploads the end-to-end server log and Playwright’s traces from where the suite writes them, at most one of each per operating system, each skipped when it finds no file (ADR-0106)'.

  The refuter's verdict: real: yes, severity: minor.

  Reason: The finding is real. At fix/fx-d2 (a34568f), the test title at scripts/test/ci.test.ts:175 is still the text commit ecfb810 wrote. That title copied the original wording of ADR-0106 item 6: "it uploads two artifacts ... One of each is uploaded per operating system". Round 1's check retire-2 found that wording untrue. Commit b1b062e corrected the ADR (:54 now says "as at most two artifacts per operating system ... Each upload is skipped when it finds no file"), and ADR-0106:126 records the correction: "item 6 said a failed job always uploads two artifacts (retire-2)". Two other places were also brought into line. The README (:200) says a job that fails before `pnpm e2e` keeps nothing. The comment in verify.yml says a job that fails in `pnpm e2e` with no browser test failed "uploads the log alone". The test title was missed in that correction. It still reads as an unconditional rule: when a job fails, it uploads the log and the traces, one of each per OS. Its own body contradicts it, because it asserts `'if-no-files-found': 'ignore'` on both upload steps. Nothing else on the branch repeats the claim (I searched for "one of each", "two artifacts" and "one artifact of each"; the only other matches are unrelated). The severity is minor, not blocking. It is a test name: the workflow behaves correctly and the ADR, README and workflow comment are all right, so someone reading the docs or using CI is not misled. Only a reader of the test list would see the old claim.

  Fix: File scripts/test/ci.test.ts, line 175. Old text: it('when a job fails, uploads the end-to-end server log and Playwright’s traces from where the suite writes them, one of each per operating system (ADR-0106)', () => {
  New text: it('when a job fails, uploads the end-to-end server log and Playwright’s traces from where the suite writes them, at most one of each per operating system, each skipped when it finds no file (ADR-0106)', () => {
  (Keep the curly apostrophe in "Playwright’s" as it is. Only the title changes; the test body already checks this behaviour.)
